<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We prove the integral scalar-curvature inequality proposed by Gromov. For every dimension <i>n</i> ≥ 3, there is a constant $`a_n\gt 0`$, depending only on <i>n</i>, such that
> 
> $`\displaystyle \int_M(\mathop{\mathrm{Scal}}\nolimits _g^-)^{n/2}\,dV_g\ge a_n\|M\|`$
> 
> for every closed connected oriented smooth <i>n</i>-manifold <i>M</i> and every smooth Riemannian metric <i>g</i>. Here $`\mathop{\mathrm{Scal}}\nolimits _g^-:=\max\{0,-\mathop{\mathrm{Scal}}\nolimits _g\}`$, and $`\|M\|`$ is real simplicial volume. The proof uses the nonnegative-scalar-curvature vanishing theorem of the companion paper on rational inessentiality.

- **claim-integral-scalar-curvature** (bound, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): For every integer $n\ge3$, there is a constant $a_n>0$, depending only on $n$, such that every closed connected oriented smooth $n$-manifold $M$ and every smooth Riemannian metric $g$ on $M$ satisfy $$\int_M(\mathop{\mathrm{Scal}}_g^-)^{n/2}\,dV_g\ge a_n\\|M\\|. \label{eq:main}$$
  - Explanation: The integral of the critical power of negative scalar curvature controls real simplicial volume with a positive constant depending only on dimension, uniformly over manifolds and smooth metrics.
- **claim-nonnegative-scalar-vanishing** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-vanishing-input)): Let $M$ be a closed connected oriented smooth manifold of positive dimension. If $M$ admits a smooth Riemannian metric with nonnegative scalar curvature, then its real simplicial volume is zero.
  - Explanation: The qualitative input forces simplicial volume to vanish whenever the stated manifold admits a smooth metric of nonnegative scalar curvature. This is imported from the companion manuscript.
- **claim-yamabe-integral-transfer** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-prop-yamabe-reduction)): Fix $n\ge3$. Suppose that a constant $C_n<\infty$ has the following property: every closed connected oriented $n$-manifold with a smooth metric $h$ of constant scalar curvature $-1$ satisfies $$\\|M\\|\le C_n\mathop{\mathrm{Vol}}_h(M). \label{eq:scalar-minus-one-target}$$ Then every smooth Riemannian metric $g$ on every such manifold satisfies $$\\|M\\|\le C_n\int_M(\mathop{\mathrm{Scal}}_g^-)^{n/2}\,dV_g . \label{eq:yamabe-transfer}$$
  - Explanation: A uniform volume bound for metrics normalized to negative constant scalar curvature transfers to the integral bound for arbitrary smooth metrics. The conformal minimizer supplies the intermediate volume comparison.
- **claim-probability-cocycle-detector** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-detector)): The form $\eta$ is closed on every finite-dimensional face of $\Delta_{\mathcal L}$. There is a smooth $\Gamma$-equivariant map $$F:\widetilde M\longrightarrow\ell^2(\mathcal L),\qquad F_\ell\ge0,\qquad \sum_\ell F_\ell^2=1,$$ with finitely many label orbits and locally finite compact supports for its coordinates. If $p_\ell=F_\ell^2$, then the descended form satisfies $$\int_M p^*\eta=I. \label{eq:detector-integral}$$
  - Explanation: A closed probability-simplex form and an equivariant root probability map realize the chosen bounded-cohomology pairing as an integral on the manifold.
- **claim-weighted-graph-identity** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-graph-identity)): Let $(X,h)$ be a smooth Riemannian manifold, let $\phi,f$ be smooth real functions, and let $N>0$. Set $$L=(1+N^2\|df\|_h^2)^{1/2},\qquad v=L^{-1},\qquad U=\frac{N\nabla_h f}{L},\qquad h'=h+N^2df\otimes df,\qquad \phi'=\phi-\log L.$$ View $h'$ as the induced metric on the graph $\Sigma=\{(x,Nf(x)):x\in X\}$ in $(X\times\mathbb R,h+dz^2)$, and extend $\phi$ constantly in the $z$-direction. Orient the graph by $\nu=(-U,v)$, put $\mathrm{II}(V,W)=\langle\bar\nabla_V\nu,W\rangle$, and let $$H=\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}_\Sigma\mathrm{II},\qquad D=H+\partial_\nu\phi=-\mathop{\mathrm{div}}_\phi U .$$ Here $D$, and its derivative $U(D)$, are regarded as functions on $X$ through the graph projection. Then $$\mathcal S(h',\phi')-\mathcal S(h,\phi) =D^2-2U(D)+\|\mathrm{II}\|_{h'}^2+\|d\log L\|_{h'}^2. \label{eq:graph-identity}$$ Moreover, $$e^{\phi'}dV_{h'}=e^\phi dV_h,\qquad N^2\|df\|_{h'}^2=1-v^2=\|U\|_h^2,\qquad \|d\log L\|_{h'}\le L\|U\|_h\|\mathrm{II}\|_{h'} . \label{eq:graph-basic-bounds}$$
  - Explanation: The graph changes weighted scalar curvature by the prescribed divergence square, its directional derivative, and nonnegative geometric squares. The accompanying weight adjustment preserves the weighted volume density.
- **claim-complete-prescribed-graphs** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-graph-prescription)): Let $(X,h)$ be a complete smooth Riemannian manifold without boundary, with bounded geometry as above, and suppose the positive-order derivatives of $\phi$ are uniformly bounded. For a graph of height $z$, set $$L_z=(1+\|dz\|_h^2)^{1/2},\quad v_z=L_z^{-1},\quad U_z=\frac{\nabla_hz}{L_z},\quad D_z=-\mathop{\mathrm{div}}_\phi U_z .$$ Consider either of the following equations $D_z=p(x,z,v_z)$.* 1. *Let $N,\epsilon,a>0$, $0<\delta\le1$, and $H_*\ge0$. Suppose $F_i,\beta$ are uniformly smooth, $\|F_i\|\le1$, and $0\le\beta\le H_*$. On the region $z>0$, prescribe $$p(x,z,v)= \frac{\epsilon a(F_i(x)^2+\delta^2)+(1-v^2)/2}{z} -(\epsilon a+\beta(x))\frac{z}{N^2}. \label{eq:graph-prescription-positive}$$* 2. *Let $e,w>0$ be constants. Suppose $B,f_0$ are uniformly smooth and $B\ge B_{\min}>0$. On the region $\|(f_0(x)-ez)/w\|<\pi/2$, prescribe $$p(x,z,v)=B(x)\tan\bigl((f_0(x)-ez)/w\bigr). \label{eq:graph-prescription-tangent}$$* *In either case there is a smooth solution of bounded height with uniform bounds on all its derivatives, and it lies uniformly inside the stated open prescription region. Its angle satisfies $v_z\ge v_0>0$. In Case <span class="upright">(i)</span> it satisfies $$c_{\min}:=\delta\sqrt{\frac{\epsilon a}{\epsilon a+H_*}} \ \le\ f:=z/N\ \le 2. \label{eq:graph-height-positive}$$ In Case <span class="upright">(ii)</span> there is $\eta>0$ such that $$\left\|\frac{f_0-ez}{w}\right\|\le\frac{\pi}{2}-\eta; \qquad \|ez-f_0\|<\frac{\pi w}{2},\quad \|z\|\le\frac{\\|f_0\\|_\infty+\pi w/2}{e}.$$ The constants $v_0,\eta$ and the derivative bounds may depend on all the indicated parameters and common bounds of the data. The solution is unique among bounded-height solutions whose $C^{2,\alpha}$ norms in all buffered charts are uniformly bounded for some $0<\alpha<1$, and whose values have a uniform margin inside the prescription region. Each such solution may have its own exponent, bound, and margin.* *The graph metric $h_z=h+dz\otimes dz$ is again complete and has bounded geometry, and $\phi_z=\phi-\log L_z$ has uniform positive-order derivatives. In particular, $$e^{\phi_z}dV_{h_z}=e^\phi dV_h,\qquad \|dz\|_{h_z}^2=1-v_z^2<1.$$ For the family assertion, let $\mathcal T$ be a standard Borel parameter space indexing complete problems of a fixed dimension. Assume a common chart radius and buffers, common bounds for the metric, its inverse, and all their chart derivatives, and common bounds for the positive-order derivatives of $\phi$. Require common bounds, including order zero, for all chart derivatives of $F_i,\beta$ in Case <span class="upright">(i)</span> or $B,f_0$ in Case <span class="upright">(ii)</span>. In Case <span class="upright">(i)</span>, let $N,\epsilon,a,\delta$ range in fixed compact subsets of their permitted positive ranges, and require a common finite upper bound on $H_*$. In Case <span class="upright">(ii)</span>, let $e,w$ range in fixed compact subsets of $(0,\infty)$, and require $B\ge B_{\min}$ for one common $B_{\min}>0$. Suppose also that the data and chart overlaps are Borel and that the charts accessible along each leaf admit a countable Borel enumeration. Then the preceding estimates for the solutions and their graph metrics and weights hold with constants common to the family, including a common positive height margin in Case <span class="upright">(i)</span> and a common margin from the poles in Case <span class="upright">(ii)</span>. The solutions are Borel in the chart representations and commute with any equivariant identifications of the complete problems.
  - Explanation: The positive and tangent prescriptions have bounded smooth solutions on complete leaves under the stated uniform geometry and coefficient bounds. Height barriers and an angle bound give regularity; strict height monotonicity gives uniqueness in the stated class and Borel equivariance for uniformly controlled families.
- **claim-localized-positive-graph** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-graph-localization)): Let $X,h,\phi$ satisfy the hypotheses of Lemma <a href="#lem:graph-prescription" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:graph-prescription">6</a>, with $X$ connected, and let $h_{\mathrm{ref}}\le h$ be a complete second metric. Require the coefficients of $h_{\mathrm{ref}}$, its inverse, and all their coordinate derivatives to have uniform bounds in the same buffered charts used for $h$. Suppose $F_i$ is uniformly smooth, $\|F_i\|\le1$, with compact support $K$, and let $\alpha\ge0$ be a uniformly smooth function. Fix $$\epsilon>0,\qquad N^2=l\epsilon,\qquad a=\tfrac12+\theta,\qquad 0<l\le1,\qquad l\le\theta\le\tfrac1{16}.$$ There is a radius $R_{\mathrm{loc}}$, depending only on the dimension and a fixed sufficiently small numerical constant $c_0>0$, with the following property. For every sufficiently large $H_*$, one can choose smooth functions $\beta_s\ge0$ and $0\le\chi\le1$ such that, with $\beta=\beta_s+\alpha$, $$0\le\beta\le H_*, \qquad N\|d\beta_s\|_h \le c_0\bigl(\theta\epsilon^{3/2}+\beta_s^{3/2}\bigr). \label{eq:graph-spatial-cutoff}$$ The function $\beta_s$ vanishes on a neighborhood of $K$. The function $\chi$ is one there, is supported in $\{\mathop{\mathrm{dist}}_{h_{\mathrm{ref}}}(x,K)<R_{\mathrm{loc}}\}$, and changes from one to zero only within a region where $F_i=0$ and $\beta=H_*$. This region and the support of $d\chi$ have fixed positive buffers in $h_{\mathrm{ref}}$, independent of $H_*$. The derivatives of $\chi$ are bounded independently of $H_*$, $\chi$ is flat on its zero set, and $\|d\chi\|_h^2\le C\chi$.* *Set $\delta=H_*^{-2}$, and let $\widetilde f=z/N$ be the complete solution of <a href="#eq:graph-prescription-positive" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:graph-prescription-positive">[eq:graph-prescription-positive]</a> for this $\beta,\delta$. Define $f=\chi\widetilde f$ and form the graph of height $Nf$. Denote its quantities by $L,U,D,\mathrm{II}$, put $A=\|U\|_h$, and set, where $f>0$, $$ND^0=\epsilon a(F_i^2/f-f)+A^2/(2f). \label{eq:graph-ideal-prescription}$$ The expressions $A^2/f$ and $ND^0$ are extended by zero where $f=0$. The bump $f$ is smooth, $0\le f\le2$, is positive on $K$, and is flat on its zero set. On the region $\chi=1$ it satisfies $$ND=ND^0-\beta f+\epsilon a\delta^2/f . \label{eq:graph-core-prescription}$$ On the region where the cutoff changes, the following quantities tend uniformly to zero as $H_*\to\infty$, with the preceding data and parameters fixed: $$\begin{gathered} N^2\bigl\|\mathcal S(h+N^2df\otimes df,\phi-\log L) -\mathcal S(h,\phi)\bigr\|,\quad A^2/f,\quad \|ND^0\|,\quad \beta f,\\ \|ND\|,\quad N\|\mathrm{II}\|,\quad N\|d\log L\|_{h+N^2df\otimes df}. \end{gathered} \label{eq:graph-cutoff-small}$$ In particular, on all of $X$ the function $\mathrm{err}:=ND-(ND^0-\beta f)$ tends uniformly to zero. All data retain the corresponding uniform bounds for each fixed choice of $H_*$, with the positive-order convention for weights. For the family assertion, let a standard Borel parameter space index such problems of a fixed dimension. Require common sizes and buffers for these simultaneous charts, and common bounds there for $h,h^{-1},h_{\mathrm{ref}},h_{\mathrm{ref}}^{-1}$ and all their coordinate derivatives. Require common bounds for all derivatives, including values, of $F_i,\alpha$, and for the positive-order derivatives of $\phi$. Let $(\epsilon,l,\theta)$ range in a fixed compact subset of the permitted parameter set. Require the data, including $h_{\mathrm{ref}}$, to be Borel in the charts and preserved by the equivariant identifications under consideration. Require also the Borel chart overlaps and countable Borel enumerations of accessible charts in Lemma <a href="#lem:graph-prescription" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:graph-prescription">6</a>. Then the choices are Borel and commute with those identifications; their bounds for each fixed $H_*$, and the limits above as $H_*\to\infty$, are uniform over the family. When $K$ is empty one may take $f=0$ and omit the operation.
  - Explanation: A complete positive graph can be localized near a compact coordinate support. An outer forcing plateau makes the cutoff errors uniformly small while preserving smoothness, flatness at the zero set, and Borel equivariance under the stated family controls.
- **claim-ordered-probability-sweep** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-sweep)): There is a numerical $c>0$ with the following property. For a sweep without boost, set $\psi=0$. For a boosted sweep, given $\epsilon_*>0$, there are constants $\kappa>0$, $c_*>0$, and $C_*<\infty$, depending only on $\epsilon_*$, for which one may use $\psi=\kappa\Psi$ whenever $\epsilon\ge\epsilon_*$.* *Let $h_0,\phi_0,F$ be a smooth equivariant Borel family on $\widetilde M\times\Omega$, with bounds uniform in the lifted charts for the instance. Suppose $h_0$ is complete, its geometry and the positive-order derivatives of $\phi_0$ have the uniform bounds above, and $$F=(F_i)_{i\in\mathcal L},\qquad F_i\ge0,\qquad \sum_iF_i^2=1$$ has finitely many free label orbits and uniformly locally finite compact coordinate supports. For each orbit representative, suppose all its transverse supports lie in a fixed compact subset of $\widetilde M$. Let $h_{\mathrm{ref}}\le h_0$ be a complete equivariant Borel leafwise metric whose coefficients, inverse, and all coordinate derivatives have uniform bounds in the same lifted buffered charts as $h_0$. Suppose a Borel equivariant coloring $\chi:\mathcal L\times\Omega\to\{1,\ldots,k\}$, $k<\infty$, has been chosen such that, for each retained $\omega$ and distinct labels $i,j$ with $\chi(i,\omega)=\chi(j,\omega)$, the $R_{\mathrm{loc}}$-neighborhoods in $h_{\mathrm{ref}}(\omega)$ of $\mathop{\mathrm{supp}}F_i(\cdot,\omega)$ and $\mathop{\mathrm{supp}}F_j(\cdot,\omega)$ are disjoint. Fix the pass parameters in <a href="#eq:sweep-parameters" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:sweep-parameters">[eq:sweep-parameters]</a>, with $\epsilon\ge\epsilon_*$ in the boosted case.* *For any $\delta_0,\eta_0>0$, there is a candidate sweep as above with smooth nonnegative bumps $f_i\le2$, supported in the indicated enlargements, positive on the supports of $F_i$, and flat on their zero sets. Its output is given by <a href="#eq:sweep-output" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:sweep-output">[eq:sweep-output]</a>. The lower bound for $r$ is uniform for the instance. The bumps, coefficients, all intermediate and final graph metrics and weights, their smooth graph quantities, and $Y$ are equivariant and Borel and retain the corresponding uniform chart bounds, with the positive-order convention for weights. The map $Y$ is again a root probability map with the same label orbits.* *For each $i$, the coefficient furnished by Lemma <a href="#lem:graph-localization" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:graph-localization">7</a> has the form $$\beta_i=\beta_{s,i}+\epsilon\psi(R_i),\qquad R_i=\sum_{j<i}f_j^2 , \label{eq:sweep-beta}$$ Here $\beta_{s,i}$ vanishes near $\mathop{\mathrm{supp}}F_i$, and the coefficients satisfy <a href="#eq:graph-spatial-cutoff" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:graph-spatial-cutoff">[eq:graph-spatial-cutoff]</a> in $h_{i-1}$. Writing $$ND_i^0=\epsilon a(F_i^2/f_i-f_i)+A_i^2/(2f_i)$$ on $\{f_i>0\}$, extended by zero off the bump, one has $$ND_i=ND_i^0-\beta_i f_i+\mathrm{err}_i,\qquad \sum_i\|\mathrm{err}_i\|^2\le\eta_0 . \label{eq:sweep-prescription-error}$$* *Define the nonnegative reserve $$\begin{split} \Pi_p:={}& T_0+c\epsilon\bigl(M_0^2+\mathbb E_\nu V_i^2+\theta\mathop{\mathrm{Var}}_\nu u\bigr) \\ &+c\frac r\epsilon\mathcal X +c_*\epsilon r^6\mathbf 1_{\{r\ge C_*\}}, \end{split} \label{eq:sweep-positive-reserve}$$ where the final term is omitted in a sweep without boost. Then, pointwise, $$\begin{aligned} Q:=\frac{rN^2}{\epsilon}\bigl[ &\mathcal S(h_+,\phi_+)-\mathcal S(h_0,\phi_0) +\epsilon e_{h_0}(F)-\epsilon e_{h_+}(Y)\bigr] \notag\\ &\ge\Pi_p-4\theta^2m-\frac r\epsilon\delta_0 . \label{eq:sweep-inequality} \end{aligned}$$ The numerical constant $c$ is independent of the instance, the number of colors, and the pass parameters. The errors $\delta_0,\eta_0$ can be prescribed independently before the pass.
  - Explanation: An ordered sweep applies localized graphs to probability coordinates separated by colors. Normalization produces a new root probability map, and the weighted scalar and energy balance retains a positive reserve with independently prescribed analytic errors.
- **claim-entropy-metrics-controlled-labels** (construction, [source](#stmt-thm-entropy)): Let $(M^n,g)$ be a closed connected Riemannian manifold, where $n\geq3$ and $\mathop{\mathrm{Scal}}_g=-1$, and put $\Gamma=\pi_1(M)$. Let $(\Omega_0,\mu_0)$ be a standard Borel probability space with a measure-preserving $\Gamma$-action; the one-point space is allowed. Suppose $\mathcal L_0$ is a countable set with finitely many free $\Gamma$-orbits and $$F^{\mathrm{in}}:\widetilde M\times\Omega_0\longrightarrow \ell^2(\mathcal L_0),\qquad F^{\mathrm{in}}_\ell\geq0,\qquad \sum_{\ell\in\mathcal L_0}(F^{\mathrm{in}}_\ell)^2=1$$ is equivariant, Borel in the transverse variable, and smooth along $\widetilde M$. Assume the following uniformity for this input. In charts lifted from a fixed finite atlas on $M$, with fixed inner buffers, the supports are locally finite with a uniform bound on their number, and all leafwise derivatives of the coordinate functions have uniform bounds. For each orbit representative $\ell$ there is a compact set $K_\ell\subset\widetilde M$ such that $\mathop{\mathrm{supp}}F^{\mathrm{in}}_{\gamma\ell}(\,\cdot\,,\omega) \subset\gamma K_\ell$ for all $\gamma\in\Gamma$ and almost every $\omega$. The bounds and compact sets in these assumptions may depend on the input.* *For every prescribed elementary function $\mathcal B$ and every finite number $E_{\min}$, there are a standard Borel probability $\Gamma$-space $(\Omega,\mu)$ extending $(\Omega_0,\mu_0)$, a countable set $\mathcal L$ with finitely many free $\Gamma$-orbits, and equivariant leafwise data on $\widetilde M\times\Omega$: a metric $h$, a function $\phi$, a positive density $\rho$, and a root probability map $F$ with labels $\mathcal L$. There are also $E>E_{\min}$, an integer $q\geq n+1$, and a constant $C=C(n,\mathcal B)<\infty$ such that, on an invariant conull set of transverse parameters, $$\label{eq:entropy-contract} \begin{split} &\rho\leq dV_g,\qquad W:=\log\frac{dV_h}{\rho}\geq0,\\ &\mathcal S(h,\phi)\geq -C+2\left(W-\frac n2\log E\right)+E e_h(F), \qquad e_h(F)^{1/2}\leq E^{-1/100},\\ &E>\mathcal B(q). \end{split}$$ Here the extension means that there is an equivariant measure-preserving map $\Omega\to\Omega_0$. All output objects are Borel transversely and smooth along leaves. In the same lifted charts, the coefficients of $h$, their derivatives of every order, and the coefficients of $h^{-1}$ have uniform bounds; the function $\phi$, the density ratio $\rho/dV_g$, its reciprocal, and their derivatives of every order have uniform bounds. The coordinates of $F$ have the same derivative and uniform local finiteness properties as the input. There are equivariant compact sets containing their supports, uniformly in the transverse parameter. All these output bounds may depend on the input and on the chosen finite construction.* *There is, in addition, a fixed $\Gamma$-invariant graph $\mathcal G$ on $\mathcal L$, independent of the transverse parameter, of finite maximum degree $\Delta$. It contains an edge between every pair of distinct labels that are simultaneously nonzero at any point of any leaf. We may take $$q\geq \max\{n+1,\#(\Gamma\backslash\mathcal L),\Delta+1\}$$ and a Borel equivariant coloring $\chi:\mathcal L\times\Omega\to\{1,\ldots,q\}$ such that labels of graph distance one or two have different colors. The set $\mathcal L$ can be taken to consist of finitely many copies of $\mathcal L_0$. The constant $C$ is independent of the manifold, metric, input family, all the uniform bounds just specified, and $E_{\min}$.
  - Explanation: The entropy construction supplies a retained density below the original density, a weighted scalar inequality with an arbitrarily large energy coefficient, and an absolutely small gradient. A fixed finite-degree overlap graph and separated coloring control the label complexity independently of the transverse parameter.
- **claim-conformal-bootstrap** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-entropy-bootstrap)): There are constants $\epsilon_*>0$ and $C_{\mathrm{boot}}<4$, depending only on $n$, with the following property. Starting with the input of Theorem <a href="#thm:entropy" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:entropy">9</a>, finitely many sweeps without boost, followed individually by conformal changes, produce a metric $h_*$, a root probability map $F_*$, and $\epsilon_0\in[\epsilon_*,2\epsilon_*]$ such that $$\label{eq:entropy-bootstrap-output} \rho:=dV_{h_*}\leq dV_g,\qquad \mathop{\mathrm{Scal}}_{h_*}\geq-C_{\mathrm{boot}}+\epsilon_0e_{h_*}(F_*).$$ The metric and map have the uniformity and support properties in Theorem <a href="#thm:entropy" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:entropy">9</a>. The number of sweeps may depend on the input.
  - Explanation: A finite input-dependent sequence of unboosted sweeps and conformal resets raises the energy coefficient to a dimensional positive range while contracting the volume density. Its scalar cost remains dimensional.
- **claim-entropy-sweep-increment** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-entropy-increment)): There are dimensional constants $m_0>0$, $D_*\geq1$, $l_0>0$, $\gamma_n>0$, and $C_n<\infty$ with the following property. For $\epsilon\geq\epsilon_*$, take a boosted sweep with $$0<l\leq l_0,\qquad \theta=D_*l\leq\tfrac1{16}, \qquad N^2=l\epsilon.$$ The errors can be chosen so that $$\label{eq:entropy-increment} \begin{split} \mathcal S(h_+,\phi_+)-(1+l)\epsilon e_{h_+}(Y) \geq{}&\mathcal S(h_0,\phi_0)-\epsilon e_{h_0}(F)\\ &+2W_p-n\log(1+l)-C_nl^2+P_p , \end{split}$$ where the nonnegative, pointwise surplus is $$\label{eq:entropy-surplus-definition} P_p=\gamma_n\frac{\epsilon}{rN^2}\Pi_p\, \mathbf 1_{\{m\geq m_0\}\,\cup\,\{\|1-r\|\geq1/4\}}.$$ In particular, on the indicated region $P_p$ retains a fixed fraction of every positive term in the sweep inequality.
  - Explanation: A boosted sweep increases the energy coefficient while paying for logarithmic volume growth and retaining surplus away from the critical region. The scalar loss is quadratic in the step size.
- **claim-surplus-volume-and-derivatives** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-entropy-surplus)): For every sweep of Lemma <a href="#lem:entropy-increment" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:entropy-increment">11</a>, $$\label{eq:entropy-pass-volume} W_p\leq C_n\epsilon^{1/4}(\epsilon+P_p)^{3/4}.$$ There is also a dimensional $\epsilon_{\mathrm{exit}}$ such that, if $\epsilon\geq\epsilon_{\mathrm{exit}}$ and $r\leq1/4$, then $$\label{eq:entropy-last-surplus} \begin{split} P_p&\geq c_n\frac{\epsilon}{lr} \left(1+\frac{m^2}{\epsilon^2}\right),\\ \sum_i\bigl(D_i^2+\|\mathrm{II}_i\|^2+\|d\log L_i\|_{h_i}^2\bigr) &\leq C_n\frac{P_p}{\theta}. \end{split}$$ The errors in the prescription may simultaneously be chosen to have $\sum_i\|\mathrm{err}_i\|^2\leq1$.
  - Explanation: The retained surplus bounds the volume logarithm of a sweep. In the small-normalization region and above the exit threshold it also controls the graph curvatures and logarithmic derivatives needed for a conformal exit.
- **claim-entropy-pass-accumulation** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-entropy-accumulation)): Suppose at parameter $\epsilon_{\mathrm{in}}\geq\epsilon_*$ that $$\mathcal S(h,\phi)\geq-C+2W-n\log\epsilon_{\mathrm{in}} +\epsilon_{\mathrm{in}}e_h(F)+P_{\mathrm{in}}, \qquad W=\log(dV_h/\rho)\geq0,\quad P_{\mathrm{in}}\geq0.$$ Successive sweeps of Lemma <a href="#lem:entropy-increment" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:entropy-increment">11</a>, with $\epsilon_{\mathrm{new}}=(1+l(\epsilon))\epsilon$, preserve this form of inequality: at the final parameter $E$ the surplus is $P=P_{\mathrm{in}}+\sum_pP_p$, the volume logarithm is $W_{\mathrm{new}}=W+\sum_pW_p$, and the increase of the scalar constant is $C_n\sum_p l(\epsilon_p)^2$. Along the entire increasing sequence of parameters, $$\label{eq:entropy-summability} \sum_p l(\epsilon_p)^2\leq C_n,\qquad \#\{p:\epsilon_p<E\}\leq C_n(1+\log^3(1+E)).$$ If the initial volume logarithm is zero, then, for all sufficiently large dimensional $E$, $$\label{eq:entropy-phase-volume} W_{\mathrm{new}}\leq E^{0.26}(E+P)^{0.75}.$$ The same assertion holds if the initial data have parameter $E_0$, surplus $P_0$, and $$W\leq E_0^{0.32}(E_0+P_0)^{0.75},$$ provided $E\geq E_0$ is sufficiently large dimensionally and $E_0^{0.32}\leq\tfrac12E^{0.26}$.
  - Explanation: Successive increments telescope the scalar-energy balance, add the nonnegative surplus and volume logarithms, and incur a summable scalar cost. The scheduled step sizes bound the number of sweeps and yield the stated phase volume estimates.
- **claim-phase-exit-small-gradient** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-entropy-phase-exit)): Consider a phase starting with root map $Y^0$ and metric $h^0$, and let $K_0$ be a finite uniform bound for $e_{h^0}(Y^0)^{1/2}$. Suppose its last sweep has input parameter $\epsilon$, output parameter $E=(1+l(\epsilon))\epsilon$, and output $(h_+,\phi_+,Y)$. Suppose, with $C\geq0$ and $P\geq P_p$ including the surplus from that last sweep, that $$\label{eq:entropy-before-exit} \begin{split} \mathcal S(h_+,\phi_+)&\geq-C+2W-n\log E+E e_{h_+}(Y)+P,\\ W&=\log(dV_{h_+}/\rho)\geq0,\qquad W\leq E^{0.26}(E+P)^{0.75}. \end{split}$$ If $E$ is sufficiently large in terms of $n,K_0$, there are a metric $\widehat h$, a weight $\widehat\phi$, and a root map $Z$ on two copies of the labels such that $$\label{eq:entropy-after-exit} \begin{split} \mathcal S(\widehat h,\widehat\phi) &\geq-(C+C_n)+2\widehat W-n\log E +E e_{\widehat h}(Z)+P',\\ \widehat W&=\log(dV_{\widehat h}/\rho)\geq0,\qquad \widehat W\leq E^{0.32}(E+P')^{0.75},\\ e_{\widehat h}(Z)^{1/2}&\leq E^{-1/100}. \end{split}$$ Here $P'\geq0$, and the added scalar cost $C_n$ is independent of $K_0$ and of all other instance constants. The changes preserve the uniform smoothness and support properties of Theorem <a href="#thm:entropy" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:entropy">9</a>.
  - Explanation: A small copy of the starting map protects the final normalization. A conformal enlargement supported where normalization is small uses the last sweep's surplus to obtain the absolute gradient bound, while preserving the entropy balance and controlled volume logarithm.
- **claim-measured-degree-estimate** (bound, [source](#stmt-thm-degree)): There are absolute constants $C_0,C_1>0$ with the following property. Let $q\ge2$, let $d$ be an integer with $0\le d\le q$, and let $\tau>0$. For $0\le r\le d$ set $$\label{eq:degree-widths} T_r=2\tau+\frac{r\tau}{q},\qquad b_r=\tau\left(1+\frac1{2q}+\frac r{4q^2}\right).$$ Suppose $(X,h,\phi,G)$ is a controlled measured $d$-band over $Q_{T_d}^d$ in the sense of Definition <a href="#def:degree-band" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="def:degree-band">15</a>. Let $B\ge1$ and $L_1,\ldots,L_d>0$. Suppose throughout $X$ that $$\label{eq:degree-horizon-bound} L_i\|dG_i\|_h\le B-\frac dq\qquad(1\le i\le d),$$ and suppose that $$\label{eq:degree-horizon} \frac{C_0(1+B)^{2/3}}{L_i}<\frac{\tau}{10q} \qquad(1\le i\le d).$$ Define $H_i(G)=2(L_i\|dG_i\|_h-1)_+$. Then $$\label{eq:degree-estimate} \|\mathcal D_X(G)\|(2\tau)^d \le \frac{\exp(C_1d/q)}{\prod_{i=1}^dL_i} \int_{\{\|G\|_\infty<b_d\}} \exp\left(\frac{-\mathcal S(h,\phi)+\sum_{i=1}^dH_i(G)}2\right)dV_h.$$ For $d=0$ the product is one, the sum is zero, and $\mathcal S(h,\phi)=0$; the assertion is the bound of a signed count by the unsigned count.
  - Explanation: For a controlled measured band satisfying the scaled coordinate-gradient and strict horizon bounds, the absolute measured degree is controlled by an exponential weighted-scalar integral with separate coordinate penalties. The scalar and volume factors cancel during dimension descent.
- **claim-measured-degree-descent** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-degree-descent)): Assume the hypotheses of Theorem <a href="#thm:degree" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:degree">16</a> with $d\ge1$, put $p=d-1$, and put $$\Theta=\exp\left(\frac{-\mathcal S(h,\phi)+\sum_{i=1}^dH_i(G)}2\right).$$ There is a Borel family of oriented regular hypersurface portions $\Sigma_s$ in the leaves, $-\tau<s<\tau$, and, for almost every $s$, a positive function $v_s$ on $\Sigma_s$, smooth along it and Borel in its charts, with the following properties. Transverse null subsets and their saturations may be removed for each such $s$.* 1. *For every nonnegative Borel function $F$ supported in $\{\|G\|_\infty<b_d\}$, $$\label{eq:degree-coarea} \int_{-\tau}^{\tau}\!ds\int_{\Sigma_s}v_sF\,dA_h \le\int_XF\,dV_h.$$* 2. *For each such $s$ there is a controlled measured $p$-band $(X_s^+,h_s^+,\phi_s^+,J_s^+)$ over $Q_{T_p}^p$ of degree $\mathcal D_X(G)$. Its coordinates are indexed by $j=2,\ldots,d$ and satisfy $$L_j\|dJ_{s,j}^+\|_{h_s^+}\le B-\frac pq.$$ With $H_j^+=2(L_j\|dJ_{s,j}^+\|_{h_s^+}-1)_+$, there is an absolute constant $C_*>0$ such that $$\label{eq:degree-child-integral} \begin{split} &\int_{\{\|J_s^+\|_\infty<b_p\}} \exp\left(\frac{-\mathcal S(h_s^+,\phi_s^+)+\sum_{j=2}^dH_j^+}{2}\right) dV_{h_s^+}\\ &\hspace{25mm}\le \frac{e^{C_*/q}}{L_1} \int_{\Sigma_s\cap\{\|G\|_\infty<b_d\}}v_s\Theta\,dA_h. \end{split}$$ In dimension $p=0$ the left side is the measured unsigned count.* *The child in the second assertion may be chosen separately for each typical $s$; no measurability of those choices in $s$ is asserted or needed.
  - Explanation: An ordered family of minimizing boundaries provides positive normal speeds and a coarea inequality. Each typical slice supports a regular controlled child of the same degree, with an integral bounded by the parent slice integral; child selection need not be measurable in the slicing parameter.
- **claim-normalized-scalar-volume** (bound, [source](#stmt-thm-scalar-volume)): For every integer $n\geq 3$, there is a finite constant $C_n>0$ such that every closed connected oriented smooth $n$-manifold $M$ and every smooth Riemannian metric $g$ with $\mathop{\mathrm{Scal}}_g\equiv-1$ satisfy $$\\|M\\|\leq C_n\mathop{\mathrm{Vol}}_g(M).$$
  - Explanation: Metrics of normalized negative constant scalar curvature satisfy a dimension-only upper bound on simplicial volume by ordinary volume. This is the quantitative estimate required by the conformal reduction.
- **claim-flat-sheet-detector** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-transfer-flat)): Put $\delta=(1000\sqrt q)^{-1}$. There is an oriented flat smooth $q$-manifold $\mathcal U_\omega$ with a local isometry $z:\mathcal U_\omega\to\mathbb R^q$, and an inclusion of $K_\omega$ into it, such that the image of every $y\in K_\omega$ has a flat ball chart of radius $10\delta$ centered at $z_y$. This construction is a standard Borel family in $\omega$, with countably many charts and an equivariant $\Gamma$-action. The map $F$ lifts smoothly to this family, and, writing $z_F=z\circ F$, $$e_h(z_F)=e_h(F)=\mathfrak e.$$ There is a closed smooth $n$-form, again denoted $\eta$, obtained in these flat charts by pulling back the detector form through the cutoff probabilities constructed below. It is Borel and equivariant in the family, and satisfies $$\label{eq:transfer-form} \int_{\mathcal M}F^*\eta=I,\qquad \|\eta\|_{\mathrm{HS}}\leq C_n.$$ Here the exterior Hilbert norm uses the Euclidean metric on the charts. For every fixed derivative order its derivatives admit an elementary bound in $q$.
  - Explanation: Compatible label configurations admit separate flat sheets. The lifted probability map preserves its energy, while cutoff probabilities produce a closed detector form with a dimension-only exterior Hilbert norm and controlled derivatives.
- **claim-unbiased-incidence-rounding** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-transfer-rounding)): There is an integer $D_0\geq1$, bounded by an elementary function of $q$ and constant in $V,s,\omega$, and a Borel equivariant random choice of coefficients $u'_C\in D_0^{-1}\mathbb Z$ such that $$\label{eq:transfer-rounding} \mathbb E_{\rm rd}u'_C=u_C,\qquad \|u'_C-u_C\|\leq r^nq^{-n}.$$ The expectation is conditional on the grid and entropy data. Every cocycle equation just described is satisfied exactly by the $u'_C$.
  - Explanation: The coefficient vector is rounded within the joint kernel of its integer incidence equations. The common denominator is uniform over the auxiliary parameters, expectation is preserved, and all required cubical balance equations hold exactly.
- **claim-smooth-measured-resolution** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-transfer-resolution)): For the diagram $\mathcal P$ there is a standard Borel measured family $\mathcal A$ of oriented smooth $m$-manifolds and a smooth map $X:\mathcal A\to\mathcal U_\omega$, equivariant and Borel in all the data. The integration identities below are for fixed grid, entropy, and rounding data, including $(V,s,\omega,\xi)$; their integrals use the conditional state measure and depend Borelly on those data. The points over $\mathcal T_\delta$ represented by original chamber tiles form a Borel leaf-open subset $\mathcal A_{\rm orig}\subset X^{-1}(\mathcal T_\delta)$. For every compactly supported smooth $m$-form $\alpha$ on $\mathcal T_\delta$, $$\label{eq:transfer-local-push} \int_{\mathcal A_{\rm orig}}X^*\alpha=Z_{\rm rd}(\alpha).$$ For the full doubled diagram, measured oriented integration also gives $$\label{eq:transfer-push} X_*[\mathcal A]=[\mathcal P].$$ For a parameter $0<\lambda<1/2$ and a choice $$\label{eq:transfer-gamma} 0<2\gamma_0<(m+1)^{-(m+1)}\lambda^{m(m+1)},$$ there are a smooth metric $k$ and a closed seam set in each tile such that $\\|dX\\|_k\leq1$, and $k=X^*k_{\rm flat}$ off the seam. There are buffered smooth charts with elementary overlap, metric, inverse-metric, and fixed-order derivative bounds whenever $\lambda^{-1}$ and $\gamma_0^{-1}$ are elementary. In the sorted charts for each tile, the seam is contained in the union of the slabs $\|Z_j\|\leq\lambda$, $1\leq j\leq m$. The $k$-volume density in those charts has an elementary upper bound chosen before $\lambda,\gamma_0$; in particular this bound does not depend on these two parameters.* *One may prescribe for each chamber over a dual cube a rotation $O_\sigma\in SO(q)$. There is also a smooth Borel equivariant $\mathcal R:\mathcal A\to SO(q)$ equal to $O_\sigma$ off the seam in that tile, equal to the identity near all tile facets, and homotopic to the identity. Its fixed-order chart derivatives and $\\|d\mathcal R\\|_k$ are elementarily bounded under the same hypotheses.
  - Explanation: A reflected measured family resolves the doubled diagram into smooth oriented leaves and preserves its current. The original side remains identifiable near the detector, while the seam has small coordinate volume and a metric-density upper bound fixed before its width is chosen.
- **claim-comparison-degree-pairing** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-transfer-degree)): The band in <a href="#eq:transfer-comparison" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:transfer-comparison">[eq:transfer-comparison]</a> satisfies the chart and integration control assumptions of Theorem <a href="#thm:degree" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:degree">16</a>, for each fixed instance, the product metric $h+L^2k$ with any fixed $L>0$, and the pullback of the entropy weight $\phi$. Its degree is Borel in the auxiliary parameters and satisfies $$\label{eq:transfer-degree} \mathbb E_{\rm rd}\mathcal D=D_0I$$ for every fixed $V,s$.
  - Explanation: The compatible original-side comparison band has the required chart and integration controls. Its measured degree has conditional expectation equal to the cleared cocycle pairing, through local current pushforward and a cubical primitive for the difference of detector forms.

Suppose $F_i,\\beta$ are uniformly smooth, $|F_i|\\le1$, and $0\\le\\beta\\le H_*$. On the region $z>0$, prescribe $$p(x,z,v)=\n       \\frac{\\epsilon a(F_i(x)^2+\\delta^2)+(1-v^2)/2}{z}\n           -(\\epsilon a+\\beta(x))\\frac{z}{N^2}.\n       \\label{eq:graph-prescription-positive}$$*\n\n2.  *Let $e,w>0$ be constants. On the region $|(f_0(x)-ez)/w|<\\pi/2$, prescribe $$p(x,z,v)=B(x)\\tan\\bigl((f_0(x)-ez)/w\\bigr).\n       \\label{eq:graph-prescription-tangent}$$*\n\n\n*In either case there is a smooth solution of bounded height with uniform bounds on all its derivatives, and it lies uniformly inside the stated open prescription region. The solution is unique among bounded-height solutions whose $C^{2,\\alpha}$ norms in all buffered charts are uniformly bounded for some $0<\\alpha<1$, and whose values have a uniform margin inside the prescription region. Each such solution may have its own exponent, bound, and margin.*\n\n*The graph metric $h_z=h+dz\\otimes dz$ is again complete and has bounded geometry, and $\\phi_z=\\phi-\\log L_z$ has uniform positive-order derivatives. Then the preceding estimates for the solutions and their graph metrics and weights hold with constants common to the family, including a common positive height margin in Case <span class=\"upright\">(i)</span> and a common margin from the poles in Case <span class=\"upright\">(ii)</span>. On the region $|(f_0(x)-ez)/w|<\\pi/2$, prescribe $$p(x,z,v)=B(x)\\tan\\bigl((f_0(x)-ez)/w\\bigr).\n       \\label{eq:graph-prescription-tangent}$$*\n\n\n*In either case there is a smooth solution of bounded height with uniform bounds on all its derivatives, and it lies uniformly inside the stated open prescription region. Its angle satisfies $v_z\\ge v_0>0$. In Case <span class=\"upright\">(i)</span> it satisfies $$c_{\\min}:=\\delta\\sqrt{\\frac{\\epsilon a}{\\epsilon a+H_*}}\n       \\ \\le\\ f:=z/N\\ \\le 2.\n \\label{eq:graph-height-positive}$$ In Case <span class=\"upright\">(ii)</span> there is $\\eta>0$ such that $$\\left|\\frac{f_0-ez}{w}\\right|\\le\\frac{\\pi}{2}-\\eta;\n \\qquad\n |ez-f_0|<\\frac{\\pi w}{2},\\quad\n |z|\\le\\frac{\\|f_0\\|_\\infty+\\pi w/2}{e}.$$ The constants $v_0,\\eta$ and the derivative bounds may depend on all the indicated parameters and common bounds of the data. The solution is unique among bounded-height solutions whose $C^{2,\\alpha}$ norms in all buffered charts are uniformly bounded for some $0<\\alpha<1$, and whose values have a uniform margin inside the prescription region. Each such solution may have its own exponent, bound, and margin.*\n\n*The graph metric $h_z=h+dz\\otimes dz$ is again complete and has bounded geometry, and $\\phi_z=\\phi-\\log L_z$ has uniform positive-order derivatives. In particular, $$e^{\\phi_z}dV_{h_z}=e^\\phi dV_h,\\qquad\n |dz|_{h_z}^2=1-v_z^2<1.$$ For the family assertion, let $\\mathcal T$ be a standard Borel parameter space indexing complete problems of a fixed dimension. Assume a common chart radius and buffers, common bounds for the metric, its inverse, and all their chart derivatives, and common bounds for the positive-order derivatives of $\\phi$. Require common bounds, including order zero, for all chart derivatives of $F_i,\\beta$ in Case <span class=\"upright\">(i)</span> or $B,f_0$ in Case <span class=\"upright\">(ii)</span>. In Case <span class=\"upright\">(i)</span>, let $N,\\epsilon,a,\\delta$ range in fixed compact subsets of their permitted positive ranges, and require a common finite upper bound on $H_*$. In Case <span class=\"upright\">(ii)</span>, let $e,w$ range in fixed compact subsets of $(0,\\infty)$, and require $B\\ge B_{\\min}$ for one common $B_{\\min}>0$. Suppose also that the data and chart overlaps are Borel and that the charts accessible along each leaf admit a countable Borel enumeration. Then the preceding estimates for the solutions and their graph metrics and weights hold with constants common to the family, including a common positive height margin in Case <span class=\"upright\">(i)</span> and a common margin from the poles in Case <span class=\"upright\">(ii)</span>. The solutions are Borel in the chart representations and commute with any equivariant identifications of the complete problems.",
      "explanation": "The positive and tangent prescriptions have bounded smooth solutions on complete leaves under the stated uniform geometry and coefficient bounds. Height barriers and an angle bound give regularity; strict height monotonicity gives uniqueness in the stated class and Borel equivariance for uniformly controlled families.",
      "fidelity": "PENDING",

        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-graph-prescription",
          "exact_quote": "Let $(X,h)$ be a complete smooth Riemannian manifold without boundary, with bounded geometry as above, and suppose the positive-order derivatives of $\\phi$ are uniformly bounded.",
          "source_id": "PM-335.1"
        },
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-graph-prescription",
          "exact_quote": "Let $N,\\epsilon,a>0$, $0<\\delta\\le1$, and $H_*\\ge0$. Suppose $F_i,\\beta$ are uniformly smooth, $|F_i|\\le1$, and $0\\le\\beta\\le H_*$.",
          "source_id": "PM-335.1"
        },

          "anchor": "stmt-lem-graph-prescription",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-graph-localization",
        "exact_quote": "Let $X,h,\\phi$ satisfy the hypotheses of Lemma <a href=\"#lem:graph-prescription\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:graph-prescription\">6</a>, with $X$ connected, and let $h_{\\mathrm{ref}}\\le h$ be a complete second metric. Require the coefficients of $h_{\\mathrm{ref}}$, its inverse, and all their coordinate derivatives to have uniform bounds in the same buffered charts used for $h$. Suppose $F_i$ is uniformly smooth, $|F_i|\\le1$, with compact support $K$, and let $\\alpha\\ge0$ be a uniformly smooth function. Fix $$\\epsilon>0,\\qquad N^2=l\\epsilon,\\qquad\n a=\\tfrac12+\\theta,\\qquad 0<l\\le1,\\qquad l\\le\\theta\\le\\tfrac1{16}.$$ There is a radius $R_{\\mathrm{loc}}$, depending only on the dimension and a fixed sufficiently small numerical constant $c_0>0$, with the following property. For every sufficiently large $H_*$, one can choose smooth functions $\\beta_s\\ge0$ and $0\\le\\chi\\le1$ such that, with $\\beta=\\beta_s+\\alpha$, $$0\\le\\beta\\le H_*,\n \\qquad\n N|d\\beta_s|_h\n       \\le c_0\\bigl(\\theta\\epsilon^{3/2}+\\beta_s^{3/2}\\bigr).\n \\label{eq:graph-spatial-cutoff}$$ The function $\\beta_s$ vanishes on a neighborhood of $K$. The function $\\chi$ is one there, is supported in $\\{\\mathop{\\mathrm{dist}}_{h_{\\mathrm{ref}}}(x,K)<R_{\\mathrm{loc}}\\}$, and changes from one to zero only within a region where $F_i=0$ and $\\beta=H_*$. This region and the support of $d\\chi$ have fixed positive buffers in $h_{\\mathrm{ref}}$, independent of $H_*$. The derivatives of $\\chi$ are bounded independently of $H_*$, $\\chi$ is flat on its zero set, and $|d\\chi|_h^2\\le C\\chi$.*\n\n\n\n*Set $\\delta=H_*^{-2}$, and let $\\widetilde f=z/N$ be the complete solution of <a href=\"#eq:graph-prescription-positive\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:graph-prescription-positive\">[eq:graph-prescription-positive]</a> for this $\\beta,\\delta$. Define $f=\\chi\\widetilde f$ and form the graph of height $Nf$. Denote its quantities by $L,U,D,\\mathrm{II}$, put $A=|U|_h$, and set, where $f>0$, $$ND^0=\\epsilon a(F_i^2/f-f)+A^2/(2f).\n \\label{eq:graph-ideal-prescription}$$ The expressions $A^2/f$ and $ND^0$ are extended by zero where $f=0$. The bump $f$ is smooth, $0\\le f\\le2$, is positive on $K$, and is flat on its zero set. On the region $\\chi=1$ it satisfies $$ND=ND^0-\\beta f+\\epsilon a\\delta^2/f .\n \\label{eq:graph-core-prescription}$$ On the region where the cutoff changes, the following quantities tend uniformly to zero as $H_*\\to\\infty$, with the preceding data and parameters fixed: $$\\begin{gathered}\n N^2\\bigl|\\mathcal S(h+N^2df\\otimes df,\\phi-\\log L)\n                      -\\mathcal S(h,\\phi)\\bigr|,\\quad\n A^2/f,\\quad |ND^0|,\\quad \\beta f,\\\\\n |ND|,\\quad N|\\mathrm{II}|,\\quad N|d\\log L|_{h+N^2df\\otimes df}.\n \\end{gathered}\n \\label{eq:graph-cutoff-small}$$ In particular, on all of $X$ the function $\\mathrm{err}:=ND-(ND^0-\\beta f)$ tends uniformly to zero. All data retain the corresponding uniform bounds for each fixed choice of $H_*$, with the positive-order convention for weights. For the family assertion, let a standard Borel parameter space index such problems of a fixed dimension. Require common sizes and buffers for these simultaneous charts, and common bounds there for $h,h^{-1},h_{\\mathrm{ref}},h_{\\mathrm{ref}}^{-1}$ and all their coordinate derivatives. Require common bounds for all derivatives, including values, of $F_i,\\alpha$, and for the positive-order derivatives of $\\phi$. Let $(\\epsilon,l,\\theta)$ range in a fixed compact subset of the permitted parameter set. Require the data, including $h_{\\mathrm{ref}}$, to be Borel in the charts and preserved by the equivariant identifications under consideration. Require also the Borel chart overlaps and countable Borel enumerations of accessible charts in Lemma <a href=\"#lem:graph-prescription\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:graph-prescription\">6</a>. Then the choices are Borel and commute with those identifications; their bounds for each fixed $H_*$, and the limits above as $H_*\\to\\infty$, are uniform over the family. When $K$ is empty one may take $f=0$ and omit the operation.",

      "explanation": "A complete positive graph can be localized near a compact coordinate support. An outer forcing plateau makes the cutoff errors uniformly small while preserving smoothness, flatness at the zero set, and Borel equivariance under the stated family controls.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-sweep",
        "exact_quote": "There is a numerical $c>0$ with the following property. For a sweep without boost, set $\\psi=0$. For a boosted sweep, given $\\epsilon_*>0$, there are constants $\\kappa>0$, $c_*>0$, and $C_*<\\infty$, depending only on $\\epsilon_*$, for which one may use $\\psi=\\kappa\\Psi$ whenever $\\epsilon\\ge\\epsilon_*$.*\n\n*Let $h_0,\\phi_0,F$ be a smooth equivariant Borel family on $\\widetilde M\\times\\Omega$, with bounds uniform in the lifted charts for the instance. Suppose $h_0$ is complete, its geometry and the positive-order derivatives of $\\phi_0$ have the uniform bounds above, and $$F=(F_i)_{i\\in\\mathcal L},\\qquad F_i\\ge0,\\qquad\n \\sum_iF_i^2=1$$ has finitely many free label orbits and uniformly locally finite compact coordinate supports. For each orbit representative, suppose all its transverse supports lie in a fixed compact subset of $\\widetilde M$. Let $h_{\\mathrm{ref}}\\le h_0$ be a complete equivariant Borel leafwise metric whose coefficients, inverse, and all coordinate derivatives have uniform bounds in the same lifted buffered charts as $h_0$. Suppose a Borel equivariant coloring $\\chi:\\mathcal L\\times\\Omega\\to\\{1,\\ldots,k\\}$, $k<\\infty$, has been chosen such that, for each retained $\\omega$ and distinct labels $i,j$ with $\\chi(i,\\omega)=\\chi(j,\\omega)$, the $R_{\\mathrm{loc}}$-neighborhoods in $h_{\\mathrm{ref}}(\\omega)$ of $\\mathop{\\mathrm{supp}}F_i(\\cdot,\\omega)$ and $\\mathop{\\mathrm{supp}}F_j(\\cdot,\\omega)$ are disjoint. Fix the pass parameters in <a href=\"#eq:sweep-parameters\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:sweep-parameters\">[eq:sweep-parameters]</a>, with $\\epsilon\\ge\\epsilon_*$ in the boosted case.*\n\n*For any $\\delta_0,\\eta_0>0$, there is a candidate sweep as above with smooth nonnegative bumps $f_i\\le2$, supported in the indicated enlargements, positive on the supports of $F_i$, and flat on their zero sets. Its output is given by <a href=\"#eq:sweep-output\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:sweep-output\">[eq:sweep-output]</a>. The lower bound for $r$ is uniform for the instance. The bumps, coefficients, all intermediate and final graph metrics and weights, their smooth graph quantities, and $Y$ are equivariant and Borel and retain the corresponding uniform chart bounds, with the positive-order convention for weights. The map $Y$ is again a root probability map with the same label orbits.*\n\n\n*For each $i$, the coefficient furnished by Lemma <a href=\"#lem:graph-localization\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:graph-localization\">7</a> has the form $$\\beta_i=\\beta_{s,i}+\\epsilon\\psi(R_i),\\qquad\n R_i=\\sum_{j<i}f_j^2 ,\n \\label{eq:sweep-beta}$$ Here $\\beta_{s,i}$ vanishes near $\\mathop{\\mathrm{supp}}F_i$, and the coefficients satisfy <a href=\"#eq:graph-spatial-cutoff\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:graph-spatial-cutoff\">[eq:graph-spatial-cutoff]</a> in $h_{i-1}$. Writing $$ND_i^0=\\epsilon a(F_i^2/f_i-f_i)+A_i^2/(2f_i)$$ on $\\{f_i>0\\}$, extended by zero off the bump, one has $$ND_i=ND_i^0-\\beta_i f_i+\\mathrm{err}_i,\\qquad\n \\sum_i|\\mathrm{err}_i|^2\\le\\eta_0 .\n \\label{eq:sweep-prescription-error}$$*\n\n\n\n*Define the nonnegative reserve $$\\begin{split}\n \\Pi_p:={}&\n T_0+c\\epsilon\\bigl(M_0^2+\\mathbb E_\\nu V_i^2+\\theta\\mathop{\\mathrm{Var}}_\\nu u\\bigr)\n       \\\\\n &+c\\frac r\\epsilon\\mathcal X\n       +c_*\\epsilon r^6\\mathbf 1_{\\{r\\ge C_*\\}},\n \\end{split}\n \\label{eq:sweep-positive-reserve}$$ where the final term is omitted in a sweep without boost. Then, pointwise, $$\\begin{aligned}\n Q:=\\frac{rN^2}{\\epsilon}\\bigl[\n &\\mathcal S(h_+,\\phi_+)-\\mathcal S(h_0,\\phi_0)\n       +\\epsilon e_{h_0}(F)-\\epsilon e_{h_+}(Y)\\bigr]\n \\notag\\\\\n &\\ge\\Pi_p-4\\theta^2m-\\frac r\\epsilon\\delta_0 .\n \\label{eq:sweep-inequality}\n\\end{aligned}$$ The numerical constant $c$ is independent of the instance, the number of colors, and the pass parameters. The errors $\\delta_0,\\eta_0$ can be prescribed independently before the pass.",
        "source_id": "PM-335.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-sweep",
          "exact_quote": "There is a numerical $c>0$ with the following property. For a sweep without boost, set $\\psi=0$. For a boosted sweep, given $\\epsilon_*>0$, there are constants $\\kappa>0$, $c_*>0$, and $C_*<\\infty$, depending only on $\\epsilon_*$, for which one may use $\\psi=\\kappa\\Psi$ whenever $\\epsilon\\ge\\epsilon_*$.*\n\n*Let $h_0,\\phi_0,F$ be a smooth equivariant Borel family on $\\widetilde M\\times\\Omega$, with bounds uniform in the lifted charts for the instance. Suppose $h_0$ is complete, its geometry and the positive-order derivatives of $\\phi_0$ have the uniform bounds above, and $$F=(F_i)_{i\\in\\mathcal L},\\qquad F_i\\ge0,\\qquad\n \\sum_iF_i^2=1$$ has finitely many free label orbits and uniformly locally finite compact coordinate supports. For each orbit representative, suppose all its transverse supports lie in a fixed compact subset of $\\widetilde M$. Let $h_{\\mathrm{ref}}\\le h_0$ be a complete equivariant Borel leafwise metric whose coefficients, inverse, and all coordinate derivatives have uniform bounds in the same lifted buffered charts as $h_0$. Suppose a Borel equivariant coloring $\\chi:\\mathcal L\\times\\Omega\\to\\{1,\\ldots,k\\}$, $k<\\infty$, has been chosen such that, for each retained $\\omega$ and distinct labels $i,j$ with $\\chi(i,\\omega)=\\chi(j,\\omega)$, the $R_{\\mathrm{loc}}$-neighborhoods in $h_{\\mathrm{ref}}(\\omega)$ of $\\mathop{\\mathrm{supp}}F_i(\\cdot,\\omega)$ and $\\mathop{\\mathrm{supp}}F_j(\\cdot,\\omega)$ are disjoint. Fix the pass parameters in <a href=\"#eq:sweep-parameters\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:sweep-parameters\">[eq:sweep-parameters]</a>, with $\\epsilon\\ge\\epsilon_*$ in the boosted case.*\n\n*For any $\\delta_0,\\eta_0>0$, there is a candidate sweep as above with smooth nonnegative bumps $f_i\\le2$, supported in the indicated enlargements, positive on the supports of $F_i$, and flat on their zero sets. Its output is given by <a href=\"#eq:sweep-output\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:sweep-output\">[eq:sweep-output]</a>. The lower bound for $r$ is uniform for the instance. The bumps, coefficients, all intermediate and final graph metrics and weights, their smooth graph quantities, and $Y$ are equivariant and Borel and retain the corresponding uniform chart bounds, with the positive-order convention for weights. The map $Y$ is again a root probability map with the same label orbits.*\n\n\n*For each $i$, the coefficient furnished by Lemma <a href=\"#lem:graph-localization\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:graph-localization\">7</a> has the form $$\\beta_i=\\beta_{s,i}+\\epsilon\\psi(R_i),\\qquad\n R_i=\\sum_{j<i}f_j^2 ,\n \\label{eq:sweep-beta}$$ Here $\\beta_{s,i}$ vanishes near $\\mathop{\\mathrm{supp}}F_i$, and the coefficients satisfy <a href=\"#eq:graph-spatial-cutoff\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:graph-spatial-cutoff\">[eq:graph-spatial-cutoff]</a> in $h_{i-1}$. Writing $$ND_i^0=\\epsilon a(F_i^2/f_i-f_i)+A_i^2/(2f_i)$$ on $\\{f_i>0\\}$, extended by zero off the bump, one has $$ND_i=ND_i^0-\\beta_i f_i+\\mathrm{err}_i,\\qquad\n \\sum_i|\\mathrm{err}_i|^2\\le\\eta_0 .\n \\label{eq:sweep-prescription-error}$$*\n\n\n\n*Define the nonnegative reserve $$\\begin{split}\n \\Pi_p:={}&\n T_0+c\\epsilon\\bigl(M_0^2+\\mathbb E_\\nu V_i^2+\\theta\\mathop{\\mathrm{Var}}_\\nu u\\bigr)\n       \\\\\n &+c\\frac r\\epsilon\\mathcal X\n       +c_*\\epsilon r^6\\mathbf 1_{\\{r\\ge C_*\\}},\n \\end{split}\n \\label{eq:sweep-positive-reserve}$$ where the final term is omitted in a sweep without boost. Then, pointwise, $$\\begin{aligned}\n Q:=\\frac{rN^2}{\\epsilon}\\bigl[\n &\\mathcal S(h_+,\\phi_+)-\\mathcal S(h_0,\\phi_0)\n       +\\epsilon e_{h_0}(F)-\\epsilon e_{h_+}(Y)\\bigr]\n \\notag\\\\\n &\\ge\\Pi_p-4\\theta^2m-\\frac r\\epsilon\\delta_0 .\n \\label{eq:sweep-inequality}\n\\end{aligned}$$ The numerical constant $c$ is independent of the instance, the number of colors, and the pass parameters. The errors $\\delta_0,\\eta_0$ can be prescribed independently before the pass.",
          "source_id": "PM-335.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "There is a numerical $c>0$ with the following property. For a sweep without boost, set $\\psi=0$. For a boosted sweep, given $\\epsilon_*>0$, there are constants $\\kappa>0$, $c_*>0$, and $C_*<\\infty$, depending only on $\\epsilon_*$, for which one may use $\\psi=\\kappa\\Psi$ whenever $\\epsilon\\ge\\epsilon_*$.*\n\n*Let $h_0,\\phi_0,F$ be a smooth equivariant Borel family on $\\widetilde M\\times\\Omega$, with bounds uniform in the lifted charts for the instance. Suppose $h_0$ is complete, its geometry and the positive-order derivatives of $\\phi_0$ have the uniform bounds above, and $$F=(F_i)_{i\\in\\mathcal L},\\qquad F_i\\ge0,\\qquad\n \\sum_iF_i^2=1$$ has finitely many free label orbits and uniformly locally finite compact coordinate supports. For each orbit representative, suppose all its transverse supports lie in a fixed compact subset of $\\widetilde M$. Let $h_{\\mathrm{ref}}\\le h_0$ be a complete equivariant Borel leafwise metric whose coefficients, inverse, and all coordinate derivatives have uniform bounds in the same lifted buffered charts as $h_0$. Suppose a Borel equivariant coloring $\\chi:\\mathcal L\\times\\Omega\\to\\{1,\\ldots,k\\}$, $k<\\infty$, has been chosen such that, for each retained $\\omega$ and distinct labels $i,j$ with $\\chi(i,\\omega)=\\chi(j,\\omega)$, the $R_{\\mathrm{loc}}$-neighborhoods in $h_{\\mathrm{ref}}(\\omega)$ of $\\mathop{\\mathrm{supp}}F_i(\\cdot,\\omega)$ and $\\mathop{\\mathrm{supp}}F_j(\\cdot,\\omega)$ are disjoint. Fix the pass parameters in <a href=\"#eq:sweep-parameters\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:sweep-parameters\">[eq:sweep-parameters]</a>, with $\\epsilon\\ge\\epsilon_*$ in the boosted case.*\n\n*For any $\\delta_0,\\eta_0>0$, there is a candidate sweep as above with smooth nonnegative bumps $f_i\\le2$, supported in the indicated enlargements, positive on the supports of $F_i$, and flat on their zero sets. Its output is given by <a href=\"#eq:sweep-output\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:sweep-output\">[eq:sweep-output]</a>. The lower bound for $r$ is uniform for the instance. The bumps, coefficients, all intermediate and final graph metrics and weights, their smooth graph quantities, and $Y$ are equivariant and Borel and retain the corresponding uniform chart bounds, with the positive-order convention for weights. The map $Y$ is again a root probability map with the same label orbits.*\n\n\n*For each $i$, the coefficient furnished by Lemma <a href=\"#lem:graph-localization\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:graph-localization\">7</a> has the form $$\\beta_i=\\beta_{s,i}+\\epsilon\\psi(R_i),\\qquad\n R_i=\\sum_{j<i}f_j^2 ,\n \\label{eq:sweep-beta}$$ Here $\\beta_{s,i}$ vanishes near $\\mathop{\\mathrm{supp}}F_i$, and the coefficients satisfy <a href=\"#eq:graph-spatial-cutoff\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:graph-spatial-cutoff\">[eq:graph-spatial-cutoff]</a> in $h_{i-1}$. Writing $$ND_i^0=\\epsilon a(F_i^2/f_i-f_i)+A_i^2/(2f_i)$$ on $\\{f_i>0\\}$, extended by zero off the bump, one has $$ND_i=ND_i^0-\\beta_i f_i+\\mathrm{err}_i,\\qquad\n \\sum_i|\\mathrm{err}_i|^2\\le\\eta_0 .\n \\label{eq:sweep-prescription-error}$$*\n\n\n\n*Define the nonnegative reserve $$\\begin{split}\n \\Pi_p:={}&\n T_0+c\\epsilon\\bigl(M_0^2+\\mathbb E_\\nu V_i^2+\\theta\\mathop{\\mathrm{Var}}_\\nu u\\bigr)\n       \\\\\n &+c\\frac r\\epsilon\\mathcal X\n       +c_*\\epsilon r^6\\mathbf 1_{\\{r\\ge C_*\\}},\n \\end{split}\n \\label{eq:sweep-positive-reserve}$$ where the final term is omitted in a sweep without boost. Then, pointwise, $$\\begin{aligned}\n Q:=\\frac{rN^2}{\\epsilon}\\bigl[\n &\\mathcal S(h_+,\\phi_+)-\\mathcal S(h_0,\\phi_0)\n       +\\epsilon e_{h_0}(F)-\\epsilon e_{h_+}(Y)\\bigr]\n \\notag\\\\\n &\\ge\\Pi_p-4\\theta^2m-\\frac r\\epsilon\\delta_0 .\n \\label{eq:sweep-inequality}\n\\end{aligned}$$ The numerical constant $c$ is independent of the instance, the number of colors, and the pass parameters. The errors $\\delta_0,\\eta_0$ can be prescribed independently before the pass.",
      "explanation": "An ordered sweep applies localized graphs to probability coordinates separated by colors. Normalization produces a new root probability map, and the weighted scalar and energy balance retains a positive reserve with independently prescribed analytic errors.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-thm-entropy",
        "exact_quote": "Let $(M^n,g)$ be a closed connected Riemannian manifold, where $n\\geq3$ and $\\mathop{\\mathrm{Scal}}_g=-1$, and put $\\Gamma=\\pi_1(M)$. Let $(\\Omega_0,\\mu_0)$ be a standard Borel probability space with a measure-preserving $\\Gamma$-action; the one-point space is allowed. Suppose $\\mathcal L_0$ is a countable set with finitely many free $\\Gamma$-orbits and $$F^{\\mathrm{in}}:\\widetilde M\\times\\Omega_0\\longrightarrow\n       \\ell^2(\\mathcal L_0),\\qquad\n F^{\\mathrm{in}}_\\ell\\geq0,\\qquad\n \\sum_{\\ell\\in\\mathcal L_0}(F^{\\mathrm{in}}_\\ell)^2=1$$ is equivariant, Borel in the transverse variable, and smooth along $\\widetilde M$. Assume the following uniformity for this input. In charts lifted from a fixed finite atlas on $M$, with fixed inner buffers, the supports are locally finite with a uniform bound on their number, and all leafwise derivatives of the coordinate functions have uniform bounds. For each orbit representative $\\ell$ there is a compact set $K_\\ell\\subset\\widetilde M$ such that $\\mathop{\\mathrm{supp}}F^{\\mathrm{in}}_{\\gamma\\ell}(\\,\\cdot\\,,\\omega)\n\\subset\\gamma K_\\ell$ for all $\\gamma\\in\\Gamma$ and almost every $\\omega$. The bounds and compact sets in these assumptions may depend on the input.*\n\n\n*For every prescribed elementary function $\\mathcal B$ and every finite number $E_{\\min}$, there are a standard Borel probability $\\Gamma$-space $(\\Omega,\\mu)$ extending $(\\Omega_0,\\mu_0)$, a countable set $\\mathcal L$ with finitely many free $\\Gamma$-orbits, and equivariant leafwise data on $\\widetilde M\\times\\Omega$: a metric $h$, a function $\\phi$, a positive density $\\rho$, and a root probability map $F$ with labels $\\mathcal L$. There are also $E>E_{\\min}$, an integer $q\\geq n+1$, and a constant $C=C(n,\\mathcal B)<\\infty$ such that, on an invariant conull set of transverse parameters, $$\\label{eq:entropy-contract}\n\\begin{split}\n &\\rho\\leq dV_g,\\qquad\n W:=\\log\\frac{dV_h}{\\rho}\\geq0,\\\\\n &\\mathcal S(h,\\phi)\\geq\n       -C+2\\left(W-\\frac n2\\log E\\right)+E e_h(F),\n       \\qquad e_h(F)^{1/2}\\leq E^{-1/100},\\\\\n &E>\\mathcal B(q).\n\\end{split}$$ Here the extension means that there is an equivariant measure-preserving map $\\Omega\\to\\Omega_0$. All output objects are Borel transversely and smooth along leaves. In the same lifted charts, the coefficients of $h$, their derivatives of every order, and the coefficients of $h^{-1}$ have uniform bounds; the function $\\phi$, the density ratio $\\rho/dV_g$, its reciprocal, and their derivatives of every order have uniform bounds. The coordinates of $F$ have the same derivative and uniform local finiteness properties as the input. There are equivariant compact sets containing their supports, uniformly in the transverse parameter. All these output bounds may depend on the input and on the chosen finite construction.*\n\n*There is, in addition, a fixed $\\Gamma$-invariant graph $\\mathcal G$ on $\\mathcal L$, independent of the transverse parameter, of finite maximum degree $\\Delta$. It contains an edge between every pair of distinct labels that are simultaneously nonzero at any point of any leaf. We may take $$q\\geq \\max\\{n+1,\\#(\\Gamma\\backslash\\mathcal L),\\Delta+1\\}$$ and a Borel equivariant coloring $\\chi:\\mathcal L\\times\\Omega\\to\\{1,\\ldots,q\\}$ such that labels of graph distance one or two have different colors. The set $\\mathcal L$ can be taken to consist of finitely many copies of $\\mathcal L_0$. The constant $C$ is independent of the manifold, metric, input family, all the uniform bounds just specified, and $E_{\\min}$.",
        "source_id": "PM-335.1"
      },

        "anchor": "eq:entropy-bootstrap-output",
        "exact_quote": "There are constants $\\epsilon_*>0$ and $C_{\\mathrm{boot}}<4$, depending only on $n$, with the following property. The number of sweeps may depend on the input.",

      "exact_statement": "There are constants $\\epsilon_*>0$ and $C_{\\mathrm{boot}}<4$, depending only on $n$, with the following property. Starting with the input of Theorem <a href=\"#thm:entropy\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"thm:entropy\">9</a>, finitely many sweeps without boost, followed individually by conformal changes, produce a metric $h_*$, a root probability map $F_*$, and $\\epsilon_0\\in[\\epsilon_*,2\\epsilon_*]$ such that $$\\label{eq:entropy-bootstrap-output}\n \\rho:=dV_{h_*}\\leq dV_g,\\qquad\n \\mathop{\\mathrm{Scal}}_{h_*}\\geq-C_{\\mathrm{boot}}+\\epsilon_0e_{h_*}(F_*).$$ The metric and map have the uniformity and support properties in Theorem <a href=\"#thm:entropy\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"thm:entropy\">9</a>.       "explanation": "A finite input-dependent sequence of unboosted sweeps and conformal resets raises the energy coefficient to a dimensional positive range while contracting the volume density. Its scalar cost remains dimensional.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "eq:entropy-increment",
        "exact_quote": "There are dimensional constants $m_0>0$, $D_*\\geq1$, $l_0>0$, $\\gamma_n>0$, and $C_n<\\infty$ with the following property. For $\\epsilon\\geq\\epsilon_*$, take a boosted sweep with $$0<l\\leq l_0,\\qquad \\theta=D_*l\\leq\\tfrac1{16},\n \\qquad N^2=l\\epsilon.$$ The errors can be chosen so that $$\\label{eq:entropy-increment}\n\\begin{split}\n \\mathcal S(h_+,\\phi_+)-(1+l)\\epsilon e_{h_+}(Y)\n \\geq{}&\\mathcal S(h_0,\\phi_0)-\\epsilon e_{h_0}(F)\\\\\n &+2W_p-n\\log(1+l)-C_nl^2+P_p ,\n\\end{split}$$ where the nonnegative, pointwise surplus is $$\\label{eq:entropy-surplus-definition}\n P_p=\\gamma_n\\frac{\\epsilon}{rN^2}\\Pi_p\\,\n \\mathbf 1_{\\{m\\geq m_0\\}\\,\\cup\\,\\{|1-r|\\geq1/4\\}}.$$ In particular, on the indicated region $P_p$ retains a fixed fraction of every positive term in the sweep inequality.",
        "source_id": "PM-335.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-entropy-increment",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-entropy-surplus",
        "exact_quote": "For every sweep of Lemma <a href=\"#lem:entropy-increment\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:entropy-increment\">11</a>, $$\\label{eq:entropy-pass-volume}\n W_p\\leq C_n\\epsilon^{1/4}(\\epsilon+P_p)^{3/4}.$$ There is also a dimensional $\\epsilon_{\\mathrm{exit}}$ such that, if $\\epsilon\\geq\\epsilon_{\\mathrm{exit}}$ and $r\\leq1/4$, then $$\\label{eq:entropy-last-surplus}\n\\begin{split}\n P_p&\\geq c_n\\frac{\\epsilon}{lr}\n                       \\left(1+\\frac{m^2}{\\epsilon^2}\\right),\\\\\n \\sum_i\\bigl(D_i^2+|\\mathrm{II}_i|^2+|d\\log L_i|_{h_i}^2\\bigr)\n       &\\leq C_n\\frac{P_p}{\\theta}.\n\\end{split}$$ The errors in the prescription may simultaneously be chosen to have $\\sum_i|\\mathrm{err}_i|^2\\leq1$.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-entropy-accumulation",
        "exact_quote": "Suppose at parameter $\\epsilon_{\\mathrm{in}}\\geq\\epsilon_*$ that $$\\mathcal S(h,\\phi)\\geq-C+2W-n\\log\\epsilon_{\\mathrm{in}}\n                  +\\epsilon_{\\mathrm{in}}e_h(F)+P_{\\mathrm{in}},\n \\qquad W=\\log(dV_h/\\rho)\\geq0,\\quad P_{\\mathrm{in}}\\geq0.$$ Successive sweeps of Lemma <a href=\"#lem:entropy-increment\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:entropy-increment\">11</a>, with $\\epsilon_{\\mathrm{new}}=(1+l(\\epsilon))\\epsilon$, preserve this form of inequality: at the final parameter $E$ the surplus is $P=P_{\\mathrm{in}}+\\sum_pP_p$, the volume logarithm is $W_{\\mathrm{new}}=W+\\sum_pW_p$, and the increase of the scalar constant is $C_n\\sum_p l(\\epsilon_p)^2$. Along the entire increasing sequence of parameters, $$\\label{eq:entropy-summability}\n \\sum_p l(\\epsilon_p)^2\\leq C_n,\\qquad\n \\#\\{p:\\epsilon_p<E\\}\\leq C_n(1+\\log^3(1+E)).$$ If the initial volume logarithm is zero, then, for all sufficiently large dimensional $E$, $$\\label{eq:entropy-phase-volume}\n W_{\\mathrm{new}}\\leq E^{0.26}(E+P)^{0.75}.$$ The same assertion holds if the initial data have parameter $E_0$, surplus $P_0$, and $$W\\leq E_0^{0.32}(E_0+P_0)^{0.75},$$ provided $E\\geq E_0$ is sufficiently large dimensionally and $E_0^{0.32}\\leq\\tfrac12E^{0.26}$.",
        "source_id": "PM-335.1"
      },

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-entropy-phase-exit",
        "exact_quote": "Consider a phase starting with root map $Y^0$ and metric $h^0$, and let $K_0$ be a finite uniform bound for $e_{h^0}(Y^0)^{1/2}$. Suppose its last sweep has input parameter $\\epsilon$, output parameter $E=(1+l(\\epsilon))\\epsilon$, and output $(h_+,\\phi_+,Y)$. Suppose, with $C\\geq0$ and $P\\geq P_p$ including the surplus from that last sweep, that $$\\label{eq:entropy-before-exit}\n\\begin{split}\n \\mathcal S(h_+,\\phi_+)&\\geq-C+2W-n\\log E+E e_{h_+}(Y)+P,\\\\\n W&=\\log(dV_{h_+}/\\rho)\\geq0,\\qquad\n W\\leq E^{0.26}(E+P)^{0.75}.\n\\end{split}$$ If $E$ is sufficiently large in terms of $n,K_0$, there are a metric $\\widehat h$, a weight $\\widehat\\phi$, and a root map $Z$ on two copies of the labels such that $$\\label{eq:entropy-after-exit}\n\\begin{split}\n \\mathcal S(\\widehat h,\\widehat\\phi)\n &\\geq-(C+C_n)+2\\widehat W-n\\log E\n                      +E e_{\\widehat h}(Z)+P',\\\\\n \\widehat W&=\\log(dV_{\\widehat h}/\\rho)\\geq0,\\qquad\n \\widehat W\\leq E^{0.32}(E+P')^{0.75},\\\\\n e_{\\widehat h}(Z)^{1/2}&\\leq E^{-1/100}.\n\\end{split}$$ Here $P'\\geq0$, and the added scalar cost $C_n$ is independent of $K_0$ and of all other instance constants. The changes preserve the uniform smoothness and support properties of Theorem <a href=\"#thm:entropy\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"thm:entropy\">9</a>.",
        "source_id": "PM-335.1"
      },
      "evidence": [

The changes preserve the uniform smoothness and support properties of Theorem <a href=\"#thm:entropy\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"thm:entropy\">9</a>.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "eq:degree-estimate",
        "exact_quote": "There are absolute constants $C_0,C_1>0$ with the following property. Let $q\\ge2$, let $d$ be an integer with $0\\le d\\le q$, and let $\\tau>0$. For $0\\le r\\le d$ set $$\\label{eq:degree-widths}\n T_r=2\\tau+\\frac{r\\tau}{q},\\qquad\n b_r=\\tau\\left(1+\\frac1{2q}+\\frac r{4q^2}\\right).$$ Suppose $(X,h,\\phi,G)$ is a controlled measured $d$-band over $Q_{T_d}^d$ in the sense of Definition <a href=\"#def:degree-band\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"def:degree-band\">15</a>. Let $B\\ge1$ and $L_1,\\ldots,L_d>0$. Suppose throughout $X$ that $$\\label{eq:degree-horizon-bound}\n             L_i|dG_i|_h\\le B-\\frac dq\\qquad(1\\le i\\le d),$$ and suppose that $$\\label{eq:degree-horizon}\n       \\frac{C_0(1+B)^{2/3}}{L_i}<\\frac{\\tau}{10q}\n                       \\qquad(1\\le i\\le d).$$ Define $H_i(G)=2(L_i|dG_i|_h-1)_+$. Then $$\\label{eq:degree-estimate}\n |\\mathcal D_X(G)|(2\\tau)^d\n \\le \\frac{\\exp(C_1d/q)}{\\prod_{i=1}^dL_i}\n     \\int_{\\{|G|_\\infty<b_d\\}}\n       \\exp\\left(\\frac{-\\mathcal S(h,\\phi)+\\sum_{i=1}^dH_i(G)}2\\right)dV_h.$$ For $d=0$ the product is one, the sum is zero, and $\\mathcal S(h,\\phi)=0$; the assertion is the bound of a signed count by the unsigned count.",
        "source_id": "PM-335.1"
      },

          "exact_quote": "There are absolute constants $C_0,C_1>0$ with the following property. Let $q\\ge2$, let $d$ be an integer with $0\\le d\\le q$, and let $\\tau>0$. For $0\\le r\\le d$ set $$\\label{eq:degree-widths}\n T_r=2\\tau+\\frac{r\\tau}{q},\\qquad\n b_r=\\tau\\left(1+\\frac1{2q}+\\frac r{4q^2}\\right).$$ Suppose $(X,h,\\phi,G)$ is a controlled measured $d$-band over $Q_{T_d}^d$ in the sense of Definition <a href=\"#def:degree-band\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"def:degree-band\">15</a>. Let $B\\ge1$ and $L_1,\\ldots,L_d>0$. Suppose throughout $X$ that $$\\label{eq:degree-horizon-bound}\n             L_i|dG_i|_h\\le B-\\frac dq\\qquad(1\\le i\\le d),$$ and suppose that $$\\label{eq:degree-horizon}\n       \\frac{C_0(1+B)^{2/3}}{L_i}<\\frac{\\tau}{10q}\n                       \\qquad(1\\le i\\le d).$$ Define $H_i(G)=2(L_i|dG_i|_h-1)_+$. Then $$\\label{eq:degree-estimate}\n |\\mathcal D_X(G)|(2\\tau)^d\n \\le \\frac{\\exp(C_1d/q)}{\\prod_{i=1}^dL_i}\n     \\int_{\\{|G|_\\infty<b_d\\}}\n       \\exp\\left(\\frac{-\\mathcal S(h,\\phi)+\\sum_{i=1}^dH_i(G)}2\\right)dV_h.$$ For $d=0$ the product is one, the sum is zero, and $\\mathcal S(h,\\phi)=0$; the assertion is the bound of a signed count by the unsigned count.",

      "exact_statement": "There are absolute constants $C_0,C_1>0$ with the following property. Let $q\\ge2$, let $d$ be an integer with $0\\le d\\le q$, and let $\\tau>0$. For $0\\le r\\le d$ set $$\\label{eq:degree-widths}\n T_r=2\\tau+\\frac{r\\tau}{q},\\qquad\n b_r=\\tau\\left(1+\\frac1{2q}+\\frac r{4q^2}\\right).$$ Suppose $(X,h,\\phi,G)$ is a controlled measured $d$-band over $Q_{T_d}^d$ in the sense of Definition <a href=\"#def:degree-band\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"def:degree-band\">15</a>. Let $B\\ge1$ and $L_1,\\ldots,L_d>0$. Suppose throughout $X$ that $$\\label{eq:degree-horizon-bound}\n             L_i|dG_i|_h\\le B-\\frac dq\\qquad(1\\le i\\le d),$$ and suppose that $$\\label{eq:degree-horizon}\n       \\frac{C_0(1+B)^{2/3}}{L_i}<\\frac{\\tau}{10q}\n                       \\qquad(1\\le i\\le d).$$ Define $H_i(G)=2(L_i|dG_i|_h-1)_+$. Then $$\\label{eq:degree-estimate}\n |\\mathcal D_X(G)|(2\\tau)^d\n \\le \\frac{\\exp(C_1d/q)}{\\prod_{i=1}^dL_i}\n     \\int_{\\{|G|_\\infty<b_d\\}}\n       \\exp\\left(\\frac{-\\mathcal S(h,\\phi)+\\sum_{i=1}^dH_i(G)}2\\right)dV_h.$$ For $d=0$ the product is one, the sum is zero, and $\\mathcal S(h,\\phi)=0$; the assertion is the bound of a signed count by the unsigned count.",

The scalar and volume factors cancel during dimension descent.",
