          "anchor": "stmt-thm-degree",
          "exact_quote": "Suppose $(X,h,\\phi,G)$ is a controlled measured $d$-band over $Q_{T_d}^d$ in the sense of Definition <a href=\"#def:degree-band\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"def:degree-band\">15</a>. Let $B\\ge1$ and $L_1,\\ldots,L_d>0$. Suppose throughout $X$ that $$\\label{eq:degree-horizon-bound}\n             L_i|dG_i|_h\\le B-\\frac dq\\qquad(1\\le i\\le d),$$ and suppose that $$\\label{eq:degree-horizon}\n       \\frac{C_0(1+B)^{2/3}}{L_i}<\\frac{\\tau}{10q}\n                       \\qquad(1\\le i\\le d).$$ Define $H_i(G)=2(L_i|dG_i|_h-1)_+$. Then $$\\label{eq:degree-estimate}\n |\\mathcal D_X(G)|(2\\tau)^d\n \\le \\frac{\\exp(C_1d/q)}{\\prod_{i=1}^dL_i}\n     \\int_{\\{|G|_\\infty<b_d\\}}\n       \\exp\\left(\\frac{-\\mathcal S(h,\\phi)+\\sum_{i=1}^dH_i(G)}2\\right)dV_h.$$ For $d=0$ the product is one, the sum is zero, and $\\mathcal S(h,\\phi)=0$; the assertion is the bound of a signed count by the unsigned count.*\n\n</div>\n\nThe proof removes one coordinate at a time. For $r\\ge1$, the gaps $T_r-T_{r-1}=\\tau/q$ and $b_r-b_{r-1}=\\tau/(4q^2)$ supply, respectively, chart buffers and room for the residual value error when locating the integration core, while the permitted scaled gradient of each retained coordinate increases by $1/q$. A family of boundaries ordered by a real parameter supplies the factor $2\\tau$ on the left. Its normal speed supplies the coarea factor on the right. In dimensions where the boundaries can be singular we will construct a regular band of the same degree; a scalar gain will pay exactly for the volume of the graphs used to construct it. The following formulation separates that construction from the final induction.\n\n\n<div id=\"lem:degree-descent\" class=\"lemma\">\n\n**Lemma 17** (One step of measured descent). *Assume the hypotheses of Theorem <a href=\"#thm:degree\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"thm:degree\">16</a> with $d\\ge1$, put $p=d-1$, and put $$\\Theta=\\exp\\left(\\frac{-\\mathcal S(h,\\phi)+\\sum_{i=1}^dH_i(G)}2\\right).$$ There is a Borel family of oriented regular hypersurface portions $\\Sigma_s$ in the leaves, $-\\tau<s<\\tau$, and, for almost every $s$, a positive function $v_s$ on $\\Sigma_s$, smooth along it and Borel in its charts, with the following properties. Transverse null subsets and their saturations may be removed for each such $s$.*\n\n\n1.  *For every nonnegative Borel function $F$ supported in $\\{|G|_\\infty<b_d\\}$, $$\\label{eq:degree-coarea}\n     \\int_{-\\tau}^{\\tau}\\!ds\\int_{\\Sigma_s}v_sF\\,dA_h\n                           \\le\\int_XF\\,dV_h.$$*\n\n\n2.  *For each such $s$ there is a controlled measured $p$-band $(X_s^+,h_s^+,\\phi_s^+,J_s^+)$ over $Q_{T_p}^p$ of degree $\\mathcal D_X(G)$. Its coordinates are indexed by $j=2,\\ldots,d$ and satisfy $$L_j|dJ_{s,j}^+|_{h_s^+}\\le B-\\frac pq.$$ With $H_j^+=2(L_j|dJ_{s,j}^+|_{h_s^+}-1)_+$, there is an absolute constant $C_*>0$ such that $$\\label{eq:degree-child-integral}\n     \\begin{split}\n     &\\int_{\\{|J_s^+|_\\infty<b_p\\}}\n            \\exp\\left(\\frac{-\\mathcal S(h_s^+,\\phi_s^+)+\\sum_{j=2}^dH_j^+}{2}\\right)\n            dV_{h_s^+}\\\\\n     &\\hspace{25mm}\\le \\frac{e^{C_*/q}}{L_1}\n           \\int_{\\Sigma_s\\cap\\{|G|_\\infty<b_d\\}}v_s\\Theta\\,dA_h.\n     \\end{split}$$ In dimension $p=0$ the left side is the measured unsigned count.*\n\n*The child in the second assertion may be chosen separately for each typical $s$; no measurability of those choices in $s$ is asserted or needed.*\n\n</div>\n\n\n#### An ordered family of boundaries\n\nWe prove Lemma <a href=\"#lem:degree-descent\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:degree-descent\">17</a> in this and the next two subsections. Fix intermediate widths $$\\label{eq:degree-intermediate-widths}\n              T_p<t_1<t_2<t_3<t_4<T_d.$$ Whenever a chart buffer is needed, choose further intermediate widths between these. The width $t_4$ controls confinement and the parent charts, $t_3$ contains the boundary portions to be used, $t_2$ will bound an auxiliary complete region, and $t_1$ will bound the child. Such choices can depend on the given band.\n\n\n\nChoose once and for all a smooth even function $\\gamma$ with $0\\le\\gamma(y)\\le y^2$ and with $\\gamma(y)$ bounded above and below by positive absolute multiples of $|y|^{3/2}$ for $|y|\\ge1$. Let $P_0$ be the solution about zero of $$\\label{eq:degree-pressure}\n P_0(0)=0,\\qquad P_0'=L_1\\psi(P_0),\\qquad\n       \\psi(y)=1+\\frac{\\gamma(y)}{8(1+B)}.$$ It is odd and increasing and has poles at $\\pm b$, where $$b=\\frac1{L_1}\\int_0^\\infty\\frac{dy}{\\psi(y)}\n       \\le\\frac{C(1+B)^{2/3}}{L_1}.$$ For the inequality, split the integral at $(1+B)^{2/3}$ and use the $|y|^{3/2}$ bound on the tail. Choose the absolute $C_0$ in <a href=\"#eq:degree-horizon\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:degree-horizon\">[eq:degree-horizon]</a> at least this $C$. Thus $$\\label{eq:degree-pole-width}\n                         b<\\frac{\\tau}{10q}.$$\n\n\n###### The local slice calculation.\n\n\nWe first record the analytic purpose of the pressure law. Let $\\Sigma$ be a smooth cooriented local hypersurface, with unit normal $\\nu$, and set $$\\begin{gathered}\n D=H+\\partial_\\nu\\phi,\\qquad\n H=\\operatorname{tr}\\mathrm{II},\\qquad\n \\mathrm{II}(Y,Z)=\\langle\\nabla_Y\\nu,Z\\rangle,\\\\\n \\mathcal P=|\\mathrm{II}|^2+\n             (\\operatorname{Ric}_h-\\nabla^2\\phi)(\\nu,\\nu).\n \\end{gathered}$$ Put $\\mathfrak a=L_1\\psi(D)>0$. When $D=P_0(s-G_1)$, the derivatives of the raw pressure are $\\partial_sP_0(s-G_1)=\\mathfrak a$ and $d_xP_0(s-G_1)=-\\mathfrak a\\,dG_1$. The following calculation uses only a positive smooth function $v$ satisfying the local equation $$\\label{eq:degree-local-lapse}\n -\\bigl(\\Delta_{\\Sigma,\\phi}+\\mathcal P\n                 -\\mathfrak a\\,dG_1(\\nu)\\bigr)v=\\mathfrak a.$$ Here $\\Delta_{\\Sigma,\\phi}$ is weighted by $e^{\\phi|_\\Sigma}dA_h$, and slice norms use the induced metric. The ordered construction below will produce this equation on almost every regular slice.\n\n\nPut $\\phi_\\Sigma=\\phi|_\\Sigma+\\ln v$ and $S_\\Sigma=\\mathcal S(h|_{T\\Sigma},\\phi_\\Sigma)$. Gauss’ equation and the normal/tangential decomposition of derivatives of $\\phi$ give $$\\mathcal S(h|_{T\\Sigma},\\phi|_\\Sigma)\n       =\\mathcal S(h,\\phi)+D^2-2\\mathcal P+|\\mathrm{II}|^2.$$ Adding $\\ln v$ to the weight changes this by $-2\\Delta_{\\Sigma,\\phi}v/v+|d\\ln v|^2$. Substitution of <a href=\"#eq:degree-local-lapse\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:degree-local-lapse\">[eq:degree-local-lapse]</a> yields the exact identity $$\\label{eq:degree-slice-identity}\n S_\\Sigma=\\mathcal S(h,\\phi)+D^2-2\\mathfrak a\\,dG_1(\\nu)\n                 +|\\mathrm{II}|^2+\\frac{2\\mathfrak a}{v}+|d\\ln v|^2.$$ This is also the hypersurface calculation behind Lemma <a href=\"#lem:graph-identity\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:graph-identity\">5</a>; it is valid for $p=0$ with left side zero.\n\n\n\nSet $t=L_1|dG_1|_h\\le B$ and $x=v/L_1$. Since $\\gamma(D)\\le D^2$, $$-2\\mathfrak a\\,dG_1(\\nu)\n       \\ge-2t\\left(1+\\frac{\\gamma(D)}{8(1+B)}\\right),\n \\qquad \\frac{t\\gamma(D)}{4(1+B)}\\le\\frac{D^2}{4}.$$ We may discard the remaining nonnegative part of $D^2$, and use $2\\mathfrak a/v\\ge2/x$. Finally, $t\\le1+(t-1)_+$ and $x^{-1}+\\ln x\\ge1$ for $x>0$. Thus <a href=\"#eq:degree-slice-identity\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:degree-slice-identity\">[eq:degree-slice-identity]</a> implies $$\\label{eq:degree-slice-bound}\n S_\\Sigma\\ge\\mathcal S(h,\\phi)-H_1(G)-2\\ln(v/L_1)+|d\\ln v|^2.$$ The square term will be used later when the weight is changed near singular points. For $j=2,\\ldots,d$, put $H_j^\\Sigma=2(L_j|d_\\Sigma G_j|-1)_+$. Tangential restriction gives $H_j^\\Sigma\\le H_j(G)$, so the full pointwise density comparison is $$\\label{eq:degree-raw-integrand}\n \\exp\\left(\\frac{-S_\\Sigma+\\sum_{j=2}^dH_j^\\Sigma}{2}\\right)\n \\le\\frac v{L_1}e^{-|d\\ln v|^2/2}\\Theta\n \\le\\frac v{L_1}\\Theta.$$ For $p=0$ the sum and slice gradient are zero, and the left side is one. The remaining work supplies the slices and speeds, and verifies the controlled-band and degree conditions needed to use this comparison.\n\nFor a small $\\eta>0$, prolong $P_0$ smoothly and strictly increasingly past the points $\\pm(b-\\eta)$, keeping it unchanged on $[-b+\\eta,b-\\eta]$. Denote the resulting smooth function by $\\widehat P$ and set $$\\mathfrak h_s(x)=\\widehat P(s-G_1(x)),\\qquad -\\tau<s<\\tau.$$ We will choose $\\eta$ sufficiently small for the given band and then keep it fixed. On every boundary portion used below, $\\widehat P$ will agree with $P_0$ and hence obey <a href=\"#eq:degree-pressure\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:degree-pressure\">[eq:degree-pressure]</a>.\n\n\n###### Least minimizers, including on noncompact leaves.\n\n\nWrite $P_\\phi(E;U)=\\int_{\\partial^*E\\cap U}e^\\phi\\,dA_h$ for the weighted perimeter of a set of locally finite perimeter. We use local differences of the functional $$\\label{eq:degree-functional}\n                  P_\\phi(E)-\\int_E\\mathfrak h_s e^\\phi\\,dV_h.$$ This is a weighted $\\mu$-bubble functional. For soap bubbles in Gromov’s terminology, see [[Gromov1996](#ref-Gromov1996), Section 556]; for the weighted functional and its local first- and second-variation formulas, see also [[ChodoshLi2024](#ref-ChodoshLi2024), Section 3, Equation (1), and Lemmas 13–14]. Neither term is required to be finite on the whole leaf. In particular, this construction uses no global weighted-volume bound.\n\nThe output will be a canonical nested family $E_s$ on each open leaf, independent of the exhaustion and jointly Borel in the plaque data and $s$. Each $E_s$ will minimize these local differences for compact variations. The local comparison argument also supplies the perimeter compactness needed later to turn convergence of sets into graphical convergence at regular limit points. We establish these properties in turn, before confining the boundaries to the raw-pressure region.\n\n*Compact domains and leastness.* For a compact subset $K$ of a leaf, minimize <a href=\"#eq:degree-functional\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:degree-functional\">[eq:degree-functional]</a> over sets which vanish off $K$, using their full perimeter in the leaf. The empty set is a competitor. On a neighborhood of $K$ the density is smooth and positive and the forcing is bounded. A bound on the functional therefore gives a perimeter bound, and BV compactness and lower semicontinuity give a minimizer; see, for example, [[Maggi2012](#ref-Maggi2012), Theorem 12.26] for the compactness theorem. Among the minimizers choose one of minimum ordinary volume. The weighted perimeter satisfies $$P_\\phi(E\\cap F)+P_\\phi(E\\cup F)\\le P_\\phi(E)+P_\\phi(F),$$ and the volume term for a fixed forcing is additive under union and intersection. Thus the intersection of any two minimizers is again a minimizer. Minimum volume forces the selected minimizer to be contained, up to null sets, in every other one. It is consequently unique as an $L^1$ set; call it the least minimizer in $K$.\n\n*Order and exhaustion.* The same inequality proves both monotonicities we need. If $K\\subset K'$ and two forcings satisfy $\\mathfrak h\\le\\mathfrak h'$, a minimizer for $(K,\\mathfrak h)$ and one for $(K',\\mathfrak h')$ obey $$\\mathcal F_{\\mathfrak h}(E\\cap F)+\\mathcal F_{\\mathfrak h'}(E\\cup F)\n              \\le \\mathcal F_{\\mathfrak h}(E)+\\mathcal F_{\\mathfrak h'}(F),$$ where $\\mathcal F$ denotes <a href=\"#eq:degree-functional\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:degree-functional\">[eq:degree-functional]</a>; the inequality for the volume terms follows on $E\\setminus F$ from $\\mathfrak h\\le\\mathfrak h'$. Minimality in the two domains makes both inequalities equalities, and leastness in $K$ implies $E\\subset F$. Exhaust a leaf by increasing finite unions of compact coordinate subboxes whose interiors exhaust it. The least minimizers increase with this exhaustion. Their union, interpreted in $L^1_{\\mathrm{loc}}$, is independent of the exhaustion, since each compact subset of either exhaustion is eventually contained in one of the other. This defines $E_s$, and the forcing monotonicity makes $E_s\\subset E_t$ for $s<t$.\n\n*Local comparison.* The limit must still minimize for compact variations, since all later regularity and compactness arguments use that property. Ball replacement in a relatively compact chart gives uniform local perimeter bounds for the exhaustion minimizers: choose a radius with controlled slicing area, replace the set inside that ball by the full or empty ball, and bound the forcing term by its local supremum times the ball volume. Thus the increasing sets converge locally in BV weakly and in $L^1$. To pass a comparison to the limit, surround the compact region to be changed by a thin outer belt on which the proposed competitor agrees with $E_s$. Choose a Lipschitz coordinate level function across the belt. Coarea bounds the integral, over its levels, of the trace mismatch between an exhaustion minimizer and $E_s$ by their $L^1$ mismatch in the belt. One may therefore splice the proposed competitor inside a level to the exhaustion minimizer outside it so that the extra trace area tends to zero. The levels can also be chosen with zero limiting perimeter on their boundaries, since this excludes only a null set of levels. Minimality before passage to the limit, convergence of the bounded forcing integrals, and lower semicontinuity give the compact comparison for $E_s$. The same argument applies under smooth local convergence of the metric, density, and forcing coefficients. Below we apply it with the unchanged limit as competitor to prove convergence of the local perimeter measures. Multiplying a density by a positive constant has no effect on any of these comparisons.\n\n*Borel dependence.* We explain why all these choices are Borel, including jointly in $s$. For this coding, subdivide the original atlas into bounded coordinate boxes with compact closure in the older boxes, and affinely normalize each to one fixed cube $Q=(-1,1)^d$. Fix once a countable list $(R_\\ell)_{\\ell\\ge1}$ of all open rational boxes with closure in $Q$. Starting at a plaque, enumerate its accessible normalized plaque occurrences Borel measurably in slots $j\\ge1$, using dummy slots when necessary. Use the fixed Polish space $$\\mathcal Z=\\prod_{j,\\ell\\ge1}L^1(R_\\ell),$$ with Lebesgue $L^1$ norms and the product topology. A set is coded by its indicator on $R_\\ell$ in occurrence $j$; dummy entries are zero. Compatibility is tested on compact exhaustions of all overlap domains, including repeated occurrences. The closures of the first finitely many accessible $R_\\ell$ in a fixed enumeration of the pairs $(j,\\ell)$ give the compact exhaustion domains. The conditions that the code is an indicator, agrees on overlaps, and vanishes off the specified domain are Borel, and for fixed plaque data are closed in $\\mathcal Z$.\n\nIntegrals against chartwise Borel smooth densities are Borel by Fubini. Perimeter is Borel as well: its supremum over divergence integrals may be taken over fixed countable families of rational smooth test fields in the $R_\\ell$, and finite sums of them. Partitions of unity in a leaf show density among all compactly supported tests. Norm constraints are checked on countably many dense coordinate points; overlap maps and a partition by the first covering chart make the integrals Borel.\n\nFix one plaque-data fiber and one compact exhaustion domain $K$. A functional sublevel for sets supported in $K$ bounds their weighted perimeter, since the forcing integral is uniformly bounded on this fixed compact set. In every active $R_\\ell$ the metric and density have a positive lower bound on its closure, so this gives a Euclidean BV bound there; the indicator’s $L^1(R_\\ell)$ norm is bounded by $|R_\\ell|$. Diagonal BV compactness gives convergence in $\\mathcal Z$ along a subsequence. The fiberwise closed indicator, overlap, and support conditions persist. The volume term is continuous, since $K$ is covered by finitely many compact subboxes, and perimeter is lower semicontinuous. Each sublevel is therefore compact in this fixed Polish product. These compactness bounds may depend on the fiber and on $R_\\ell$; the projection theorem requires compact fibers, not a common bound across plaque data. Strict sublevels are countable unions of such compact sections. The Borel projection theorem for sets with $\\sigma$-compact sections therefore makes the minimum value a Borel function of the chart data. Apply it again to minimize volume in the compact set of minimizers. The selected section is a singleton in the $L^1$ coding, by leastness, so the Borel singleton-section theorem gives the selected minimizer as a Borel function. These are standard forms of the Arsenin–Kunugui and Lusin–Souslin theorems; see [[Kechris1995](#ref-Kechris1995), Theorem 18.18 and Corollary 15.",

Then $$\\label{eq:degree-estimate}\n |\\mathcal D_X(G)|(2\\tau)^d\n \\le \\frac{\\exp(C_1d/q)}{\\prod_{i=1}^dL_i}\n     \\int_{\\{|G|_\\infty<b_d\\}}\n       \\exp\\left(\\frac{-\\mathcal S(h,\\phi)+\\sum_{i=1}^dH_i(G)}2\\right)dV_h.$$ For $d=0$ the product is one, the sum is zero, and $\\mathcal S(h,\\phi)=0$; the assertion is the bound of a signed count by the unsigned count.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-degree-descent",
        "exact_quote": "Assume the hypotheses of Theorem <a href=\"#thm:degree\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"thm:degree\">16</a> with $d\\ge1$, put $p=d-1$, and put $$\\Theta=\\exp\\left(\\frac{-\\mathcal S(h,\\phi)+\\sum_{i=1}^dH_i(G)}2\\right).$$ There is a Borel family of oriented regular hypersurface portions $\\Sigma_s$ in the leaves, $-\\tau<s<\\tau$, and, for almost every $s$, a positive function $v_s$ on $\\Sigma_s$, smooth along it and Borel in its charts, with the following properties. Transverse null subsets and their saturations may be removed for each such $s$.*\n\n\n1.  *For every nonnegative Borel function $F$ supported in $\\{|G|_\\infty<b_d\\}$, $$\\label{eq:degree-coarea}\n     \\int_{-\\tau}^{\\tau}\\!ds\\int_{\\Sigma_s}v_sF\\,dA_h\n                           \\le\\int_XF\\,dV_h.$$*\n\n\n2.  *For each such $s$ there is a controlled measured $p$-band $(X_s^+,h_s^+,\\phi_s^+,J_s^+)$ over $Q_{T_p}^p$ of degree $\\mathcal D_X(G)$. Its coordinates are indexed by $j=2,\\ldots,d$ and satisfy $$L_j|dJ_{s,j}^+|_{h_s^+}\\le B-\\frac pq.$$ With $H_j^+=2(L_j|dJ_{s,j}^+|_{h_s^+}-1)_+$, there is an absolute constant $C_*>0$ such that $$\\label{eq:degree-child-integral}\n     \\begin{split}\n     &\\int_{\\{|J_s^+|_\\infty<b_p\\}}\n            \\exp\\left(\\frac{-\\mathcal S(h_s^+,\\phi_s^+)+\\sum_{j=2}^dH_j^+}{2}\\right)\n            dV_{h_s^+}\\\\\n     &\\hspace{25mm}\\le \\frac{e^{C_*/q}}{L_1}\n           \\int_{\\Sigma_s\\cap\\{|G|_\\infty<b_d\\}}v_s\\Theta\\,dA_h.\n     \\end{split}$$ In dimension $p=0$ the left side is the measured unsigned count.*\n\n*The child in the second assertion may be chosen separately for each typical $s$; no measurability of those choices in $s$ is asserted or needed.",
        "source_id": "PM-335.1"
      },
      "evidence": [

        "anchor": "eq:transfer-form",
        "exact_quote": "Put $\\delta=(1000\\sqrt q)^{-1}$. There is an oriented flat smooth $q$-manifold $\\mathcal U_\\omega$ with a local isometry $z:\\mathcal U_\\omega\\to\\mathbb R^q$, and an inclusion of $K_\\omega$ into it, such that the image of every $y\\in K_\\omega$ has a flat ball chart of radius $10\\delta$ centered at $z_y$. This construction is a standard Borel family in $\\omega$, with countably many charts and an equivariant $\\Gamma$-action. The map $F$ lifts smoothly to this family, and, writing $z_F=z\\circ F$, $$e_h(z_F)=e_h(F)=\\mathfrak e.$$ There is a closed smooth $n$-form, again denoted $\\eta$, obtained in these flat charts by pulling back the detector form through the cutoff probabilities constructed below. It is Borel and equivariant in the family, and satisfies $$\\label{eq:transfer-form}\n        \\int_{\\mathcal M}F^*\\eta=I,\\qquad |\\eta|_{\\mathrm{HS}}\\leq C_n.$$ Here the exterior Hilbert norm uses the Euclidean metric on the charts. For every fixed derivative order its derivatives admit an elementary bound in $q$.",
        "source_id": "PM-335.1"
      },
      "evidence": [

        "anchor": "eq:transfer-rounding",

      "explanation": "The coefficient vector is rounded within the joint kernel of its integer incidence equations. The common denominator is uniform over the auxiliary parameters, expectation is preserved, and all required cubical balance equations hold exactly.",

      "hypotheses": [

          "anchor": "eq:transfer-cell-value",
          "exact_quote": "A primal $n$-cell has an axis set $\\mathcal I\\subset\\{1,\\ldots,q\\}$ of size $n$, varies along those grid intervals, and has the remaining coordinates at grid vertices. Orient it in increasing axis order. Include each lift $C$ whose center is at distance less than $4\\delta$ from some witness $z_y$ in its sheet chart. The full cell fits in that chart. Its dual $m$-cube $C^\\#$ has the same center, varies along the complementary axes by $[-r/2,r/2]$, and has the other coordinates fixed. It too fits in the chart. Orient it so that the primal oriented basis, followed by the negatives of its dual oriented basis, is a positive basis of $\\mathbb R^q$. Give the primal cell the coefficient $$\\label{eq:transfer-cell-value}\n                              u_C=\\int_C\\eta.$$",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "eq:transfer-local-push",
        "exact_quote": "For the diagram $\\mathcal P$ there is a standard Borel measured family $\\mathcal A$ of oriented smooth $m$-manifolds and a smooth map $X:\\mathcal A\\to\\mathcal U_\\omega$, equivariant and Borel in all the data. The integration identities below are for fixed grid, entropy, and rounding data, including $(V,s,\\omega,\\xi)$; their integrals use the conditional state measure and depend Borelly on those data. The points over $\\mathcal T_\\delta$ represented by original chamber tiles form a Borel leaf-open subset $\\mathcal A_{\\rm orig}\\subset X^{-1}(\\mathcal T_\\delta)$. For every compactly supported smooth $m$-form $\\alpha$ on $\\mathcal T_\\delta$, $$\\label{eq:transfer-local-push}\n                 \\int_{\\mathcal A_{\\rm orig}}X^*\\alpha=Z_{\\rm rd}(\\alpha).$$ For the full doubled diagram, measured oriented integration also gives $$\\label{eq:transfer-push}\n                 X_*[\\mathcal A]=[\\mathcal P].$$ For a parameter $0<\\lambda<1/2$ and a choice $$\\label{eq:transfer-gamma}\n  0<2\\gamma_0<(m+1)^{-(m+1)}\\lambda^{m(m+1)},$$ there are a smooth metric $k$ and a closed seam set in each tile such that $\\|dX\\|_k\\leq1$, and $k=X^*k_{\\rm flat}$ off the seam. There are buffered smooth charts with elementary overlap, metric, inverse-metric, and fixed-order derivative bounds whenever $\\lambda^{-1}$ and $\\gamma_0^{-1}$ are elementary. In the sorted charts for each tile, the seam is contained in the union of the slabs $|Z_j|\\leq\\lambda$, $1\\leq j\\leq m$. The $k$-volume density in those charts has an elementary upper bound chosen before $\\lambda,\\gamma_0$; in particular this bound does not depend on these two parameters.",
        "source_id": "PM-335.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-transfer-resolution",
          "exact_quote": "For the diagram $\\mathcal P$ there is a standard Borel measured family $\\mathcal A$ of oriented smooth $m$-manifolds and a smooth map $X:\\mathcal A\\to\\mathcal U_\\omega$, equivariant and Borel in all the data. The integration identities below are for fixed grid, entropy, and rounding data, including $(V,s,\\omega,\\xi)$; their integrals use the conditional state measure and depend Borelly on those data. The points over $\\mathcal T_\\delta$ represented by original chamber tiles form a Borel leaf-open subset $\\mathcal A_{\\rm orig}\\subset X^{-1}(\\mathcal T_\\delta)$. For every compactly supported smooth $m$-form $\\alpha$ on $\\mathcal T_\\delta$, $$\\label{eq:transfer-local-push}\n                 \\int_{\\mathcal A_{\\rm orig}}X^*\\alpha=Z_{\\rm rd}(\\alpha).$$ For the full doubled diagram, measured oriented integration also gives $$\\label{eq:transfer-push}\n                 X_*[\\mathcal A]=[\\mathcal P].$$ For a parameter $0<\\lambda<1/2$ and a choice $$\\label{eq:transfer-gamma}\n  0<2\\gamma_0<(m+1)^{-(m+1)}\\lambda^{m(m+1)},$$ there are a smooth metric $k$ and a closed seam set in each tile such that $\\|dX\\|_k\\leq1$, and $k=X^*k_{\\rm flat}$ off the seam. There are buffered smooth charts with elementary overlap, metric, inverse-metric, and fixed-order derivative bounds whenever $\\lambda^{-1}$ and $\\gamma_0^{-1}$ are elementary. In the sorted charts for each tile, the seam is contained in the union of the slabs $|Z_j|\\leq\\lambda$, $1\\leq j\\leq m$. The $k$-volume density in those charts has an elementary upper bound chosen before $\\lambda,\\gamma_0$; in particular this bound does not depend on these two parameters.*\n\n*One may prescribe for each chamber over a dual cube a rotation $O_\\sigma\\in SO(q)$. There is also a smooth Borel equivariant $\\mathcal R:\\mathcal A\\to SO(q)$ equal to $O_\\sigma$ off the seam in that tile, equal to the identity near all tile facets, and homotopic to the identity.       "exact_statement": "For the diagram $\\mathcal P$ there is a standard Borel measured family $\\mathcal A$ of oriented smooth $m$-manifolds and a smooth map $X:\\mathcal A\\to\\mathcal U_\\omega$, equivariant and Borel in all the data. The integration identities below are for fixed grid, entropy, and rounding data, including $(V,s,\\omega,\\xi)$; their integrals use the conditional state measure and depend Borelly on those data. The points over $\\mathcal T_\\delta$ represented by original chamber tiles form a Borel leaf-open subset $\\mathcal A_{\\rm orig}\\subset X^{-1}(\\mathcal T_\\delta)$. For every compactly supported smooth $m$-form $\\alpha$ on $\\mathcal T_\\delta$, $$\\label{eq:transfer-local-push}\n                 \\int_{\\mathcal A_{\\rm orig}}X^*\\alpha=Z_{\\rm rd}(\\alpha).$$ For the full doubled diagram, measured oriented integration also gives $$\\label{eq:transfer-push}\n                 X_*[\\mathcal A]=[\\mathcal P].$$ For a parameter $0<\\lambda<1/2$ and a choice $$\\label{eq:transfer-gamma}\n  0<2\\gamma_0<(m+1)^{-(m+1)}\\lambda^{m(m+1)},$$ there are a smooth metric $k$ and a closed seam set in each tile such that $\\|dX\\|_k\\leq1$, and $k=X^*k_{\\rm flat}$ off the seam. There are buffered smooth charts with elementary overlap, metric, inverse-metric, and fixed-order derivative bounds whenever $\\lambda^{-1}$ and $\\gamma_0^{-1}$ are elementary. In the sorted charts for each tile, the seam is contained in the union of the slabs $|Z_j|\\leq\\lambda$, $1\\leq j\\leq m$. The $k$-volume density in those charts has an elementary upper bound chosen before $\\lambda,\\gamma_0$; in particular this bound does not depend on these two parameters.",
          "source_id": "PM-335.1"
        }
      ],

<!-- PM:END pm.claims.v2 -->

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **Real simplicial volume** ([source](#sec:introduction)): The simplicial volume of a closed connected oriented $n$-manifold $M$ is the $\ell^1$-seminorm of its real fundamental class: $$\\|M\\|= \inf\left\{\sum_j\|a_j\|: \sum_j a_j\sigma_j\text{ is a finite real singular cycle representing } [M]_{\mathbb R}\right\}.$$
- **Negative part of scalar curvature** ([source](#sec:introduction)): With $$\mathop{\mathrm{Scal}}_g^-:=\max\{0,-\mathop{\mathrm{Scal}}_g\},$$ the integral $\int_M(\mathop{\mathrm{Scal}}_g^-)^{n/2}\,dV_g$ is invariant under constant rescaling of $g$.
- **Yamabe constant** ([source](#pm-h-5)): Write $Y(M,[g])$ for the Yamabe constant of the conformal class of $g$: $$Y(M,[g])= \inf_{u\in C^\infty(M),\,u>0} \frac{\displaystyle\int_M \left(\frac{4(n-1)}{n-2}\|du\|_g^2+\mathop{\mathrm{Scal}}_g u^2\right)dV_g} {\displaystyle\left(\int_Mu^{2n/(n-2)}\,dV_g\right)^{(n-2)/n}}.$$
- **Probability simplex and cocycle form** ([source](#eq:probability-form)): Let $$\Delta_{\mathcal L}= \left\{p=(p_\ell)_{\ell\in\mathcal L}: p_\ell\ge0,\ \sum_\ell p_\ell=1,\ p\text{ has finite support}\right\}.$$ On each finite-dimensional face define the $n$-form $$\eta_p(v_1,\ldots,v_n)=n!\,c(p,v_1,\ldots,v_n). \label{eq:probability-form}$$
- **Root probability map** ([source](#pm-h-6)): We call a map $F=(F_\ell)$ with nonnegative coordinates and $\sum_\ell F_\ell^2=1$ a *root probability map*.
- **Measured equivariant family** ([source](#pm-h-7)): A measured equivariant family over $M$ consists of $\Gamma$-equivariant Borel data on $\widetilde M\times\Omega$, smooth in the $\widetilde M$ variable.
- **Measured integration convention** ([source](#eq:measured-integral)): Choose a finite evenly covered atlas $U_a$ of $M$, chosen lifts $\widetilde U_a$, and a smooth partition of unity $\chi_a$ subordinate to the atlas. For an integrable equivariant leafwise $n$-form $\alpha$, set $$\int_{\mathcal M}\alpha = \sum_a\int_\Omega\int_{\widetilde U_a} (\chi_a\circ\pi)\,\alpha_\omega\,d\mu(\omega). \label{eq:measured-integral}$$
- **Weighted scalar curvature and weighted operators** ([source](#sec:graphs)): For a metric $h$ and a smooth weight $\phi$, write $$\mathcal S(h,\phi)=\mathop{\mathrm{Scal}}_h-2\Delta_h\phi-\|d\phi\|_h^2, \qquad \mathop{\mathrm{div}}_\phi V=e^{-\phi}\mathop{\mathrm{div}}(e^\phi V), \qquad \Delta_\phi=\mathop{\mathrm{div}}_\phi\nabla .$$
- **Bounded geometry** ([source](#pm-h-11)): A manifold has *bounded geometry* here if it has coordinate charts of a common positive size whose smaller concentric domains cover the manifold, with a common positive buffer to each chart boundary, and with uniform bounds on the metric, its inverse, and all their coordinate derivatives.
- **Uniformly smooth function** ([source](#pm-h-11)): A function is uniformly smooth if all its coordinate derivatives, including its value, are uniformly bounded on these charts.
- **Root-map energy** ([source](#pm-h-13)): For a root probability map, its energy is $$e_h(F)=\|dF\|_{h,\mathrm{HS}}^2=\sum_i\|dF_i\|_h^2.$$
- **Elementary function** ([source](#sec:entropy)): We call a function $\mathcal B:[1,\infty)\to[0,\infty)$ *elementary* if, for some fixed $H\in\mathbb N\cup\{0\}$ and $A,B>0$, $$\mathcal B(q)\leq \exp^{[H]}\bigl(B(1+q)^A\bigr)\qquad(q\geq1).$$
- **Controlled measured band** ([source](#stmt-def-degree-band)): Let $T>0$. A controlled measured $d$-band over $Q_T^d$ consists of a measured space of oriented $d$-manifolds as above, a leafwise metric $h$, a function $\phi$, and a map $G=(G_1,\ldots,G_d):X\to Q_T^d$. For every $0<t<T$ the part $\{\|G\|_\infty\le t\}$ has a countable flow-box cover with the following properties. 1. The boxes have one common positive size and fixed positive buffers: their smaller concentric boxes cover the indicated part. They can be chosen inside $\{\|G\|_\infty<t'\}$ for some $t<t'<T$. After rescaling coordinates we may, and do, call them unit boxes with buffers. 2. In these boxes the coefficients of $h$ and $h^{-1}$ and all their leafwise coordinate derivatives are bounded by constants common to the cover. The same holds for all derivatives of $G$ and for all positive-order derivatives of $\phi$. No bound on the value of $\phi$ is required. 3. The sum of the measures of the transversals of this cover is finite. There is a common bound on the number of plaques of the cover whose buffered boxes meet any one plaque of the cover. The cover and every bound in this definition may depend on $t$ and on the given band. The leaves are not assumed complete, and $G$ is not assumed proper.
- **Measured degree** ([source](#eq:degree-definition)): If $\omega$ is a smooth, compactly supported $d$-form on $Q_T^d$ with $\int_{Q_T^d}\omega=1$, define $$\label{eq:degree-definition} \mathcal D_X(G)=\int_X G^*\omega.$$
- **Coordinate gradient penalty** ([source](#stmt-thm-degree)): Define $H_i(G)=2(L_i\|dG_i\|_h-1)_+$.
- **Comparison map** ([source](#eq:transfer-comparison)): Define there $$\label{eq:transfer-comparison} G(x,a)=\mathcal R(a)(z_F(x)-z_{X(a)}).$$

The boxes have one common positive size and fixed positive buffers: their smaller concentric boxes cover the indicated part. They can be chosen inside $\\{|G|_\\infty<t'\\}$ for some $t<t'<T$. After rescaling coordinates we may, and do, call them unit boxes with buffers.\n\n2.  In these boxes the coefficients of $h$ and $h^{-1}$ and all their leafwise coordinate derivatives are bounded by constants common to the cover. The same holds for all derivatives of $G$ and for all positive-order derivatives of $\\phi$. No bound on the value of $\\phi$ is required.\n\n3.  The sum of the measures of the transversals of this cover is finite. There is a common bound on the number of plaques of the cover whose buffered boxes meet any one plaque of the cover.\n\nThe cover and every bound in this definition may depend on $t$ and on the given band. The leaves are not assumed complete, and $G$ is not assumed proper.",
      "interpretation": "Uniform buffered flow boxes control the metric, map, weight derivatives, transverse mass, and plaque overlap on each inner target band. Completeness and properness are not required.",
      "kind": "definition",
      "name": "Controlled measured band",
      "scope": "Measured oriented leaf manifolds mapping to a cube."
    },
    {
      "definition_anchor": "eq:degree-definition",

          "source_id": "PM-335.1"

      ],

      "interpretation": "The signed pullback integral is independent of the compactly supported unit-integral target form under the controlled-band Stokes convention.",

| q-integral-lower-bound | Integral of the critical power of negative scalar curvature | ≥ | $a_n\\|M\\|$ | The positive constant depends only on dimension. |
| q-normalized-volume-upper-bound | Real simplicial volume | ≤ | $C_n\mathop{\mathrm{Vol}}_g(M)$ | The finite positive constant is dimensional; no evaluated numerical constant is supplied. |
| q-main-constant-reciprocal | Integral comparison constant | = | $C_n^{-1}$ | The reciprocal uses the dimensional positive normalized-volume constant. |
| q-detector-pairing-lower-bound | Bounded cocycle pairing | ≥ | $\frac12\\|M\\|$ | The cocycle is chosen using bounded-cohomology duality and approximation of its seminorm. |
| q-preserved-weighted-density | Weighted graph volume density | = | $e^\phi dV_h$ | Smooth input functions and positive graph scale. |
| q-positive-graph-height-upper-bound | Scaled positive prescribed graph height | ≤ | $2$ | The height bounds apply to the solution under the positive prescription parameters. |
| q-entropy-absolute-gradient | Root-map gradient norm | ≤ | $E^{-1/100}$ | The energy coefficient exceeds the prescribed elementary threshold and finite minimum. |
| q-sweep-volume-surplus-bound | Single-sweep volume logarithm | ≤ | $C_n\epsilon^{1/4}(\epsilon+P_p)^{3/4}$ | The bound is dimensional and concerns the current sweep parameter and surplus. |
| q-entropy-sweep-count | Number of sweeps below the final parameter | ≤ | $C_n(1+\log^3(1+E))$ | This counts analytic sweeps; it is not a runtime bound for solving the graph equations. |
| q-measured-degree-upper-bound | Absolute measured degree multiplied by the target-width factor | ≤ | $\frac{\exp(C_1d/q)}{\prod_{i=1}^dL_i} \int_{\{\|G\|_\infty<b_d\}} \exp\left(\frac{-\mathcal S(h,\phi)+\sum_{i=1}^dH_i(G)}2\right)dV_h.$ | Positive row lengths, a strict horizon condition, and dimensional descent within the ambient parameter. |
| q-rounded-coefficient-error | Absolute change of a cubical coefficient under rounding | ≤ | $r^nq^{-n}$ | The common denominator is independent of the grid rotation, shift, and transverse entropy parameter. |
| q-tangent-comparison-length | Tangent comparison length | = | $b_*\sqrt E$ | The positive dimensional factor is chosen before the entropy construction and is shared by the tangent and normal lengths. |
| q-normal-comparison-length | Normal comparison length | = | $b_*\sqrt{qE}$ | The auxiliary dimension enters this length to balance the angular averaging bound. |
| q-transfer-density-cancellation | Combined length, energy, and volume-density factor | = | $b_*^{-n}q^{-n/2}\rho$ | The cancellation removes tangent volume and all powers of the energy coefficient. |

          "exact_quote": "Let $(X,h)$ be a smooth Riemannian manifold, let $\\phi,f$ be smooth real functions, and let $N>0$. Set $$L=(1+N^2|df|_h^2)^{1/2},\\qquad v=L^{-1},\\qquad\n U=\\frac{N\\nabla_h f}{L},\\qquad\n h'=h+N^2df\\otimes df,\\qquad \\phi'=\\phi-\\log L.$$ View $h'$ as the induced metric on the graph $\\Sigma=\\{(x,Nf(x)):x\\in X\\}$ in $(X\\times\\mathbb R,h+dz^2)$, and extend $\\phi$ constantly in the $z$-direction. Orient the graph by $\\nu=(-U,v)$, put $\\mathrm{II}(V,W)=\\langle\\bar\\nabla_V\\nu,W\\rangle$, and let $$H=\\mathop{\\mathrm{tr}}_\\Sigma\\mathrm{II},\\qquad\n D=H+\\partial_\\nu\\phi=-\\mathop{\\mathrm{div}}_\\phi U .$$ Here $D$, and its derivative $U(D)$, are regarded as functions on $X$ through the graph projection. Then $$\\mathcal S(h',\\phi')-\\mathcal S(h,\\phi)\n =D^2-2U(D)+|\\mathrm{II}|_{h'}^2+|d\\log L|_{h'}^2.\n \\label{eq:graph-identity}$$ Moreover, $$e^{\\phi'}dV_{h'}=e^\\phi dV_h,\\qquad\n N^2|df|_{h'}^2=1-v^2=|U|_h^2,\\qquad\n |d\\log L|_{h'}\\le L|U|_h|\\mathrm{II}|_{h'} .\n \\label{eq:graph-basic-bounds}$$",
          "source_id": "PM-335.1"
        }

            "anchor": "eq:entropy-step-schedule",
            "exact_quote": "The step size has two jobs: the scalar losses $O_n(l^2)$ must be summable, and the number of support enlargements before reaching $E$ must grow at most cubically in $\\log E$. Since one sweep changes $\\log(1+\\epsilon)$ by a quantity comparable to $l$, we take $$\\label{eq:entropy-step-schedule}\n l(\\epsilon)=\\frac{l_*}{(1+\\log(1+\\epsilon))^2},$$ where the dimensional $l_*>0$ is chosen below $l_0$ from Lemma <a href=\"#lem:entropy-increment\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:entropy-increment\">11</a>. Thus all the parameter choices in that lemma, including $\\theta=D_*l$, apply.",
            "source_id": "PM-335.1"
          }

          "anchor": "eq:degree-estimate",
          "exact_quote": "There are absolute constants $C_0,C_1>0$ with the following property. Let $q\\ge2$, let $d$ be an integer with $0\\le d\\le q$, and let $\\tau>0$. For $0\\le r\\le d$ set $$\\label{eq:degree-widths}\n T_r=2\\tau+\\frac{r\\tau}{q},\\qquad\n b_r=\\tau\\left(1+\\frac1{2q}+\\frac r{4q^2}\\right).$$ Suppose $(X,h,\\phi,G)$ is a controlled measured $d$-band over $Q_{T_d}^d$ in the sense of Definition <a href=\"#def:degree-band\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"def:degree-band\">15</a>. Suppose throughout $X$ that $$\\label{eq:degree-horizon-bound}\n             L_i|dG_i|_h\\le B-\\frac dq\\qquad(1\\le i\\le d),$$ and suppose that $$\\label{eq:degree-horizon}\n       \\frac{C_0(1+B)^{2/3}}{L_i}<\\frac{\\tau}{10q}\n                       \\qquad(1\\le i\\le d).$$ Define $H_i(G)=2(L_i|dG_i|_h-1)_+$. Then $$\\label{eq:degree-estimate}\n |\\mathcal D_X(G)|(2\\tau)^d\n \\le \\frac{\\exp(C_1d/q)}{\\prod_{i=1}^dL_i}\n     \\int_{\\{|G|_\\infty<b_d\\}}\n       \\exp\\left(\\frac{-\\mathcal S(h,\\phi)+\\sum_{i=1}^dH_i(G)}2\\right)dV_h.$$ For $d=0$ the product is one, the sum is zero, and $\\mathcal S(h,\\phi)=0$; the assertion is the bound of a signed count by the unsigned count.",

            "anchor": "stmt-thm-degree",
            "exact_quote": "Suppose $(X,h,\\phi,G)$ is a controlled measured $d$-band over $Q_{T_d}^d$ in the sense of Definition <a href=\"#def:degree-band\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"def:degree-band\">15</a>. Let $B\\ge1$ and $L_1,\\ldots,L_d>0$. Suppose throughout $X$ that $$\\label{eq:degree-horizon-bound}\n             L_i|dG_i|_h\\le B-\\frac dq\\qquad(1\\le i\\le d),$$ and suppose that $$\\label{eq:degree-horizon}\n       \\frac{C_0(1+B)^{2/3}}{L_i}<\\frac{\\tau}{10q}\n                       \\qquad(1\\le i\\le d).$$ Define $H_i(G)=2(L_i|dG_i|_h-1)_+$. Then $$\\label{eq:degree-estimate}\n |\\mathcal D_X(G)|(2\\tau)^d\n \\le \\frac{\\exp(C_1d/q)}{\\prod_{i=1}^dL_i}\n     \\int_{\\{|G|_\\infty<b_d\\}}\n       \\exp\\left(\\frac{-\\mathcal S(h,\\phi)+\\sum_{i=1}^dH_i(G)}2\\right)dV_h.$$ For $d=0$ the product is one, the sum is zero, and $\\mathcal S(h,\\phi)=0$; the assertion is the bound of a signed count by the unsigned count.*\n\n</div>\n\nThe proof removes one coordinate at a time. For $r\\ge1$, the gaps $T_r-T_{r-1}=\\tau/q$ and $b_r-b_{r-1}=\\tau/(4q^2)$ supply, respectively, chart buffers and room for the residual value error when locating the integration core, while the permitted scaled gradient of each retained coordinate increases by $1/q$. A family of boundaries ordered by a real parameter supplies the factor $2\\tau$ on the left. Its normal speed supplies the coarea factor on the right. In dimensions where the boundaries can be singular we will construct a regular band of the same degree; a scalar gain will pay exactly for the volume of the graphs used to construct it. The following formulation separates that construction from the final induction.\n\n\n<div id=\"lem:degree-descent\" class=\"lemma\">\n\n**Lemma 17** (One step of measured descent). *Assume the hypotheses of Theorem <a href=\"#thm:degree\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"thm:degree\">16</a> with $d\\ge1$, put $p=d-1$, and put $$\\Theta=\\exp\\left(\\frac{-\\mathcal S(h,\\phi)+\\sum_{i=1}^dH_i(G)}2\\right).$$ There is a Borel family of oriented regular hypersurface portions $\\Sigma_s$ in the leaves, $-\\tau<s<\\tau$, and, for almost every $s$, a positive function $v_s$ on $\\Sigma_s$, smooth along it and Borel in its charts, with the following properties. Transverse null subsets and their saturations may be removed for each such $s$.*\n\n\n1.  *For every nonnegative Borel function $F$ supported in $\\{|G|_\\infty<b_d\\}$, $$\\label{eq:degree-coarea}\n     \\int_{-\\tau}^{\\tau}\\!ds\\int_{\\Sigma_s}v_sF\\,dA_h\n                           \\le\\int_XF\\,dV_h.$$*\n\n\n2.  *For each such $s$ there is a controlled measured $p$-band $(X_s^+,h_s^+,\\phi_s^+,J_s^+)$ over $Q_{T_p}^p$ of degree $\\mathcal D_X(G)$. Its coordinates are indexed by $j=2,\\ldots,d$ and satisfy $$L_j|dJ_{s,j}^+|_{h_s^+}\\le B-\\frac pq.$$ With $H_j^+=2(L_j|dJ_{s,j}^+|_{h_s^+}-1)_+$, there is an absolute constant $C_*>0$ such that $$\\label{eq:degree-child-integral}\n     \\begin{split}\n     &\\int_{\\{|J_s^+|_\\infty<b_p\\}}\n            \\exp\\left(\\frac{-\\mathcal S(h_s^+,\\phi_s^+)+\\sum_{j=2}^dH_j^+}{2}\\right)\n            dV_{h_s^+}\\\\\n     &\\hspace{25mm}\\le \\frac{e^{C_*/q}}{L_1}\n           \\int_{\\Sigma_s\\cap\\{|G|_\\infty<b_d\\}}v_s\\Theta\\,dA_h.\n     \\end{split}$$ In dimension $p=0$ the left side is the measured unsigned count.*\n\n*The child in the second assertion may be chosen separately for each typical $s$; no measurability of those choices in $s$ is asserted or needed.*\n\n</div>\n\n\n#### An ordered family of boundaries\n\nWe prove Lemma <a href=\"#lem:degree-descent\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:degree-descent\">17</a> in this and the next two subsections. Fix intermediate widths $$\\label{eq:degree-intermediate-widths}\n              T_p<t_1<t_2<t_3<t_4<T_d.$$ Whenever a chart buffer is needed, choose further intermediate widths between these. The width $t_4$ controls confinement and the parent charts, $t_3$ contains the boundary portions to be used, $t_2$ will bound an auxiliary complete region, and $t_1$ will bound the child. Such choices can depend on the given band.\n\n\n\nChoose once and for all a smooth even function $\\gamma$ with $0\\le\\gamma(y)\\le y^2$ and with $\\gamma(y)$ bounded above and below by positive absolute multiples of $|y|^{3/2}$ for $|y|\\ge1$. Let $P_0$ be the solution about zero of $$\\label{eq:degree-pressure}\n P_0(0)=0,\\qquad P_0'=L_1\\psi(P_0),\\qquad\n       \\psi(y)=1+\\frac{\\gamma(y)}{8(1+B)}.$$ It is odd and increasing and has poles at $\\pm b$, where $$b=\\frac1{L_1}\\int_0^\\infty\\frac{dy}{\\psi(y)}\n       \\le\\frac{C(1+B)^{2/3}}{L_1}.$$ For the inequality, split the integral at $(1+B)^{2/3}$ and use the $|y|^{3/2}$ bound on the tail. Choose the absolute $C_0$ in <a href=\"#eq:degree-horizon\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:degree-horizon\">[eq:degree-horizon]</a> at least this $C$. Thus $$\\label{eq:degree-pole-width}\n                         b<\\frac{\\tau}{10q}.$$\n\n\n###### The local slice calculation.\n\n\nWe first record the analytic purpose of the pressure law. Let $\\Sigma$ be a smooth cooriented local hypersurface, with unit normal $\\nu$, and set $$\\begin{gathered}\n D=H+\\partial_\\nu\\phi,\\qquad\n H=\\operatorname{tr}\\mathrm{II},\\qquad\n \\mathrm{II}(Y,Z)=\\langle\\nabla_Y\\nu,Z\\rangle,\\\\\n \\mathcal P=|\\mathrm{II}|^2+\n             (\\operatorname{Ric}_h-\\nabla^2\\phi)(\\nu,\\nu).\n \\end{gathered}$$ Put $\\mathfrak a=L_1\\psi(D)>0$. When $D=P_0(s-G_1)$, the derivatives of the raw pressure are $\\partial_sP_0(s-G_1)=\\mathfrak a$ and $d_xP_0(s-G_1)=-\\mathfrak a\\,dG_1$. The following calculation uses only a positive smooth function $v$ satisfying the local equation $$\\label{eq:degree-local-lapse}\n -\\bigl(\\Delta_{\\Sigma,\\phi}+\\mathcal P\n                 -\\mathfrak a\\,dG_1(\\nu)\\bigr)v=\\mathfrak a.$$ Here $\\Delta_{\\Sigma,\\phi}$ is weighted by $e^{\\phi|_\\Sigma}dA_h$, and slice norms use the induced metric. The ordered construction below will produce this equation on almost every regular slice.\n\n\nPut $\\phi_\\Sigma=\\phi|_\\Sigma+\\ln v$ and $S_\\Sigma=\\mathcal S(h|_{T\\Sigma},\\phi_\\Sigma)$. Gauss’ equation and the normal/tangential decomposition of derivatives of $\\phi$ give $$\\mathcal S(h|_{T\\Sigma},\\phi|_\\Sigma)\n       =\\mathcal S(h,\\phi)+D^2-2\\mathcal P+|\\mathrm{II}|^2.$$ Adding $\\ln v$ to the weight changes this by $-2\\Delta_{\\Sigma,\\phi}v/v+|d\\ln v|^2$. Substitution of <a href=\"#eq:degree-local-lapse\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:degree-local-lapse\">[eq:degree-local-lapse]</a> yields the exact identity $$\\label{eq:degree-slice-identity}\n S_\\Sigma=\\mathcal S(h,\\phi)+D^2-2\\mathfrak a\\,dG_1(\\nu)\n                 +|\\mathrm{II}|^2+\\frac{2\\mathfrak a}{v}+|d\\ln v|^2.$$ This is also the hypersurface calculation behind Lemma <a href=\"#lem:graph-identity\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:graph-identity\">5</a>; it is valid for $p=0$ with left side zero.\n\n\n\nSet $t=L_1|dG_1|_h\\le B$ and $x=v/L_1$. Since $\\gamma(D)\\le D^2$, $$-2\\mathfrak a\\,dG_1(\\nu)\n       \\ge-2t\\left(1+\\frac{\\gamma(D)}{8(1+B)}\\right),\n \\qquad \\frac{t\\gamma(D)}{4(1+B)}\\le\\frac{D^2}{4}.$$ We may discard the remaining nonnegative part of $D^2$, and use $2\\mathfrak a/v\\ge2/x$. Finally, $t\\le1+(t-1)_+$ and $x^{-1}+\\ln x\\ge1$ for $x>0$. Thus <a href=\"#eq:degree-slice-identity\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:degree-slice-identity\">[eq:degree-slice-identity]</a> implies $$\\label{eq:degree-slice-bound}\n S_\\Sigma\\ge\\mathcal S(h,\\phi)-H_1(G)-2\\ln(v/L_1)+|d\\ln v|^2.$$ The square term will be used later when the weight is changed near singular points. For $j=2,\\ldots,d$, put $H_j^\\Sigma=2(L_j|d_\\Sigma G_j|-1)_+$. Tangential restriction gives $H_j^\\Sigma\\le H_j(G)$, so the full pointwise density comparison is $$\\label{eq:degree-raw-integrand}\n \\exp\\left(\\frac{-S_\\Sigma+\\sum_{j=2}^dH_j^\\Sigma}{2}\\right)\n \\le\\frac v{L_1}e^{-|d\\ln v|^2/2}\\Theta\n \\le\\frac v{L_1}\\Theta.$$ For $p=0$ the sum and slice gradient are zero, and the left side is one. The remaining work supplies the slices and speeds, and verifies the controlled-band and degree conditions needed to use this comparison.\n\nFor a small $\\eta>0$, prolong $P_0$ smoothly and strictly increasingly past the points $\\pm(b-\\eta)$, keeping it unchanged on $[-b+\\eta,b-\\eta]$. Denote the resulting smooth function by $\\widehat P$ and set $$\\mathfrak h_s(x)=\\widehat P(s-G_1(x)),\\qquad -\\tau<s<\\tau.$$ We will choose $\\eta$ sufficiently small for the given band and then keep it fixed. On every boundary portion used below, $\\widehat P$ will agree with $P_0$ and hence obey <a href=\"#eq:degree-pressure\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:degree-pressure\">[eq:degree-pressure]</a>.\n\n\n###### Least minimizers, including on noncompact leaves.\n\n\nWrite $P_\\phi(E;U)=\\int_{\\partial^*E\\cap U}e^\\phi\\,dA_h$ for the weighted perimeter of a set of locally finite perimeter. We use local differences of the functional $$\\label{eq:degree-functional}\n                  P_\\phi(E)-\\int_E\\mathfrak h_s e^\\phi\\,dV_h.$$ This is a weighted $\\mu$-bubble functional. For soap bubbles in Gromov’s terminology, see [[Gromov1996](#ref-Gromov1996), Section 556]; for the weighted functional and its local first- and second-variation formulas, see also [[ChodoshLi2024](#ref-ChodoshLi2024), Section 3, Equation (1), and Lemmas 13–14]. Neither term is required to be finite on the whole leaf. In particular, this construction uses no global weighted-volume bound.\n\nThe output will be a canonical nested family $E_s$ on each open leaf, independent of the exhaustion and jointly Borel in the plaque data and $s$. Each $E_s$ will minimize these local differences for compact variations. The local comparison argument also supplies the perimeter compactness needed later to turn convergence of sets into graphical convergence at regular limit points. We establish these properties in turn, before confining the boundaries to the raw-pressure region.\n\n*Compact domains and leastness.* For a compact subset $K$ of a leaf, minimize <a href=\"#eq:degree-functional\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:degree-functional\">[eq:degree-functional]</a> over sets which vanish off $K$, using their full perimeter in the leaf. The empty set is a competitor. On a neighborhood of $K$ the density is smooth and positive and the forcing is bounded. A bound on the functional therefore gives a perimeter bound, and BV compactness and lower semicontinuity give a minimizer; see, for example, [[Maggi2012](#ref-Maggi2012), Theorem 12.26] for the compactness theorem. Among the minimizers choose one of minimum ordinary volume. The weighted perimeter satisfies $$P_\\phi(E\\cap F)+P_\\phi(E\\cup F)\\le P_\\phi(E)+P_\\phi(F),$$ and the volume term for a fixed forcing is additive under union and intersection. Thus the intersection of any two minimizers is again a minimizer. Minimum volume forces the selected minimizer to be contained, up to null sets, in every other one. It is consequently unique as an $L^1$ set; call it the least minimizer in $K$.\n\n*Order and exhaustion.* The same inequality proves both monotonicities we need. If $K\\subset K'$ and two forcings satisfy $\\mathfrak h\\le\\mathfrak h'$, a minimizer for $(K,\\mathfrak h)$ and one for $(K',\\mathfrak h')$ obey $$\\mathcal F_{\\mathfrak h}(E\\cap F)+\\mathcal F_{\\mathfrak h'}(E\\cup F)\n              \\le \\mathcal F_{\\mathfrak h}(E)+\\mathcal F_{\\mathfrak h'}(F),$$ where $\\mathcal F$ denotes <a href=\"#eq:degree-functional\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:degree-functional\">[eq:degree-functional]</a>; the inequality for the volume terms follows on $E\\setminus F$ from $\\mathfrak h\\le\\mathfrak h'$. Minimality in the two domains makes both inequalities equalities, and leastness in $K$ implies $E\\subset F$. Exhaust a leaf by increasing finite unions of compact coordinate subboxes whose interiors exhaust it. The least minimizers increase with this exhaustion. Their union, interpreted in $L^1_{\\mathrm{loc}}$, is independent of the exhaustion, since each compact subset of either exhaustion is eventually contained in one of the other. This defines $E_s$, and the forcing monotonicity makes $E_s\\subset E_t$ for $s<t$.\n\n*Local comparison.* The limit must still minimize for compact variations, since all later regularity and compactness arguments use that property. Ball replacement in a relatively compact chart gives uniform local perimeter bounds for the exhaustion minimizers: choose a radius with controlled slicing area, replace the set inside that ball by the full or empty ball, and bound the forcing term by its local supremum times the ball volume. Thus the increasing sets converge locally in BV weakly and in $L^1$. To pass a comparison to the limit, surround the compact region to be changed by a thin outer belt on which the proposed competitor agrees with $E_s$. Choose a Lipschitz coordinate level function across the belt. Coarea bounds the integral, over its levels, of the trace mismatch between an exhaustion minimizer and $E_s$ by their $L^1$ mismatch in the belt. One may therefore splice the proposed competitor inside a level to the exhaustion minimizer outside it so that the extra trace area tends to zero. The levels can also be chosen with zero limiting perimeter on their boundaries, since this excludes only a null set of levels. Minimality before passage to the limit, convergence of the bounded forcing integrals, and lower semicontinuity give the compact comparison for $E_s$. The same argument applies under smooth local convergence of the metric, density, and forcing coefficients. Below we apply it with the unchanged limit as competitor to prove convergence of the local perimeter measures. Multiplying a density by a positive constant has no effect on any of these comparisons.\n\n*Borel dependence.* We explain why all these choices are Borel, including jointly in $s$. For this coding, subdivide the original atlas into bounded coordinate boxes with compact closure in the older boxes, and affinely normalize each to one fixed cube $Q=(-1,1)^d$. Fix once a countable list $(R_\\ell)_{\\ell\\ge1}$ of all open rational boxes with closure in $Q$. Starting at a plaque, enumerate its accessible normalized plaque occurrences Borel measurably in slots $j\\ge1$, using dummy slots when necessary. Use the fixed Polish space $$\\mathcal Z=\\prod_{j,\\ell\\ge1}L^1(R_\\ell),$$ with Lebesgue $L^1$ norms and the product topology. A set is coded by its indicator on $R_\\ell$ in occurrence $j$; dummy entries are zero. Compatibility is tested on compact exhaustions of all overlap domains, including repeated occurrences. The closures of the first finitely many accessible $R_\\ell$ in a fixed enumeration of the pairs $(j,\\ell)$ give the compact exhaustion domains. The conditions that the code is an indicator, agrees on overlaps, and vanishes off the specified domain are Borel, and for fixed plaque data are closed in $\\mathcal Z$.\n\nIntegrals against chartwise Borel smooth densities are Borel by Fubini. Perimeter is Borel as well: its supremum over divergence integrals may be taken over fixed countable families of rational smooth test fields in the $R_\\ell$, and finite sums of them. Partitions of unity in a leaf show density among all compactly supported tests. Norm constraints are checked on countably many dense coordinate points; overlap maps and a partition by the first covering chart make the integrals Borel.\n\nFix one plaque-data fiber and one compact exhaustion domain $K$. A functional sublevel for sets supported in $K$ bounds their weighted perimeter, since the forcing integral is uniformly bounded on this fixed compact set. In every active $R_\\ell$ the metric and density have a positive lower bound on its closure, so this gives a Euclidean BV bound there; the indicator’s $L^1(R_\\ell)$ norm is bounded by $|R_\\ell|$. Diagonal BV compactness gives convergence in $\\mathcal Z$ along a subsequence. The fiberwise closed indicator, overlap, and support conditions persist. The volume term is continuous, since $K$ is covered by finitely many compact subboxes, and perimeter is lower semicontinuous. Each sublevel is therefore compact in this fixed Polish product. These compactness bounds may depend on the fiber and on $R_\\ell$; the projection theorem requires compact fibers, not a common bound across plaque data. Strict sublevels are countable unions of such compact sections. The Borel projection theorem for sets with $\\sigma$-compact sections therefore makes the minimum value a Borel function of the chart data. Apply it again to minimize volume in the compact set of minimizers. The selected section is a singleton in the $L^1$ coding, by leastness, so the Borel singleton-section theorem gives the selected minimizer as a Borel function. These are standard forms of the Arsenin–Kunugui and Lusin–Souslin theorems; see [[Kechris1995](#ref-Kechris1995), Theorem 18.18 and Corollary 15.",

            "anchor": "eq:transfer-cell-value",
            "exact_quote": "A primal $n$-cell has an axis set $\\mathcal I\\subset\\{1,\\ldots,q\\}$ of size $n$, varies along those grid intervals, and has the remaining coordinates at grid vertices. Orient it in increasing axis order. Include each lift $C$ whose center is at distance less than $4\\delta$ from some witness $z_y$ in its sheet chart. The full cell fits in that chart. Its dual $m$-cube $C^\\#$ has the same center, varies along the complementary axes by $[-r/2,r/2]$, and has the other coordinates fixed. It too fits in the chart. Orient it so that the primal oriented basis, followed by the negatives of its dual oriented basis, is a positive basis of $\\mathbb R^q$. <a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **step-import-vanishing** [claim](#stmt-thm-vanishing-input) / [proof](#pm-h-5): Use the explicitly imported nonnegative-scalar-curvature vanishing theorem. -> Vanishing of real simplicial volume.
- **step-yamabe-transfer** [claim](#stmt-prop-yamabe-reduction) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-yamabe-reduction): Choose the conformal Yamabe minimizer, force its scalar curvature to be negative by vanishing, normalize it, and apply Hölder's inequality. -> The normalized minimizing volume is bounded by the original negative-scalar integral, so the assumed volume estimate transfers.
- **step-realize-detector** [claim](#stmt-prop-detector) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-detector): Extend an alternating bounded cocycle multilinearly, prove closedness on probability faces, and normalize lifted bump functions to a root probability map. -> An equivariant smooth probability map whose detector integral is the chosen fundamental-class pairing.
- **step-weighted-graph-calculation** [claim](#stmt-lem-graph-identity) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-graph-identity): Combine the weighted Gauss equation with the vertical translation identity and the rank-one determinant and inverse formulas. -> The weighted scalar graph identity and exact preservation of weighted density.
- **step-complete-graph-solves** [claim](#stmt-lem-graph-prescription) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-graph-prescription): Use height barriers, an angle estimate, elliptic regularity, continuation in uniform spaces, comparison, and singleton Borel coding. -> Unique bounded-height smooth prescribed graphs with uniform estimates and Borel equivariance.
- **step-localize-coordinate-graph** [claim](#stmt-lem-graph-localization) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-graph-localization): Build a bounded-width forcing plateau, estimate decay of the complete solution there, and cut it off with controlled derivatives. -> A localized smooth graph whose scalar and prescription errors become uniformly small.
- **step-sweep-probability-coordinates** [claim](#stmt-prop-sweep) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-sweep): Process separated colors in order, telescope graph identities, estimate the normalized output energy, and absorb forcing costs into mean, variance, and derivative reserves. -> A root probability output and the single-sweep scalar-energy inequality with positive reserve.
- **step-conformal-bootstrap** [claim](#stmt-lem-entropy-bootstrap) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-entropy-bootstrap): Alternate unboosted sweeps with conformal resets that contract the volume density; sum the coefficient increments and scalar costs. -> A dimensional positive energy coefficient and a retained density below the original density.
- **step-prove-entropy-increment** [claim](#stmt-lem-entropy-increment) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-entropy-increment): Use the inverse-metric trace identity and a coarse coercive region, then recover the sharp trace-square coefficient in the critical region. -> An entropy increment with quadratic scalar loss and retained nonnegative surplus.
- **step-control-surplus** [claim](#stmt-lem-entropy-surplus) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-entropy-surplus): Combine the mean reserve and boost with logarithmic concavity and retained derivative squares. -> Bounds for sweep volume logarithms and, in the small-normalization region, graph curvature and weight derivatives.
- **step-accumulate-entropy-passes** [claim](#stmt-lem-entropy-accumulation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-entropy-accumulation): Telescope the entropy balance along the scheduled step sizes and compare sweep-count and loss sums with integrals. -> Summable total scalar loss, controlled sweep count, and a phase volume-logarithm bound.
- **step-exit-phase** [claim](#stmt-lem-entropy-phase-exit) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-entropy-phase-exit): Retain a small copy of the starting root map, conformally enlarge only where normalization is small, and pay derivative and volume costs from the last surplus. -> A new entropy balance and volume bound with an absolutely small root-map gradient.
- **step-close-entropy-label-control** [claim](#stmt-thm-entropy) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-entropy-phase-exit-2): Use random-priority Borel colorings, support-distance control from the small gradient, graph-square recurrences, and phase targets that outgrow the prescribed elementary bound. -> Entropy metrics with controlled labels and an energy coefficient above the required complexity threshold.
- **step-build-degree-child** [claim](#stmt-lem-degree-descent) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-degree-descent): Construct nested least minimizing boundaries, confine them away from pressure poles, differentiate typical regular slices, and preserve degree through singular-region avoidance and controlled graph modifications. -> A regular child band of unchanged measured degree and a lapse-weighted integral comparison.
- **step-induct-measured-degree** [claim](#stmt-thm-degree) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-degree-descent-2): Induct from the signed-count case, apply the child estimate for each typical slice, and integrate only the measurable parent expression using coarea. -> The measured degree estimate with separate coordinate-gradient penalties.
- **step-build-flat-sheets** [claim](#stmt-lem-transfer-flat) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-transfer-flat): Separate incompatible label witnesses into flat sheets and pull back the cocycle form through cutoff probabilities. -> A flat neighborhood preserving root-map energy and detector pairing with a dimensional Hilbert norm.
- **step-round-cubical-incidence** [claim](#stmt-lem-transfer-rounding) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-transfer-rounding): Encode all local cell coefficients in a finite ordered type vector, choose an integer basis of the joint incidence kernel, and round its coefficients to adjacent lattice values without bias. -> Exactly balanced rational coefficients with a common denominator and controlled rounding error.
- **step-resolve-dual-current** [claim](#stmt-lem-transfer-resolution) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-transfer-resolution): Pair balanced facets, double unmatched ones, use measure-preserving reflection states and signed square-root charts, and construct a smooth tile map with a controlled seam metric. -> A smooth measured resolution representing the original current locally and the doubled diagram globally, with controlled density and frame interpolation.
- **step-identify-comparison-degree** [claim](#stmt-lem-transfer-degree) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-transfer-degree): Verify controlled product charts, remove the frame rotation by a supported homotopy, convolve the original dual current, and use a compatible cubical primitive to identify its expected detector pairing. -> The expected comparison degree equals the cleared fundamental-class detector pairing.
- **step-prove-normalized-volume-bound** [claim](#stmt-thm-scalar-volume) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-transfer-degree-2): Choose distinct tangent and normal lengths, apply measured degree descent, average rotations and grid shifts, and choose the seam collar before fixing the required energy threshold. -> A conditional detector estimate with dimensional constants; the entropy theorem then supplies data satisfying its threshold and gives the normalized scalar-volume bound.
- **step-deduce-integral-bound** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-transfer-degree-3): Apply the conformal transfer to the normalized scalar-volume theorem and take the reciprocal comparison constant. -> The integral scalar-curvature inequality for every metric in the main theorem.

There is a smooth $\\Gamma$-equivariant map $$F:\\widetilde M\\longrightarrow\\ell^2(\\mathcal L),\\qquad\n F_\\ell\\ge0,\\qquad \\sum_\\ell F_\\ell^2=1,$$ with finitely many label orbits and locally finite compact supports for its coordinates. If $p_\\ell=F_\\ell^2$, then the descended form satisfies $$\\int_M p^*\\eta=I.\n  \\label{eq:detector-integral}$$",

        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-graph-identity",
          "exact_quote": "Let $(X,h)$ be a smooth Riemannian manifold, let $\\phi,f$ be smooth real functions, and let $N>0$. Set $$L=(1+N^2|df|_h^2)^{1/2},\\qquad v=L^{-1},\\qquad\n U=\\frac{N\\nabla_h f}{L},\\qquad\n h'=h+N^2df\\otimes df,\\qquad \\phi'=\\phi-\\log L.$$ View $h'$ as the induced metric on the graph $\\Sigma=\\{(x,Nf(x)):x\\in X\\}$ in $(X\\times\\mathbb R,h+dz^2)$, and extend $\\phi$ constantly in the $z$-direction. Orient the graph by $\\nu=(-U,v)$, put $\\mathrm{II}(V,W)=\\langle\\bar\\nabla_V\\nu,W\\rangle$, and let $$H=\\mathop{\\mathrm{tr}}_\\Sigma\\mathrm{II},\\qquad\n D=H+\\partial_\\nu\\phi=-\\mathop{\\mathrm{div}}_\\phi U .$$ Here $D$, and its derivative $U(D)$, are regarded as functions on $X$ through the graph projection. Then $$\\mathcal S(h',\\phi')-\\mathcal S(h,\\phi)\n =D^2-2U(D)+|\\mathrm{II}|_{h'}^2+|d\\log L|_{h'}^2.\n \\label{eq:graph-identity}$$ Moreover, $$e^{\\phi'}dV_{h'}=e^\\phi dV_h,\\qquad\n N^2|df|_{h'}^2=1-v^2=|U|_h^2,\\qquad\n |d\\log L|_{h'}\\le L|U|_h|\\mathrm{II}|_{h'} .\n \\label{eq:graph-basic-bounds}$$",
          "source_id": "PM-335.1"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-weighted-graph-calculation",
      "inputs": [
        "A smooth leafwise metric and weight",
        "A smooth graph function and positive scale"
      ],
      "method": "Combine the weighted Gauss equation with the vertical translation identity and the rank-one determinant and inverse formulas.",
      "output": "The weighted scalar graph identity and exact preservation of weighted density.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-lem-graph-identity"
    },
    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-lem-graph-prescription",
      "depends_on": [
        "step-weighted-graph-calculation"
      ],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-graph-prescription",
          "exact_quote": "Let $(X,h)$ be a complete smooth Riemannian manifold without boundary, with bounded geometry as above, and suppose the positive-order derivatives of $\\phi$ are uniformly bounded. For a graph of height $z$, set $$L_z=(1+|dz|_h^2)^{1/2},\\quad v_z=L_z^{-1},\\quad\n U_z=\\frac{\\nabla_hz}{L_z},\\quad\n D_z=-\\mathop{\\mathrm{div}}_\\phi U_z .$$ Consider either of the following equations $D_z=p(x,z,v_z)$.*\n\n\n1.  *Let $N,\\epsilon,a>0$, $0<\\delta\\le1$, and $H_*\\ge0$. Suppose $F_i,\\beta$ are uniformly smooth, $|F_i|\\le1$, and $0\\le\\beta\\le H_*$. On the region $z>0$, prescribe $$p(x,z,v)=\n       \\frac{\\epsilon a(F_i(x)^2+\\delta^2)+(1-v^2)/2}{z}\n           -(\\epsilon a+\\beta(x))\\frac{z}{N^2}.\n       \\label{eq:graph-prescription-positive}$$*\n\n2.  *Let $e,w>0$ be constants. Suppose $B,f_0$ are uniformly smooth and $B\\ge B_{\\min}>0$. On the region $|(f_0(x)-ez)/w|<\\pi/2$, prescribe $$p(x,z,v)=B(x)\\tan\\bigl((f_0(x)-ez)/w\\bigr).\n       \\label{eq:graph-prescription-tangent}$$*\n\n\n*In either case there is a smooth solution of bounded height with uniform bounds on all its derivatives, and it lies uniformly inside the stated open prescription region. Its angle satisfies $v_z\\ge v_0>0$. In Case <span class=\"upright\">(i)</span> it satisfies $$c_{\\min}:=\\delta\\sqrt{\\frac{\\epsilon a}{\\epsilon a+H_*}}\n       \\ \\le\\ f:=z/N\\ \\le 2.\n \\label{eq:graph-height-positive}$$ In Case <span class=\"upright\">(ii)</span> there is $\\eta>0$ such that $$\\left|\\frac{f_0-ez}{w}\\right|\\le\\frac{\\pi}{2}-\\eta;\n \\qquad\n |ez-f_0|<\\frac{\\pi w}{2},\\quad\n |z|\\le\\frac{\\|f_0\\|_\\infty+\\pi w/2}{e}.$$ The constants $v_0,\\eta$ and the derivative bounds may depend on all the indicated parameters and common bounds of the data. The solution is unique among bounded-height solutions whose $C^{2,\\alpha}$ norms in all buffered charts are uniformly bounded for some $0<\\alpha<1$, and whose values have a uniform margin inside the prescription region. Each such solution may have its own exponent, bound, and margin.*\n\n*The graph metric $h_z=h+dz\\otimes dz$ is again complete and has bounded geometry, and $\\phi_z=\\phi-\\log L_z$ has uniform positive-order derivatives. In particular, $$e^{\\phi_z}dV_{h_z}=e^\\phi dV_h,\\qquad\n |dz|_{h_z}^2=1-v_z^2<1.$$ For the family assertion, let $\\mathcal T$ be a standard Borel parameter space indexing complete problems of a fixed dimension. Assume a common chart radius and buffers, common bounds for the metric, its inverse, and all their chart derivatives, and common bounds for the positive-order derivatives of $\\phi$. Require common bounds, including order zero, for all chart derivatives of $F_i,\\beta$ in Case <span class=\"upright\">(i)</span> or $B,f_0$ in Case <span class=\"upright\">(ii)</span>. In Case <span class=\"upright\">(i)</span>, let $N,\\epsilon,a,\\delta$ range in fixed compact subsets of their permitted positive ranges, and require a common finite upper bound on $H_*$. In Case <span class=\"upright\">(ii)</span>, let $e,w$ range in fixed compact subsets of $(0,\\infty)$, and require $B\\ge B_{\\min}$ for one common $B_{\\min}>0$. Suppose also that the data and chart overlaps are Borel and that the charts accessible along each leaf admit a countable Borel enumeration. Then the preceding estimates for the solutions and their graph metrics and weights hold with constants common to the family, including a common positive height margin in Case <span class=\"upright\">(i)</span> and a common margin from the poles in Case <span class=\"upright\">(ii)</span>. The solutions are Borel in the chart representations and commute with any equivariant identifications of the complete problems.",

        }

          "anchor": "stmt-lem-graph-localization",
          "exact_quote": "Let $X,h,\\phi$ satisfy the hypotheses of Lemma <a href=\"#lem:graph-prescription\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:graph-prescription\">6</a>, with $X$ connected, and let $h_{\\mathrm{ref}}\\le h$ be a complete second metric. Require the coefficients of $h_{\\mathrm{ref}}$, its inverse, and all their coordinate derivatives to have uniform bounds in the same buffered charts used for $h$. Suppose $F_i$ is uniformly smooth, $|F_i|\\le1$, with compact support $K$, and let $\\alpha\\ge0$ be a uniformly smooth function. Fix $$\\epsilon>0,\\qquad N^2=l\\epsilon,\\qquad\n a=\\tfrac12+\\theta,\\qquad 0<l\\le1,\\qquad l\\le\\theta\\le\\tfrac1{16}.$$ There is a radius $R_{\\mathrm{loc}}$, depending only on the dimension and a fixed sufficiently small numerical constant $c_0>0$, with the following property. For every sufficiently large $H_*$, one can choose smooth functions $\\beta_s\\ge0$ and $0\\le\\chi\\le1$ such that, with $\\beta=\\beta_s+\\alpha$, $$0\\le\\beta\\le H_*,\n \\qquad\n N|d\\beta_s|_h\n       \\le c_0\\bigl(\\theta\\epsilon^{3/2}+\\beta_s^{3/2}\\bigr).\n \\label{eq:graph-spatial-cutoff}$$ The function $\\beta_s$ vanishes on a neighborhood of $K$. The function $\\chi$ is one there, is supported in $\\{\\mathop{\\mathrm{dist}}_{h_{\\mathrm{ref}}}(x,K)<R_{\\mathrm{loc}}\\}$, and changes from one to zero only within a region where $F_i=0$ and $\\beta=H_*$. This region and the support of $d\\chi$ have fixed positive buffers in $h_{\\mathrm{ref}}$, independent of $H_*$. The derivatives of $\\chi$ are bounded independently of $H_*$, $\\chi$ is flat on its zero set, and $|d\\chi|_h^2\\le C\\chi$.*\n\n\n\n*Set $\\delta=H_*^{-2}$, and let $\\widetilde f=z/N$ be the complete solution of <a href=\"#eq:graph-prescription-positive\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:graph-prescription-positive\">[eq:graph-prescription-positive]</a> for this $\\beta,\\delta$. Define $f=\\chi\\widetilde f$ and form the graph of height $Nf$. Denote its quantities by $L,U,D,\\mathrm{II}$, put $A=|U|_h$, and set, where $f>0$, $$ND^0=\\epsilon a(F_i^2/f-f)+A^2/(2f).\n \\label{eq:graph-ideal-prescription}$$ The expressions $A^2/f$ and $ND^0$ are extended by zero where $f=0$. The bump $f$ is smooth, $0\\le f\\le2$, is positive on $K$, and is flat on its zero set. On the region $\\chi=1$ it satisfies $$ND=ND^0-\\beta f+\\epsilon a\\delta^2/f .\n \\label{eq:graph-core-prescription}$$ On the region where the cutoff changes, the following quantities tend uniformly to zero as $H_*\\to\\infty$, with the preceding data and parameters fixed: $$\\begin{gathered}\n N^2\\bigl|\\mathcal S(h+N^2df\\otimes df,\\phi-\\log L)\n                      -\\mathcal S(h,\\phi)\\bigr|,\\quad\n A^2/f,\\quad |ND^0|,\\quad \\beta f,\\\\\n |ND|,\\quad N|\\mathrm{II}|,\\quad N|d\\log L|_{h+N^2df\\otimes df}.\n \\end{gathered}\n \\label{eq:graph-cutoff-small}$$ In particular, on all of $X$ the function $\\mathrm{err}:=ND-(ND^0-\\beta f)$ tends uniformly to zero. All data retain the corresponding uniform bounds for each fixed choice of $H_*$, with the positive-order convention for weights. For the family assertion, let a standard Borel parameter space index such problems of a fixed dimension. Require common sizes and buffers for these simultaneous charts, and common bounds there for $h,h^{-1},h_{\\mathrm{ref}},h_{\\mathrm{ref}}^{-1}$ and all their coordinate derivatives. Require common bounds for all derivatives, including values, of $F_i,\\alpha$, and for the positive-order derivatives of $\\phi$. Let $(\\epsilon,l,\\theta)$ range in a fixed compact subset of the permitted parameter set. Require the data, including $h_{\\mathrm{ref}}$, to be Borel in the charts and preserved by the equivariant identifications under consideration. Require also the Borel chart overlaps and countable Borel enumerations of accessible charts in Lemma <a href=\"#lem:graph-prescription\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:graph-prescription\">6</a>. Then the choices are Borel and commute with those identifications; their bounds for each fixed $H_*$, and the limits above as $H_*\\to\\infty$, are uniform over the family. When $K$ is empty one may take $f=0$ and omit the operation.",

          "anchor": "stmt-prop-sweep",
          "exact_quote": "There is a numerical $c>0$ with the following property. For a sweep without boost, set $\\psi=0$. For a boosted sweep, given $\\epsilon_*>0$, there are constants $\\kappa>0$, $c_*>0$, and $C_*<\\infty$, depending only on $\\epsilon_*$, for which one may use $\\psi=\\kappa\\Psi$ whenever $\\epsilon\\ge\\epsilon_*$.*\n\n*Let $h_0,\\phi_0,F$ be a smooth equivariant Borel family on $\\widetilde M\\times\\Omega$, with bounds uniform in the lifted charts for the instance. Suppose $h_0$ is complete, its geometry and the positive-order derivatives of $\\phi_0$ have the uniform bounds above, and $$F=(F_i)_{i\\in\\mathcal L},\\qquad F_i\\ge0,\\qquad\n \\sum_iF_i^2=1$$ has finitely many free label orbits and uniformly locally finite compact coordinate supports. For each orbit representative, suppose all its transverse supports lie in a fixed compact subset of $\\widetilde M$. Let $h_{\\mathrm{ref}}\\le h_0$ be a complete equivariant Borel leafwise metric whose coefficients, inverse, and all coordinate derivatives have uniform bounds in the same lifted buffered charts as $h_0$. Suppose a Borel equivariant coloring $\\chi:\\mathcal L\\times\\Omega\\to\\{1,\\ldots,k\\}$, $k<\\infty$, has been chosen such that, for each retained $\\omega$ and distinct labels $i,j$ with $\\chi(i,\\omega)=\\chi(j,\\omega)$, the $R_{\\mathrm{loc}}$-neighborhoods in $h_{\\mathrm{ref}}(\\omega)$ of $\\mathop{\\mathrm{supp}}F_i(\\cdot,\\omega)$ and $\\mathop{\\mathrm{supp}}F_j(\\cdot,\\omega)$ are disjoint. Fix the pass parameters in <a href=\"#eq:sweep-parameters\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:sweep-parameters\">[eq:sweep-parameters]</a>, with $\\epsilon\\ge\\epsilon_*$ in the boosted case.*\n\n*For any $\\delta_0,\\eta_0>0$, there is a candidate sweep as above with smooth nonnegative bumps $f_i\\le2$, supported in the indicated enlargements, positive on the supports of $F_i$, and flat on their zero sets. Its output is given by <a href=\"#eq:sweep-output\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:sweep-output\">[eq:sweep-output]</a>. The lower bound for $r$ is uniform for the instance. The bumps, coefficients, all intermediate and final graph metrics and weights, their smooth graph quantities, and $Y$ are equivariant and Borel and retain the corresponding uniform chart bounds, with the positive-order convention for weights. The map $Y$ is again a root probability map with the same label orbits.*\n\n\n*For each $i$, the coefficient furnished by Lemma <a href=\"#lem:graph-localization\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:graph-localization\">7</a> has the form $$\\beta_i=\\beta_{s,i}+\\epsilon\\psi(R_i),\\qquad\n R_i=\\sum_{j<i}f_j^2 ,\n \\label{eq:sweep-beta}$$ Here $\\beta_{s,i}$ vanishes near $\\mathop{\\mathrm{supp}}F_i$, and the coefficients satisfy <a href=\"#eq:graph-spatial-cutoff\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:graph-spatial-cutoff\">[eq:graph-spatial-cutoff]</a> in $h_{i-1}$. Writing $$ND_i^0=\\epsilon a(F_i^2/f_i-f_i)+A_i^2/(2f_i)$$ on $\\{f_i>0\\}$, extended by zero off the bump, one has $$ND_i=ND_i^0-\\beta_i f_i+\\mathrm{err}_i,\\qquad\n \\sum_i|\\mathrm{err}_i|^2\\le\\eta_0 .\n \\label{eq:sweep-prescription-error}$$*\n\n\n\n*Define the nonnegative reserve $$\\begin{split}\n \\Pi_p:={}&\n T_0+c\\epsilon\\bigl(M_0^2+\\mathbb E_\\nu V_i^2+\\theta\\mathop{\\mathrm{Var}}_\\nu u\\bigr)\n       \\\\\n &+c\\frac r\\epsilon\\mathcal X\n       +c_*\\epsilon r^6\\mathbf 1_{\\{r\\ge C_*\\}},\n \\end{split}\n \\label{eq:sweep-positive-reserve}$$ where the final term is omitted in a sweep without boost. Then, pointwise, $$\\begin{aligned}\n Q:=\\frac{rN^2}{\\epsilon}\\bigl[\n &\\mathcal S(h_+,\\phi_+)-\\mathcal S(h_0,\\phi_0)\n       +\\epsilon e_{h_0}(F)-\\epsilon e_{h_+}(Y)\\bigr]\n \\notag\\\\\n &\\ge\\Pi_p-4\\theta^2m-\\frac r\\epsilon\\delta_0 .\n \\label{eq:sweep-inequality}\n\\end{aligned}$$ The numerical constant $c$ is independent of the instance, the number of colors, and the pass parameters. The errors $\\delta_0,\\eta_0$ can be prescribed independently before the pass.",
