          "anchor": "stmt-lem-entropy-bootstrap",
          "exact_quote": "There are constants $\\epsilon_*>0$ and $C_{\\mathrm{boot}}<4$, depending only on $n$, with the following property. Starting with the input of Theorem <a href=\"#thm:entropy\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"thm:entropy\">9</a>, finitely many sweeps without boost, followed individually by conformal changes, produce a metric $h_*$, a root probability map $F_*$, and $\\epsilon_0\\in[\\epsilon_*,2\\epsilon_*]$ such that $$\\label{eq:entropy-bootstrap-output}\n \\rho:=dV_{h_*}\\leq dV_g,\\qquad\n \\mathop{\\mathrm{Scal}}_{h_*}\\geq-C_{\\mathrm{boot}}+\\epsilon_0e_{h_*}(F_*).$$ The metric and map have the uniformity and support properties in Theorem <a href=\"#thm:entropy\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"thm:entropy\">9</a>. The number of sweeps may depend on the input.",
          "source_id": "PM-335.1"
        }

      "method": "Alternate unboosted sweeps with conformal resets that contract the volume density; sum the coefficient increments and scalar costs.",

    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-lem-entropy-increment",
      "depends_on": [
        "step-sweep-probability-coordinates",

      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-entropy-increment",
          "exact_quote": "There are dimensional constants $m_0>0$, $D_*\\geq1$, $l_0>0$, $\\gamma_n>0$, and $C_n<\\infty$ with the following property. For $\\epsilon\\geq\\epsilon_*$, take a boosted sweep with $$0<l\\leq l_0,\\qquad \\theta=D_*l\\leq\\tfrac1{16},\n \\qquad N^2=l\\epsilon.$$ The errors can be chosen so that $$\\label{eq:entropy-increment}\n\\begin{split}\n \\mathcal S(h_+,\\phi_+)-(1+l)\\epsilon e_{h_+}(Y)\n \\geq{}&\\mathcal S(h_0,\\phi_0)-\\epsilon e_{h_0}(F)\\\\\n &+2W_p-n\\log(1+l)-C_nl^2+P_p ,\n\\end{split}$$ where the nonnegative, pointwise surplus is $$\\label{eq:entropy-surplus-definition}\n P_p=\\gamma_n\\frac{\\epsilon}{rN^2}\\Pi_p\\,\n \\mathbf 1_{\\{m\\geq m_0\\}\\,\\cup\\,\\{|1-r|\\geq1/4\\}}.$$ In particular, on the indicated region $P_p$ retains a fixed fraction of every positive term in the sweep inequality.",
          "source_id": "PM-335.1"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-prove-entropy-increment",
      "inputs": [
        "Boosted sweep reserve",
        "The volume logarithm",
        "Earlier- and later-color inverse-metric telescopes"

      "method": "Use the inverse-metric trace identity and a coarse coercive region, then recover the sharp trace-square coefficient in the critical region.",
      "output": "An entropy increment with quadratic scalar loss and retained nonnegative surplus.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-lem-entropy-increment"
    },

      "depends_on": [

      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-entropy-surplus",
          "exact_quote": "For every sweep of Lemma <a href=\"#lem:entropy-increment\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:entropy-increment\">11</a>, $$\\label{eq:entropy-pass-volume}\n W_p\\leq C_n\\epsilon^{1/4}(\\epsilon+P_p)^{3/4}.$$ There is also a dimensional $\\epsilon_{\\mathrm{exit}}$ such that, if $\\epsilon\\geq\\epsilon_{\\mathrm{exit}}$ and $r\\leq1/4$, then $$\\label{eq:entropy-last-surplus}\n\\begin{split}\n P_p&\\geq c_n\\frac{\\epsilon}{lr}\n                       \\left(1+\\frac{m^2}{\\epsilon^2}\\right),\\\\\n \\sum_i\\bigl(D_i^2+|\\mathrm{II}_i|^2+|d\\log L_i|_{h_i}^2\\bigr)\n       &\\leq C_n\\frac{P_p}{\\theta}.\n\\end{split}$$ The errors in the prescription may simultaneously be chosen to have $\\sum_i|\\mathrm{err}_i|^2\\leq1$.",
          "source_id": "PM-335.1"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-control-surplus",
      "inputs": [
        "The entropy increment surplus",
        "The graph stretching factors",

      ],
      "method": "Combine the mean reserve and boost with logarithmic concavity and retained derivative squares.",
      "output": "Bounds for sweep volume logarithms and, in the small-normalization region, graph curvature and weight derivatives.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-lem-entropy-surplus"

    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-lem-entropy-accumulation",
      "depends_on": [
        "step-prove-entropy-increment",
        "step-control-surplus"
      ],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-entropy-accumulation",
          "exact_quote": "Suppose at parameter $\\epsilon_{\\mathrm{in}}\\geq\\epsilon_*$ that $$\\mathcal S(h,\\phi)\\geq-C+2W-n\\log\\epsilon_{\\mathrm{in}}\n                  +\\epsilon_{\\mathrm{in}}e_h(F)+P_{\\mathrm{in}},\n \\qquad W=\\log(dV_h/\\rho)\\geq0,\\quad P_{\\mathrm{in}}\\geq0.$$ Successive sweeps of Lemma <a href=\"#lem:entropy-increment\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:entropy-increment\">11</a>, with $\\epsilon_{\\mathrm{new}}=(1+l(\\epsilon))\\epsilon$, preserve this form of inequality: at the final parameter $E$ the surplus is $P=P_{\\mathrm{in}}+\\sum_pP_p$, the volume logarithm is $W_{\\mathrm{new}}=W+\\sum_pW_p$, and the increase of the scalar constant is $C_n\\sum_p l(\\epsilon_p)^2$. Along the entire increasing sequence of parameters, $$\\label{eq:entropy-summability}\n \\sum_p l(\\epsilon_p)^2\\leq C_n,\\qquad\n \\#\\{p:\\epsilon_p<E\\}\\leq C_n(1+\\log^3(1+E)).$$ If the initial volume logarithm is zero, then, for all sufficiently large dimensional $E$, $$\\label{eq:entropy-phase-volume}\n W_{\\mathrm{new}}\\leq E^{0.26}(E+P)^{0.75}.$$ The same assertion holds if the initial data have parameter $E_0$, surplus $P_0$, and $$W\\leq E_0^{0.32}(E_0+P_0)^{0.75},$$ provided $E\\geq E_0$ is sufficiently large dimensionally and $E_0^{0.32}\\leq\\tfrac12E^{0.26}$.",
          "source_id": "PM-335.1"
        }

      "id": "step-accumulate-entropy-passes",
      "inputs": [
        "The entropy increment",
        "The prescribed parameter schedule",
        "The surplus volume bound"

      "method": "Telescope the entropy balance along the scheduled step sizes and compare sweep-count and loss sums with integrals.",
      "output": "Summable total scalar loss, controlled sweep count, and a phase volume-logarithm bound.",

    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-lem-entropy-phase-exit",
      "depends_on": [
        "step-accumulate-entropy-passes",

      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-entropy-phase-exit",
          "exact_quote": "Consider a phase starting with root map $Y^0$ and metric $h^0$, and let $K_0$ be a finite uniform bound for $e_{h^0}(Y^0)^{1/2}$. Suppose its last sweep has input parameter $\\epsilon$, output parameter $E=(1+l(\\epsilon))\\epsilon$, and output $(h_+,\\phi_+,Y)$. Suppose, with $C\\geq0$ and $P\\geq P_p$ including the surplus from that last sweep, that $$\\label{eq:entropy-before-exit}\n\\begin{split}\n \\mathcal S(h_+,\\phi_+)&\\geq-C+2W-n\\log E+E e_{h_+}(Y)+P,\\\\\n W&=\\log(dV_{h_+}/\\rho)\\geq0,\\qquad\n W\\leq E^{0.26}(E+P)^{0.75}.\n\\end{split}$$ If $E$ is sufficiently large in terms of $n,K_0$, there are a metric $\\widehat h$, a weight $\\widehat\\phi$, and a root map $Z$ on two copies of the labels such that $$\\label{eq:entropy-after-exit}\n\\begin{split}\n \\mathcal S(\\widehat h,\\widehat\\phi)\n &\\geq-(C+C_n)+2\\widehat W-n\\log E\n                      +E e_{\\widehat h}(Z)+P',\\\\\n \\widehat W&=\\log(dV_{\\widehat h}/\\rho)\\geq0,\\qquad\n \\widehat W\\leq E^{0.32}(E+P')^{0.75},\\\\\n e_{\\widehat h}(Z)^{1/2}&\\leq E^{-1/100}.\n\\end{split}$$ Here $P'\\geq0$, and the added scalar cost $C_n$ is independent of $K_0$ and of all other instance constants. The changes preserve the uniform smoothness and support properties of Theorem <a href=\"#thm:entropy\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"thm:entropy\">9</a>.",
          "source_id": "PM-335.1"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-exit-phase",
      "inputs": [
        "The phase scalar-energy balance",
        "A uniform starting gradient bound",
        "The small-normalization surplus estimates"
      ],
      "method": "Retain a small copy of the starting root map, conformally enlarge only where normalization is small, and pay derivative and volume costs from the last surplus.",
      "output": "A new entropy balance and volume bound with an absolutely small root-map gradient.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-lem-entropy-phase-exit"
    },
    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-thm-entropy",
      "depends_on": [
        "step-conformal-bootstrap",
        "step-accumulate-entropy-passes",
        "step-exit-phase"
      ],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-thm-entropy",
          "exact_quote": "Let $(M^n,g)$ be a closed connected Riemannian manifold, where $n\\geq3$ and $\\mathop{\\mathrm{Scal}}_g=-1$, and put $\\Gamma=\\pi_1(M)$. Let $(\\Omega_0,\\mu_0)$ be a standard Borel probability space with a measure-preserving $\\Gamma$-action; the one-point space is allowed. Suppose $\\mathcal L_0$ is a countable set with finitely many free $\\Gamma$-orbits and $$F^{\\mathrm{in}}:\\widetilde M\\times\\Omega_0\\longrightarrow\n       \\ell^2(\\mathcal L_0),\\qquad\n F^{\\mathrm{in}}_\\ell\\geq0,\\qquad\n \\sum_{\\ell\\in\\mathcal L_0}(F^{\\mathrm{in}}_\\ell)^2=1$$ is equivariant, Borel in the transverse variable, and smooth along $\\widetilde M$. Assume the following uniformity for this input. In charts lifted from a fixed finite atlas on $M$, with fixed inner buffers, the supports are locally finite with a uniform bound on their number, and all leafwise derivatives of the coordinate functions have uniform bounds. For each orbit representative $\\ell$ there is a compact set $K_\\ell\\subset\\widetilde M$ such that $\\mathop{\\mathrm{supp}}F^{\\mathrm{in}}_{\\gamma\\ell}(\\,\\cdot\\,,\\omega)\n\\subset\\gamma K_\\ell$ for all $\\gamma\\in\\Gamma$ and almost every $\\omega$. The bounds and compact sets in these assumptions may depend on the input.*\n\n\n*For every prescribed elementary function $\\mathcal B$ and every finite number $E_{\\min}$, there are a standard Borel probability $\\Gamma$-space $(\\Omega,\\mu)$ extending $(\\Omega_0,\\mu_0)$, a countable set $\\mathcal L$ with finitely many free $\\Gamma$-orbits, and equivariant leafwise data on $\\widetilde M\\times\\Omega$: a metric $h$, a function $\\phi$, a positive density $\\rho$, and a root probability map $F$ with labels $\\mathcal L$. There are also $E>E_{\\min}$, an integer $q\\geq n+1$, and a constant $C=C(n,\\mathcal B)<\\infty$ such that, on an invariant conull set of transverse parameters, $$\\label{eq:entropy-contract}\n\\begin{split}\n &\\rho\\leq dV_g,\\qquad\n W:=\\log\\frac{dV_h}{\\rho}\\geq0,\\\\\n &\\mathcal S(h,\\phi)\\geq\n       -C+2\\left(W-\\frac n2\\log E\\right)+E e_h(F),\n       \\qquad e_h(F)^{1/2}\\leq E^{-1/100},\\\\\n &E>\\mathcal B(q).\n\\end{split}$$ Here the extension means that there is an equivariant measure-preserving map $\\Omega\\to\\Omega_0$. All output objects are Borel transversely and smooth along leaves. In the same lifted charts, the coefficients of $h$, their derivatives of every order, and the coefficients of $h^{-1}$ have uniform bounds; the function $\\phi$, the density ratio $\\rho/dV_g$, its reciprocal, and their derivatives of every order have uniform bounds. The coordinates of $F$ have the same derivative and uniform local finiteness properties as the input. There are equivariant compact sets containing their supports, uniformly in the transverse parameter. All these output bounds may depend on the input and on the chosen finite construction.*\n\n*There is, in addition, a fixed $\\Gamma$-invariant graph $\\mathcal G$ on $\\mathcal L$, independent of the transverse parameter, of finite maximum degree $\\Delta$. It contains an edge between every pair of distinct labels that are simultaneously nonzero at any point of any leaf. We may take $$q\\geq \\max\\{n+1,\\#(\\Gamma\\backslash\\mathcal L),\\Delta+1\\}$$ and a Borel equivariant coloring $\\chi:\\mathcal L\\times\\Omega\\to\\{1,\\ldots,q\\}$ such that labels of graph distance one or two have different colors. The set $\\mathcal L$ can be taken to consist of finitely many copies of $\\mathcal L_0$. The constant $C$ is independent of the manifold, metric, input family, all the uniform bounds just specified, and $E_{\\min}$.",
          "source_id": "PM-335.1"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-close-entropy-label-control",
      "inputs": [

      "method": "Use random-priority Borel colorings, support-distance control from the small gradient, graph-square recurrences, and phase targets that outgrow the prescribed elementary bound.",

      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-lem-entropy-phase-exit-2"
    },
    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-lem-degree-descent",
      "depends_on": [
        "step-complete-graph-solves",

      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-degree-descent",

Transverse null subsets and their saturations may be removed for each such $s$.*\n\n\n1.  *For every nonnegative Borel function $F$ supported in $\\{|G|_\\infty<b_d\\}$, $$\\label{eq:degree-coarea}\n     \\int_{-\\tau}^{\\tau}\\!ds\\int_{\\Sigma_s}v_sF\\,dA_h\n                           \\le\\int_XF\\,dV_h.$$*\n\n\n2.  *For each such $s$ there is a controlled measured $p$-band $(X_s^+,h_s^+,\\phi_s^+,J_s^+)$ over $Q_{T_p}^p$ of degree $\\mathcal D_X(G)$. <a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **invariant-integral-topological-bound**: The integral of the critical power of negative scalar curvature bounds real simplicial volume from below with a positive constant depending only on dimension.
- **invariant-weighted-graph-density**: A graph deformation preserves the weighted volume density while its weighted scalar curvature changes according to the graph identity.
- **invariant-entropy-label-control**: The entropy construction retains a density bounded by the original volume density, makes the root probability gradient small, and produces an energy coefficient exceeding the prescribed elementary bound on label complexity.
- **invariant-measured-degree-control**: The measured degree estimate bounds absolute degree by an integral involving weighted scalar curvature and separate coordinate gradient penalties.
- **invariant-balanced-unbiased-rounding**: The common rounded coefficient vector preserves every required cocycle equation exactly and preserves the original cell coefficients in conditional expectation.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-discrete-curvature-substitution**: Apply the scalar-curvature inequality directly to a discrete dependency network or mesh using an arbitrary graph curvature score. -> A graph score does not supply the smooth Riemannian scalar curvature, volume density, or oriented fundamental class required by the theorem.
- **anti-global-bound-on-open-domain**: Use the global integral inequality unchanged on an open domain or a manifold with boundary. -> The stated global estimate does not cover that setting, and the controlled-band degree theorem does not remove the closed-manifold hypothesis of the main result.
- **anti-positive-obstruction-from-vanishing-volume**: Infer a strictly positive negative-curvature requirement for a manifold admitting nonnegative scalar curvature. -> The vanishing input makes its real simplicial volume zero, so the main inequality supplies no strictly positive lower bound from that invariant.
- **anti-incomplete-prescribed-graph-solve**: Invoke the prescribed-graph existence and uniqueness result on an arbitrary incomplete region or without common geometric bounds. -> The existence, comparison, and measurable-dependence arguments require complete problems with the stated bounded geometry and uniform data control.
- **anti-coordinatewise-independent-rounding**: Round each cubical coefficient independently and use the result as a balanced dual chain. -> Independent coefficient rounding can destroy the incidence equations; unbiasedness alone does not ensure exact boundary balance.
- **anti-degree-from-uncontrolled-band**: Apply the measured degree bound to a smooth map into a cube based solely on its coordinate gradients. -> Coordinate gradient bounds alone do not establish the chart, transverse integration, or strict horizon conditions needed for degree descent.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `not_applicable`. The global scalar-curvature inequality and its entropy and measured-degree constructions concern smooth manifolds, elliptic equations, and measured families rather than a finite input-output algorithm. The prescribed-graph proof states: “A diagonal local limit solves the equation on the component with the uniform bound.” The degree construction additionally uses exhaustion limits of perimeter minimizers. Finite incidence rounding is an internal construction whose inputs depend on these continuous geometric data; testing a finite rounding instance would not certify the paper's geometric conclusions.

<a id="pm-s5"></a>
## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| software | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The global curvature estimate concerns smooth Riemannian manifolds and supplies no executable procedure for computing simplicial volume from a software representation. |
| games_simulation | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The conformal reduction uses an attained Yamabe minimum on a closed manifold. It supplies no discretisation, simulation update rule, or error control for a geometric game model. |
| tools | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The detector requires a bounded group cocycle and an equivariant smooth probability map. Its existence does not provide a general tool for extracting those inputs from an arbitrary manifold description. |
| algorithms | True | moderate | adaptable | inferred | The unbiased rounding proof gives a finite construction inside an integer incidence kernel. For a supplied rational finite-type vector, its basis decomposition and adjacent lattice choices can be implemented with exact arithmetic. This restricted construction preserves the supplied balance relations; it does not compute the geometric inputs of the full theorem. |
| data_structures | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The flat sheet neighborhood distinguishes label configurations through smooth ball charts and their identifications. The statement establishes a geometric representation, but gives no finite storage or query contract for the entire countable sheet family. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The graph identity relates smooth derivatives, induced metrics, and second fundamental forms. It contains no arithmetic workload, precision contract, or hardware execution model from which an accelerator implementation follows. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The measured resolution uses invariant probability laws to preserve oriented integration through geometric gluing. Those probability choices establish no secrecy, unpredictability, or adversarial security property. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The root probability map is deformed to satisfy a weighted scalar inequality with controlled supports and gradients. The entropy theorem supplies no training objective, statistical model, or learning guarantee. |
| optimisation_operations_research | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The prescribed graph construction proves existence and uniqueness through analytic estimates and continuation on complete manifolds. It gives no finite optimisation solver, stopping certificate, or computational complexity for those solutions. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The sweep uses an equivariant Borel coloring to separate geometric supports. Its hypotheses do not specify communicating processes, message delivery, faults, or a distributed coordination protocol. |
| other | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The measured degree estimate is a foundational geometric bound requiring controlled bands and strict gradient and horizon conditions. Its general measured and smooth inputs do not constitute a finite engineering interface. |

          "exact_quote": "Put $\\delta=(1000\\sqrt q)^{-1}$.           "anchor": "stmt-lem-graph-identity",
          "exact_quote": "Let $(X,h)$ be a smooth Riemannian manifold, let $\\phi,f$ be smooth real functions, and let $N>0$. Set $$L=(1+N^2|df|_h^2)^{1/2},\\qquad v=L^{-1},\\qquad\n U=\\frac{N\\nabla_h f}{L},\\qquad\n h'=h+N^2df\\otimes df,\\qquad \\phi'=\\phi-\\log L.$$ View $h'$ as the induced metric on the graph $\\Sigma=\\{(x,Nf(x)):x\\in X\\}$ in $(X\\times\\mathbb R,h+dz^2)$, and extend $\\phi$ constantly in the $z$-direction. Orient the graph by $\\nu=(-U,v)$, put $\\mathrm{II}(V,W)=\\langle\\bar\\nabla_V\\nu,W\\rangle$, and let $$H=\\mathop{\\mathrm{tr}}_\\Sigma\\mathrm{II},\\qquad\n D=H+\\partial_\\nu\\phi=-\\mathop{\\mathrm{div}}_\\phi U .$$ Here $D$, and its derivative $U(D)$, are regarded as functions on $X$ through the graph projection. Then $$\\mathcal S(h',\\phi')-\\mathcal S(h,\\phi)\n =D^2-2U(D)+|\\mathrm{II}|_{h'}^2+|d\\log L|_{h'}^2.\n \\label{eq:graph-identity}$$ Moreover, $$e^{\\phi'}dV_{h'}=e^\\phi dV_h,\\qquad\n N^2|df|_{h'}^2=1-v^2=|U|_h^2,\\qquad\n |d\\log L|_{h'}\\le L|U|_h|\\mathrm{II}|_{h'} .\n \\label{eq:graph-basic-bounds}$$",
          "source_object": "stmt-lem-graph-identity"
        }
      ],

          "anchor": "stmt-lem-transfer-resolution",
          "exact_quote": "For the diagram $\\mathcal P$ there is a standard Borel measured family $\\mathcal A$ of oriented smooth $m$-manifolds and a smooth map $X:\\mathcal A\\to\\mathcal U_\\omega$, equivariant and Borel in all the data. The integration identities below are for fixed grid, entropy, and rounding data, including $(V,s,\\omega,\\xi)$; their integrals use the conditional state measure and depend Borelly on those data. The points over $\\mathcal T_\\delta$ represented by original chamber tiles form a Borel leaf-open subset $\\mathcal A_{\\rm orig}\\subset X^{-1}(\\mathcal T_\\delta)$. For every compactly supported smooth $m$-form $\\alpha$ on $\\mathcal T_\\delta$, $$\\label{eq:transfer-local-push}\n                 \\int_{\\mathcal A_{\\rm orig}}X^*\\alpha=Z_{\\rm rd}(\\alpha).$$ For the full doubled diagram, measured oriented integration also gives $$\\label{eq:transfer-push}\n                 X_*[\\mathcal A]=[\\mathcal P].$$ For a parameter $0<\\lambda<1/2$ and a choice $$\\label{eq:transfer-gamma}\n  0<2\\gamma_0<(m+1)^{-(m+1)}\\lambda^{m(m+1)},$$ there are a smooth metric $k$ and a closed seam set in each tile such that $\\|dX\\|_k\\leq1$, and $k=X^*k_{\\rm flat}$ off the seam. There are buffered smooth charts with elementary overlap, metric, inverse-metric, and fixed-order derivative bounds whenever $\\lambda^{-1}$ and $\\gamma_0^{-1}$ are elementary. In the sorted charts for each tile, the seam is contained in the union of the slabs $|Z_j|\\leq\\lambda$, $1\\leq j\\leq m$. The $k$-volume density in those charts has an elementary upper bound chosen before $\\lambda,\\gamma_0$; in particular this bound does not depend on these two parameters.*\n\n*One may prescribe for each chamber over a dual cube a rotation $O_\\sigma\\in SO(q)$. There is also a smooth Borel equivariant $\\mathcal R:\\mathcal A\\to SO(q)$ equal to $O_\\sigma$ off the seam in that tile, equal to the identity near all tile facets, and homotopic to the identity. Its fixed-order chart derivatives and $\\|d\\mathcal R\\|_k$ are elementarily bounded under the same hypotheses.",

        }

          "anchor": "stmt-thm-entropy",
          "exact_quote": "Let $(M^n,g)$ be a closed connected Riemannian manifold, where $n\\geq3$ and $\\mathop{\\mathrm{Scal}}_g=-1$, and put $\\Gamma=\\pi_1(M)$. Let $(\\Omega_0,\\mu_0)$ be a standard Borel probability space with a measure-preserving $\\Gamma$-action; the one-point space is allowed. Suppose $\\mathcal L_0$ is a countable set with finitely many free $\\Gamma$-orbits and $$F^{\\mathrm{in}}:\\widetilde M\\times\\Omega_0\\longrightarrow\n       \\ell^2(\\mathcal L_0),\\qquad\n F^{\\mathrm{in}}_\\ell\\geq0,\\qquad\n \\sum_{\\ell\\in\\mathcal L_0}(F^{\\mathrm{in}}_\\ell)^2=1$$ is equivariant, Borel in the transverse variable, and smooth along $\\widetilde M$. Assume the following uniformity for this input. In charts lifted from a fixed finite atlas on $M$, with fixed inner buffers, the supports are locally finite with a uniform bound on their number, and all leafwise derivatives of the coordinate functions have uniform bounds. For each orbit representative $\\ell$ there is a compact set $K_\\ell\\subset\\widetilde M$ such that $\\mathop{\\mathrm{supp}}F^{\\mathrm{in}}_{\\gamma\\ell}(\\,\\cdot\\,,\\omega)\n\\subset\\gamma K_\\ell$ for all $\\gamma\\in\\Gamma$ and almost every $\\omega$. The bounds and compact sets in these assumptions may depend on the input.*\n\n\n*For every prescribed elementary function $\\mathcal B$ and every finite number $E_{\\min}$, there are a standard Borel probability $\\Gamma$-space $(\\Omega,\\mu)$ extending $(\\Omega_0,\\mu_0)$, a countable set $\\mathcal L$ with finitely many free $\\Gamma$-orbits, and equivariant leafwise data on $\\widetilde M\\times\\Omega$: a metric $h$, a function $\\phi$, a positive density $\\rho$, and a root probability map $F$ with labels $\\mathcal L$. There are also $E>E_{\\min}$, an integer $q\\geq n+1$, and a constant $C=C(n,\\mathcal B)<\\infty$ such that, on an invariant conull set of transverse parameters, $$\\label{eq:entropy-contract}\n\\begin{split}\n &\\rho\\leq dV_g,\\qquad\n W:=\\log\\frac{dV_h}{\\rho}\\geq0,\\\\\n &\\mathcal S(h,\\phi)\\geq\n       -C+2\\left(W-\\frac n2\\log E\\right)+E e_h(F),\n       \\qquad e_h(F)^{1/2}\\leq E^{-1/100},\\\\\n &E>\\mathcal B(q).\n\\end{split}$$ Here the extension means that there is an equivariant measure-preserving map $\\Omega\\to\\Omega_0$. All output objects are Borel transversely and smooth along leaves. In the same lifted charts, the coefficients of $h$, their derivatives of every order, and the coefficients of $h^{-1}$ have uniform bounds; the function $\\phi$, the density ratio $\\rho/dV_g$, its reciprocal, and their derivatives of every order have uniform bounds. The coordinates of $F$ have the same derivative and uniform local finiteness properties as the input. There are equivariant compact sets containing their supports, uniformly in the transverse parameter. All these output bounds may depend on the input and on the chosen finite construction.*\n\n*There is, in addition, a fixed $\\Gamma$-invariant graph $\\mathcal G$ on $\\mathcal L$, independent of the transverse parameter, of finite maximum degree $\\Delta$. It contains an edge between every pair of distinct labels that are simultaneously nonzero at any point of any leaf. We may take $$q\\geq \\max\\{n+1,\\#(\\Gamma\\backslash\\mathcal L),\\Delta+1\\}$$ and a Borel equivariant coloring $\\chi:\\mathcal L\\times\\Omega\\to\\{1,\\ldots,q\\}$ such that labels of graph distance one or two have different colors. The set $\\mathcal L$ can be taken to consist of finitely many copies of $\\mathcal L_0$. The constant $C$ is independent of the manifold, metric, input family, all the uniform bounds just specified, and $E_{\\min}$.",

        }

          "anchor": "stmt-lem-graph-prescription",
          "exact_quote": "Let $(X,h)$ be a complete smooth Riemannian manifold without boundary, with bounded geometry as above, and suppose the positive-order derivatives of $\\phi$ are uniformly bounded. For a graph of height $z$, set $$L_z=(1+|dz|_h^2)^{1/2},\\quad v_z=L_z^{-1},\\quad\n U_z=\\frac{\\nabla_hz}{L_z},\\quad\n D_z=-\\mathop{\\mathrm{div}}_\\phi U_z .$$ Consider either of the following equations $D_z=p(x,z,v_z)$.*\n\n\n1.  *Let $N,\\epsilon,a>0$, $0<\\delta\\le1$, and $H_*\\ge0$. Suppose $F_i,\\beta$ are uniformly smooth, $|F_i|\\le1$, and $0\\le\\beta\\le H_*$. On the region $z>0$, prescribe $$p(x,z,v)=\n       \\frac{\\epsilon a(F_i(x)^2+\\delta^2)+(1-v^2)/2}{z}\n           -(\\epsilon a+\\beta(x))\\frac{z}{N^2}.\n       \\label{eq:graph-prescription-positive}$$*\n\n2.  *Let $e,w>0$ be constants. Suppose $B,f_0$ are uniformly smooth and $B\\ge B_{\\min}>0$. On the region $|(f_0(x)-ez)/w|<\\pi/2$, prescribe $$p(x,z,v)=B(x)\\tan\\bigl((f_0(x)-ez)/w\\bigr).\n       \\label{eq:graph-prescription-tangent}$$*\n\n\n*In either case there is a smooth solution of bounded height with uniform bounds on all its derivatives, and it lies uniformly inside the stated open prescription region. Its angle satisfies $v_z\\ge v_0>0$. In Case <span class=\"upright\">(i)</span> it satisfies $$c_{\\min}:=\\delta\\sqrt{\\frac{\\epsilon a}{\\epsilon a+H_*}}\n       \\ \\le\\ f:=z/N\\ \\le 2.\n \\label{eq:graph-height-positive}$$ In Case <span class=\"upright\">(ii)</span> there is $\\eta>0$ such that $$\\left|\\frac{f_0-ez}{w}\\right|\\le\\frac{\\pi}{2}-\\eta;\n \\qquad\n |ez-f_0|<\\frac{\\pi w}{2},\\quad\n |z|\\le\\frac{\\|f_0\\|_\\infty+\\pi w/2}{e}.$$ The constants $v_0,\\eta$ and the derivative bounds may depend on all the indicated parameters and common bounds of the data. The solution is unique among bounded-height solutions whose $C^{2,\\alpha}$ norms in all buffered charts are uniformly bounded for some $0<\\alpha<1$, and whose values have a uniform margin inside the prescription region. Each such solution may have its own exponent, bound, and margin.*\n\n*The graph metric $h_z=h+dz\\otimes dz$ is again complete and has bounded geometry, and $\\phi_z=\\phi-\\log L_z$ has uniform positive-order derivatives. In particular, $$e^{\\phi_z}dV_{h_z}=e^\\phi dV_h,\\qquad\n |dz|_{h_z}^2=1-v_z^2<1.$$ For the family assertion, let $\\mathcal T$ be a standard Borel parameter space indexing complete problems of a fixed dimension. Assume a common chart radius and buffers, common bounds for the metric, its inverse, and all their chart derivatives, and common bounds for the positive-order derivatives of $\\phi$. Require common bounds, including order zero, for all chart derivatives of $F_i,\\beta$ in Case <span class=\"upright\">(i)</span> or $B,f_0$ in Case <span class=\"upright\">(ii)</span>. In Case <span class=\"upright\">(i)</span>, let $N,\\epsilon,a,\\delta$ range in fixed compact subsets of their permitted positive ranges, and require a common finite upper bound on $H_*$. In Case <span class=\"upright\">(ii)</span>, let $e,w$ range in fixed compact subsets of $(0,\\infty)$, and require $B\\ge B_{\\min}$ for one common $B_{\\min}>0$. Suppose also that the data and chart overlaps are Borel and that the charts accessible along each leaf admit a countable Borel enumeration. Then the preceding estimates for the solutions and their graph metrics and weights hold with constants common to the family, including a common positive height margin in Case <span class=\"upright\">(i)</span> and a common margin from the poles in Case <span class=\"upright\">(ii)</span>. The solutions are Borel in the chart representations and commute with any equivariant identifications of the complete problems.",

        }

          "anchor": "stmt-prop-sweep",
          "exact_quote": "There is a numerical $c>0$ with the following property. For a sweep without boost, set $\\psi=0$. For a boosted sweep, given $\\epsilon_*>0$, there are constants $\\kappa>0$, $c_*>0$, and $C_*<\\infty$, depending only on $\\epsilon_*$, for which one may use $\\psi=\\kappa\\Psi$ whenever $\\epsilon\\ge\\epsilon_*$.*\n\n*Let $h_0,\\phi_0,F$ be a smooth equivariant Borel family on $\\widetilde M\\times\\Omega$, with bounds uniform in the lifted charts for the instance. Suppose $h_0$ is complete, its geometry and the positive-order derivatives of $\\phi_0$ have the uniform bounds above, and $$F=(F_i)_{i\\in\\mathcal L},\\qquad F_i\\ge0,\\qquad\n \\sum_iF_i^2=1$$ has finitely many free label orbits and uniformly locally finite compact coordinate supports. For each orbit representative, suppose all its transverse supports lie in a fixed compact subset of $\\widetilde M$. Let $h_{\\mathrm{ref}}\\le h_0$ be a complete equivariant Borel leafwise metric whose coefficients, inverse, and all coordinate derivatives have uniform bounds in the same lifted buffered charts as $h_0$. Suppose a Borel equivariant coloring $\\chi:\\mathcal L\\times\\Omega\\to\\{1,\\ldots,k\\}$, $k<\\infty$, has been chosen such that, for each retained $\\omega$ and distinct labels $i,j$ with $\\chi(i,\\omega)=\\chi(j,\\omega)$, the $R_{\\mathrm{loc}}$-neighborhoods in $h_{\\mathrm{ref}}(\\omega)$ of $\\mathop{\\mathrm{supp}}F_i(\\cdot,\\omega)$ and $\\mathop{\\mathrm{supp}}F_j(\\cdot,\\omega)$ are disjoint. Fix the pass parameters in <a href=\"#eq:sweep-parameters\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:sweep-parameters\">[eq:sweep-parameters]</a>, with $\\epsilon\\ge\\epsilon_*$ in the boosted case.*\n\n*For any $\\delta_0,\\eta_0>0$, there is a candidate sweep as above with smooth nonnegative bumps $f_i\\le2$, supported in the indicated enlargements, positive on the supports of $F_i$, and flat on their zero sets. Its output is given by <a href=\"#eq:sweep-output\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:sweep-output\">[eq:sweep-output]</a>. The lower bound for $r$ is uniform for the instance. The bumps, coefficients, all intermediate and final graph metrics and weights, their smooth graph quantities, and $Y$ are equivariant and Borel and retain the corresponding uniform chart bounds, with the positive-order convention for weights. The map $Y$ is again a root probability map with the same label orbits.*\n\n\n*For each $i$, the coefficient furnished by Lemma <a href=\"#lem:graph-localization\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:graph-localization\">7</a> has the form $$\\beta_i=\\beta_{s,i}+\\epsilon\\psi(R_i),\\qquad\n R_i=\\sum_{j<i}f_j^2 ,\n \\label{eq:sweep-beta}$$ Here $\\beta_{s,i}$ vanishes near $\\mathop{\\mathrm{supp}}F_i$, and the coefficients satisfy <a href=\"#eq:graph-spatial-cutoff\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:graph-spatial-cutoff\">[eq:graph-spatial-cutoff]</a> in $h_{i-1}$. Writing $$ND_i^0=\\epsilon a(F_i^2/f_i-f_i)+A_i^2/(2f_i)$$ on $\\{f_i>0\\}$, extended by zero off the bump, one has $$ND_i=ND_i^0-\\beta_i f_i+\\mathrm{err}_i,\\qquad\n \\sum_i|\\mathrm{err}_i|^2\\le\\eta_0 .\n \\label{eq:sweep-prescription-error}$$*\n\n\n\n*Define the nonnegative reserve $$\\begin{split}\n \\Pi_p:={}&\n T_0+c\\epsilon\\bigl(M_0^2+\\mathbb E_\\nu V_i^2+\\theta\\mathop{\\mathrm{Var}}_\\nu u\\bigr)\n       \\\\\n &+c\\frac r\\epsilon\\mathcal X\n       +c_*\\epsilon r^6\\mathbf 1_{\\{r\\ge C_*\\}},\n \\end{split}\n \\label{eq:sweep-positive-reserve}$$ where the final term is omitted in a sweep without boost. Then, pointwise, $$\\begin{aligned}\n Q:=\\frac{rN^2}{\\epsilon}\\bigl[\n &\\mathcal S(h_+,\\phi_+)-\\mathcal S(h_0,\\phi_0)\n       +\\epsilon e_{h_0}(F)-\\epsilon e_{h_+}(Y)\\bigr]\n \\notag\\\\\n &\\ge\\Pi_p-4\\theta^2m-\\frac r\\epsilon\\delta_0 .\n \\label{eq:sweep-inequality}\n\\end{aligned}$$ The numerical constant $c$ is independent of the instance, the number of colors, and the pass parameters. The errors $\\delta_0,\\eta_0$ can be prescribed independently before the pass.",

        }

          "anchor": "stmt-thm-degree",
          "exact_quote": "There are absolute constants $C_0,C_1>0$ with the following property. Let $q\\ge2$, let $d$ be an integer with $0\\le d\\le q$, and let $\\tau>0$. For $0\\le r\\le d$ set $$\\label{eq:degree-widths}\n T_r=2\\tau+\\frac{r\\tau}{q},\\qquad\n b_r=\\tau\\left(1+\\frac1{2q}+\\frac r{4q^2}\\right).$$ Suppose $(X,h,\\phi,G)$ is a controlled measured $d$-band over $Q_{T_d}^d$ in the sense of Definition <a href=\"#def:degree-band\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"def:degree-band\">15</a>. Let $B\\ge1$ and $L_1,\\ldots,L_d>0$. Suppose throughout $X$ that $$\\label{eq:degree-horizon-bound}\n             L_i|dG_i|_h\\le B-\\frac dq\\qquad(1\\le i\\le d),$$ and suppose that $$\\label{eq:degree-horizon}\n       \\frac{C_0(1+B)^{2/3}}{L_i}<\\frac{\\tau}{10q}\n                       \\qquad(1\\le i\\le d).$$ Define $H_i(G)=2(L_i|dG_i|_h-1)_+$. Then $$\\label{eq:degree-estimate}\n |\\mathcal D_X(G)|(2\\tau)^d\n \\le \\frac{\\exp(C_1d/q)}{\\prod_{i=1}^dL_i}\n     \\int_{\\{|G|_\\infty<b_d\\}}\n       \\exp\\left(\\frac{-\\mathcal S(h,\\phi)+\\sum_{i=1}^dH_i(G)}2\\right)dV_h.$$ For $d=0$ the product is one, the sum is zero, and $\\mathcal S(h,\\phi)=0$; the assertion is the bound of a signed count by the unsigned count.",
          "source_object": "stmt-thm-degree"
        },
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-def-degree-band",
          "exact_quote": "Let $T>0$. A controlled measured $d$-band over $Q_T^d$ consists of a measured space of oriented $d$-manifolds as above, a leafwise metric $h$, a function $\\phi$, and a map $G=(G_1,\\ldots,G_d):X\\to Q_T^d$. For every $0<t<T$ the part $\\{|G|_\\infty\\le t\\}$ has a countable flow-box cover with the following properties.\n\n1.  The boxes have one common positive size and fixed positive buffers: their smaller concentric boxes cover the indicated part. They can be chosen inside $\\{|G|_\\infty<t'\\}$ for some $t<t'<T$. After rescaling coordinates we may, and do, call them unit boxes with buffers.\n\n2.  In these boxes the coefficients of $h$ and $h^{-1}$ and all their leafwise coordinate derivatives are bounded by constants common to the cover. The same holds for all derivatives of $G$ and for all positive-order derivatives of $\\phi$. No bound on the value of $\\phi$ is required.\n\n3.  The sum of the measures of the transversals of this cover is finite. There is a common bound on the number of plaques of the cover whose buffered boxes meet any one plaque of the cover.\n\nThe cover and every bound in this definition may depend on $t$ and on the given band. The leaves are not assumed complete, and $G$ is not assumed proper.",
          "source_object": "stmt-def-degree-band"
        }

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **limit-global-manifold-regime** (hypothesis): The global integral comparison requires a closed connected oriented smooth manifold, the stated integer dimension range, and a smooth Riemannian metric. Blocked use: ['Applying the global comparison unchanged to manifolds with boundary, noncompact manifolds, nonorientable spaces, or arbitrary discrete networks.', "Extending the dimensional range beyond the theorem's stated hypotheses."]
- **limit-imported-vanishing-input** (formal_scope): The conformal reduction depends on a qualitative vanishing theorem imported from the companion manuscript without proof here. Blocked use: ['Presenting the integral comparison as independent of the imported vanishing theorem.', 'Treating the local graph calculations alone as a proof of the global topological comparison.']
- **limit-vanishing-positive-obstruction** (edge_case): When the manifold admits nonnegative scalar curvature, its real simplicial volume vanishes. The integral comparison then supplies no positive obstruction from simplicial volume. Blocked use: ['Inferring a strictly positive required negative-curvature integral on a manifold covered by the vanishing input.']
- **limit-normalized-comparison-transfer** (hypothesis): The Yamabe transfer assumes a uniform scalar-volume bound for all manifolds and normalized metrics in the stated dimension. An estimate for a restricted geometric subclass does not establish that antecedent. Blocked use: ["Transferring an instance-dependent normalized bound into the theorem's uniform integral comparison.", 'Using a subclass-specific volume estimate as the universal premise of the conformal reduction.']
- **limit-unevaluated-comparison-constant** (unknown): The main theorem supplies a positive dimensional comparison constant, and the final deduction identifies it as a reciprocal. An evaluated numerical value or optimality certificate is ABSENT. Blocked use: ['Using an invented numerical threshold to certify a geometric model.', 'Claiming that the stated dimensional constant is optimal.']
- **limit-detector-cocycle-selection** (nonconstructive): The detector depends on choosing a bounded cohomology class and an alternating cocycle representative through duality and seminorm approximation. A general finite procedure for obtaining this input from an arbitrary manifold description is ABSENT. Blocked use: ['Advertising the detector as an executable simplicial-volume calculator.', 'Assuming that a supplied finite triangulation automatically supplies the required bounded group cocycle.']
- **limit-measured-integration-convention** (hypothesis): The auxiliary parameters must carry the stated measure-preserving action, and leafwise integration includes transverse averaging. Uniform chart controls justify integrability, differentiation, and Stokes. Blocked use: ['Replacing measured integration by an unweighted sum of leaves.', 'Assuming detector homotopy invariance without the support and derivative controls required for measured Stokes.']
- **limit-prescribed-graph-existence-regime** (hypothesis): Prescribed graph existence requires complete boundaryless leaves with buffered bounded geometry and the specified uniform coefficient and weight-derivative bounds. Uniqueness is restricted to bounded-height solutions with the stated regularity and uniform prescription margin. Blocked use: ['Invoking the existence theorem on an arbitrary incomplete region.', 'Claiming uniqueness among all smooth solutions without the bounded-height, uniform regularity, and prescription-margin conditions.', 'Inferring a finite exact elliptic solver from analytic existence.']
- **limit-localization-parameter-order** (hypothesis): Localization requires compact coordinate support, a complete controlled reference metric dominated by the current metric, and the permitted parameter relations. Cutoff errors become small with preceding data and parameters fixed. Blocked use: ['Applying the localized construction to uncontrolled noncompact coordinate supports.', 'Treating its limiting error statement as uniform over simultaneously changing preceding geometry.', 'Bounding differentiated scalar errors solely by the value of the prescription error.']
- **limit-ordered-separated-sweep** (hypothesis): The sweep needs finitely many free label orbits, uniformly locally finite compact coordinate supports, and an equivariant coloring separating the enlarged supports within each color. Earlier graph metrics enter subsequent prescriptions. Blocked use: ['Reordering coordinate operations while assuming the same sweep statistics and estimate.', 'Performing overlapping graphs simultaneously within a color.', 'Dropping orbitwise compact support or uniform derivative controls.']
- **limit-entropy-input-and-complexity** (hypothesis): The entropy theorem assumes normalized negative scalar curvature and uniformly controlled equivariant input maps. Its guaranteed complexity comparison concerns prescribed elementary functions; output smoothness and support bounds may depend on the input and finite construction. Blocked use: ['Applying the entropy contract to arbitrary measurable probability maps without the leafwise smoothness and uniform support assumptions.', 'Replacing the prescribed elementary threshold by an unrestricted growth function.', "Interpreting the scalar constant's uniformity as uniform bounds for every output derivative or construction size."]
- **limit-entropy-schedule-and-exit** (hypothesis): Entropy increments use the stated boosted-sweep parameter regime. Accumulation uses the specified step schedule and initial volume conditions, while phase exit requires the volume-logarithm bound, retained last-sweep surplus, and a sufficiently large parameter relative to the starting gradient bound. Blocked use: ['Treating the sweep-count estimate as a runtime bound for solving graph equations.', 'Applying phase exit using a scalar inequality alone, without its volume and surplus hypotheses.', 'Assuming an input-independent threshold for the first phase exit.']
- **limit-controlled-band-and-horizon** (hypothesis): The measured degree estimate requires controlled inner-band charts, common geometric and derivative bounds, finite total transverse-cover measure, bounded plaque overlap, scaled coordinate-gradient bounds, and strict horizon inequalities. Smoothness alone is insufficient. Blocked use: ['Applying degree descent solely from coordinate-gradient estimates.', 'Replacing the strict horizon inequality by equality.', 'Assuming that complete leaves or a proper map compensate for missing measured-band controls.']
- **limit-measured-degree-and-slice-selection** (formal_scope): Measured degree is a signed integral with transverse measures and need not be an integer. Regular children are supplied for typical slice parameters, without an asserted measurable selection of children across those parameters. Blocked use: ['Substituting an ordinary integer count for measured degree without checking the integration convention.', 'Integrating child constructions as if joint measurability in the slicing parameter had been proved.', 'Discarding the treatment of singular regions before applying the child-band estimate.']
- **limit-common-incidence-rounding** (hypothesis): Unbiased rounding is performed on one finite-type coefficient vector within the joint kernel of exact integer incidence equations. The expectation identity alone does not guarantee boundary balance, and the source does not provide certified approximate integration of the geometric coefficients. Blocked use: ['Rounding individual cell coefficients independently.', 'Using floating-point zero tests as certificates of exact incidence relations.', 'Extending the finite rational adaptation into a certificate for the continuous geometric transfer.']
- **limit-compatible-original-side-resolution** (formal_scope): The resolution represents the original rounded current locally on the designated original-side subset. The full doubled diagram has a different global pushforward, and compatible sheets remain part of the comparison domain. Blocked use: ['Replacing the original-side local pushforward by integration over the entire doubled family.', 'Comparing coincident color coordinates from incompatible label sheets.', 'Ignoring seam metric control when estimating the resolved family.']
- **limit-conditional-transfer-threshold** (hypothesis): The conditional transfer requires the energy coefficient to exceed the transfer threshold fixed from the auxiliary dimension. Seam-density bounds precede the collar choice, which precedes curvature, rotation, comparison-width, and energy-threshold choices. Blocked use: ['Applying the final averaging estimate before verifying the curvature and strict horizon requirements.', 'Choosing the seam collar from bounds that themselves depend on that collar without establishing the required dependency order.', 'Treating tangent and normal comparison lengths as interchangeable.']

          "exact_quote": "There is a numerical $c>0$ with the following property. For a sweep without boost, set $\\psi=0$. The bumps, coefficients, all intermediate and final graph metrics and weights, their smooth graph quantities, and $Y$ are equivariant and Borel and retain the corresponding uniform chart bounds, with the positive-order convention for weights. Writing $$ND_i^0=\\epsilon a(F_i^2/f_i-f_i)+A_i^2/(2f_i)$$ on $\\{f_i>0\\}$, extended by zero off the bump, one has $$ND_i=ND_i^0-\\beta_i f_i+\\mathrm{err}_i,\\qquad\n \\sum_i|\\mathrm{err}_i|^2\\le\\eta_0 .\n \\label{eq:sweep-prescription-error}$$*\n\n\n\n*Define the nonnegative reserve $$\\begin{split}\n \\Pi_p:={}&\n T_0+c\\epsilon\\bigl(M_0^2+\\mathbb E_\\nu V_i^2+\\theta\\mathop{\\mathrm{Var}}_\\nu u\\bigr)\n       \\\\\n &+c\\frac r\\epsilon\\mathcal X\n       +c_*\\epsilon r^6\\mathbf 1_{\\{r\\ge C_*\\}},\n \\end{split}\n \\label{eq:sweep-positive-reserve}$$ where the final term is omitted in a sweep without boost. Then, pointwise, $$\\begin{aligned}\n Q:=\\frac{rN^2}{\\epsilon}\\bigl[\n &\\mathcal S(h_+,\\phi_+)-\\mathcal S(h_0,\\phi_0)\n       +\\epsilon e_{h_0}(F)-\\epsilon e_{h_+}(Y)\\bigr]\n \\notag\\\\\n &\\ge\\Pi_p-4\\theta^2m-\\frac r\\epsilon\\delta_0 .\n \\label{eq:sweep-inequality}\n\\end{aligned}$$ The numerical constant $c$ is independent of the instance, the number of colors, and the pass parameters. There are also $E>E_{\\min}$, an integer $q\\geq n+1$, and a constant $C=C(n,\\mathcal B)<\\infty$ such that, on an invariant conull set of transverse parameters, $$\\label{eq:entropy-contract}\n\\begin{split}\n &\\rho\\leq dV_g,\\qquad\n W:=\\log\\frac{dV_h}{\\rho}\\geq0,\\\\\n &\\mathcal S(h,\\phi)\\geq\n       -C+2\\left(W-\\frac n2\\log E\\right)+E e_h(F),\n       \\qquad e_h(F)^{1/2}\\leq E^{-1/100},\\\\\n &E>\\mathcal B(q).\n\\end{split}$$ Here the extension means that there is an equivariant measure-preserving map $\\Omega\\to\\Omega_0$. Along the entire increasing sequence of parameters, $$\\label{eq:entropy-summability}\n \\sum_p l(\\epsilon_p)^2\\leq C_n,\\qquad\n \\#\\{p:\\epsilon_p<E\\}\\leq C_n(1+\\log^3(1+E)).$$ If the initial volume logarithm is zero, then, for all sufficiently large dimensional $E$, $$\\label{eq:entropy-phase-volume}\n W_{\\mathrm{new}}\\leq E^{0.26}(E+P)^{0.75}.$$ The same assertion holds if the initial data have parameter $E_0$, surplus $P_0$, and $$W\\leq E_0^{0.32}(E_0+P_0)^{0.75},$$ provided $E\\geq E_0$ is sufficiently large dimensionally and $E_0^{0.32}\\leq\\tfrac12E^{0.26}$.",

          "exact_quote": "Let $T>0$. Then $$\\label{eq:degree-estimate}\n |\\mathcal D_X(G)|(2\\tau)^d\n \\le \\frac{\\exp(C_1d/q)}{\\prod_{i=1}^dL_i}\n     \\int_{\\{|G|_\\infty<b_d\\}}\n       \\exp\\left(\\frac{-\\mathcal S(h,\\phi)+\\sum_{i=1}^dH_i(G)}2\\right)dV_h.$$ For $d=0$ the product is one, the sum is zero, and $\\mathcal S(h,\\phi)=0$; the assertion is the bound of a signed count by the unsigned count.",

          "quote": "Let $(X,h)$ be a smooth Riemannian manifold, let $\\phi,f$ be smooth real functions, and let $N>0$. Set $$L=(1+N^2|df|_h^2)^{1/2},\\qquad v=L^{-1},\\qquad\n U=\\frac{N\\nabla_h f}{L},\\qquad\n h'=h+N^2df\\otimes df,\\qquad \\phi'=\\phi-\\log L.$$ View $h'$ as the induced metric on the graph $\\Sigma=\\{(x,Nf(x)):x\\in X\\}$ in $(X\\times\\mathbb R,h+dz^2)$, and extend $\\phi$ constantly in the $z$-direction. Orient the graph by $\\nu=(-U,v)$, put $\\mathrm{II}(V,W)=\\langle\\bar\\nabla_V\\nu,W\\rangle$, and let $$H=\\mathop{\\mathrm{tr}}_\\Sigma\\mathrm{II},\\qquad\n D=H+\\partial_\\nu\\phi=-\\mathop{\\mathrm{div}}_\\phi U .$$ Here $D$, and its derivative $U(D)$, are regarded as functions on $X$ through the graph projection. Then $$\\mathcal S(h',\\phi')-\\mathcal S(h,\\phi)\n =D^2-2U(D)+|\\mathrm{II}|_{h'}^2+|d\\log L|_{h'}^2.\n \\label{eq:graph-identity}$$ Moreover, $$e^{\\phi'}dV_{h'}=e^\\phi dV_h,\\qquad\n N^2|df|_{h'}^2=1-v^2=|U|_h^2,\\qquad\n |d\\log L|_{h'}\\le L|U|_h|\\mathrm{II}|_{h'} .\n \\label{eq:graph-basic-bounds}$$",

Orient the graph by $\\nu=(-U,v)$, put $\\mathrm{II}(V,W)=\\langle\\bar\\nabla_V\\nu,W\\rangle$, and let $$H=\\mathop{\\mathrm{tr}}_\\Sigma\\mathrm{II},\\qquad\n D=H+\\partial_\\nu\\phi=-\\mathop{\\mathrm{div}}_\\phi U .$$ Here $D$, and its derivative $U(D)$, are regarded as functions on $X$ through the graph projection. <a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


We prove the integral scalar-curvature inequality proposed by Gromov. For every dimension <i>n</i> ≥ 3, there is a constant $`a_n\gt 0`$, depending only on <i>n</i>, such that

$`\displaystyle \int_M(\mathop{\mathrm{Scal}}\nolimits _g^-)^{n/2}\,dV_g\ge a_n\|M\|`$

for every closed connected oriented smooth <i>n</i>-manifold <i>M</i> and every smooth Riemannian metric <i>g</i>. Here $`\mathop{\mathrm{Scal}}\nolimits _g^-:=\max\{0,-\mathop{\mathrm{Scal}}\nolimits _g\}`$, and $`\|M\|`$ is real simplicial volume. The proof uses the nonnegative-scalar-curvature vanishing theorem of the companion paper on rational inessentiality.

<a id="sec:introduction"></a>
### Introduction

The simplicial volume of a closed connected oriented $n$-manifold $M$ is the $\ell^1$-seminorm of its real fundamental class: $$\|M\|=
\inf\left\{\sum_j|a_j|:
 \sum_j a_j\sigma_j\text{ is a finite real singular cycle representing }
 [M]_{\mathbb R}\right\}.$$ It measures topological complexity through the cost of a fundamental cycle. Gromov’s work on bounded cohomology connected this invariant with Riemannian volume under lower Ricci curvature bounds <a id="pm-cite-1"></a>[[Gromov1982](#ref-Gromov1982)]. A lower scalar curvature bound retains much less directional information. The possibility that it still controls simplicial volume is therefore a particularly strong relation between local geometry and global topology.

In 1986, Gromov proposed precisely the integral estimate considered here: Conjecture 3.A, Remark (B), Equation (8$'$) of <a id="pm-cite-2"></a>[[Gromov1986](#ref-Gromov1986)] asks for a dimension-dependent upper bound on simplicial volume by the integral of the $n/2$-power of the negative part of scalar curvature. With $$\mathop{\mathrm{Scal}}_g^-:=\max\{0,-\mathop{\mathrm{Scal}}_g\},$$ the integral $\int_M(\mathop{\mathrm{Scal}}_g^-)^{n/2}\,dV_g$ is invariant under constant rescaling of $g$. It records both the size and the concentration of negative scalar curvature.

The conjecture is closely related to the uniform volume assertion $$\mathop{\mathrm{Scal}}_g\ge-\kappa^2
\quad\Longrightarrow\quad
\|M\|\le C_n\kappa^n\mathop{\mathrm{Vol}}_g(M),
\qquad \kappa\ge0.$$ Although the integral formulation keeps track of a varying scalar curvature, the two assertions, when required for every manifold and metric in a fixed dimension $n\ge3$, are equivalent by the Yamabe reformulation. A precise statement appears in <a id="pm-cite-3"></a>[[MinZhengZhu2026](#ref-MinZhengZhu2026), Lemma 2.1].

Several results establish related forms under additional geometric or topological hypotheses. Braun and Sauer prove a macroscopic counterpart when the volumes of unit balls in the universal cover have a uniform upper bound; their constant also depends on that bound <a id="pm-cite-4"></a>[[BraunSauer2021](#ref-BraunSauer2021), Theorem 1.1]. For the qualitative case of nonnegative scalar curvature, Ma, Wang, Xie, Yu, and Zhu prove vanishing when the universal cover is spin <a id="pm-cite-5"></a>[[MaWangXieYuZhu2026](#ref-MaWangXieYuZhu2026)]. Min, Zheng, and Zhu prove the integral estimate for every Riemannian metric on every closed Kähler surface, with coefficient $27/2$ in the upper bound for $\|M\|$, and also treat an infinite family of non-Kähler symplectic four-manifolds <a id="pm-cite-6"></a>[[MinZhengZhu2026](#ref-MinZhengZhu2026), Theorem 1.2 and Section 4].

Our argument uses the vanishing theorem of the companion manuscript <a id="pm-cite-7"></a>[[OpenAIScalarInessentiality2026](#ref-OpenAIScalarInessentiality2026), Corollary 1.5]: a closed connected oriented smooth manifold of positive dimension admitting a metric of nonnegative scalar curvature has zero real simplicial volume. We state the exact input in Theorem <a href="#thm:vanishing-input" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:vanishing-input">2</a>. The work of this paper is the passage from that qualitative vanishing result to a uniform quantitative estimate.

<a id="stmt-thm-main"></a>
<div id="thm:main" class="theorem">

<a id="eq:main"></a>
**Theorem 1**. *For every integer $n\ge3$, there is a constant $a_n>0$, depending only on $n$, such that every closed connected oriented smooth $n$-manifold $M$ and every smooth Riemannian metric $g$ on $M$ satisfy $$\int_M(\mathop{\mathrm{Scal}}_g^-)^{n/2}\,dV_g\ge a_n\|M\|.
  \label{eq:main}$$*

</div>

For example, a metric with $\mathop{\mathrm{Scal}}_g\ge-n(n-1)$ satisfies $$\mathop{\mathrm{Vol}}_g(M)\ge
  a_n[n(n-1)]^{-n/2}\|M\|.$$ The constant is uniform over the topology of $M$ and over all its metrics. In particular, <a href="#eq:main" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:main">[eq:main]</a> permits the negative scalar curvature to be concentrated in a small region.

<a id="pm-h-3"></a>
#### The route to the estimate

The first step is a conformal reduction. If $\|M\|>0$, the vanishing input forces the Yamabe minimizer in every conformal class to have negative scalar curvature. Normalize it to scalar curvature $-1$. Hölder’s inequality bounds the volume of this normalized metric by the integral in <a href="#eq:main" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:main">[eq:main]</a> for the original metric. It remains to prove $$\|M\|\le C_n\mathop{\mathrm{Vol}}_g(M)\qquad\text{when }\mathop{\mathrm{Scal}}_g=-1.$$ This reduction is given in Proposition <a href="#prop:yamabe-reduction" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:yamabe-reduction">3</a>. The quantitative argument begins with this normalized problem.

Bounded cohomology supplies a cocycle of supremum norm at most one whose value $I$ on the fundamental class is at least $\|M\|/2$. We realize it as a closed differential form on a simplex of probability vectors. An equivariant probability map from the universal cover pulls this form back with integral $I$. Thus the remaining task is to control this integral by the original volume.

The first construction changes the metric and the probability map together. For a metric $h$ and weight $\phi$, the relevant scalar quantity is $$\mathcal S(h,\phi)=\mathop{\mathrm{Scal}}_h-2\Delta_h\phi-|d\phi|_h^2.$$ With $\phi=-f$, this is Perelman’s modified scalar curvature for the measure $e^{-f}dV_h$ <a id="pm-cite-8"></a>[[Perelman2002](#ref-Perelman2002), Section 1.3]. A graph deformation enlarges $h$ while changing $\phi$ so that $e^\phi dV_h$ is preserved. Its scalar identity contains positive squares and a derivative of the prescribed weighted mean curvature. This adapts the graph stabilization and prescribed-curvature method of <a id="pm-cite-9"></a>[[OpenAIScalarInessentiality2026](#ref-OpenAIScalarInessentiality2026), Proposition 4.1 and Theorem 5.2]. Choosing that prescription in terms of one probability coordinate at a time gives a gain in the energy of the probability map. Iterating the construction produces a measured equivariant family over $M$, with a density $\rho\le dV_g$, a new metric and weight, and a large coefficient $E$ satisfying the leafwise inequality $$\mathcal S(h,\phi)\ge
  -C_n+2\log\frac{dV_h}{\rho}-n\log E+E|dF|_{h,\mathrm{HS}}^2.$$ Integrals over this family include its auxiliary probability measure. The logarithmic volume term and the energy term have distinct roles: the first will cancel the volume expansion, and the second will pay for derivatives of comparison coordinates. The construction also makes $|dF|_{h,\mathrm{HS}}$ small in absolute terms. This allows its label overlaps to be controlled through a fixed graph even after many deformations. Theorem <a href="#thm:entropy" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:entropy">9</a> states the full result, including the relation between $E$ and the complexity of that graph.

The second construction estimates the degree of a map from a measured family of $d$-manifolds to a $d$-cube. Its bound contains $-\mathcal S/2$ and a separate penalty for the derivative of each coordinate. The perimeter-minus-forcing functional is the $\mu$-bubble functional used in scalar curvature geometry <a id="pm-cite-10"></a>[[Gromov1996](#ref-Gromov1996), [ChodoshLi2024](#ref-ChodoshLi2024)]. Here an ordered family of its minimizing boundaries reduces the dimension one coordinate at a time. Their normal speed, or lapse, appears both in the scalar identity and in coarea, producing the needed cancellation. In high dimensions these boundaries can be singular. We move the residual map away from the target near the singular region, while weighted distance potentials supply the scalar gain needed to control the change. The resulting Theorem <a href="#thm:degree" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:degree">16</a> is formulated for general controlled measured bands; it does not depend on simplicial volume.

The final construction connects the cocycle integral $I$ to these degrees. Integrating the cocycle form on small primal grid cells gives a balanced chain of complementary dual cubes. This follows the probability-map and auxiliary-degree strategy of <a id="pm-cite-11"></a>[[OpenAIScalarInessentiality2026](#ref-OpenAIScalarInessentiality2026), Section 3]. An unbiased rounding preserves the balance relations with integer multiplicities. We adapt Gaifullin’s reflected permutohedral realization <a id="pm-cite-12"></a>[[Gaifullin2008](#ref-Gaifullin2008), [Gaifullin2013](#ref-Gaifullin2013)], equipping the pairing choices with normalized probability laws and controlling the resulting smooth charts. An oppositely oriented mirror copy closes the unmatched faces. In the neighborhood used for comparison, the resolved family retains a side whose oriented pushforward is exactly the original rounded chain. The construction supplies the chart and metric bounds needed for the degree estimate and the final averaging.

Compare the probability map with the resolved dual cubes in dimension $q=n+m$, where $m$ is the dimension of a dual cube. Let $h,\rho,E$ be the deformed metric, retained density, and energy coefficient produced above, and set $W=\log(dV_h/\rho)$. Assign the comparison lengths $L_T=b_*\sqrt E$ in the $m$ directions tangent to the cube and $L_N=b_*\sqrt{qE}$ in the $n$ normal directions, with $b_*>0$ fixed dimensionally. The degree estimate contributes $(L_T^mL_N^n)^{-1}$, the product metric contributes $L_T^m$, and the scalar term $2W-n\log E$ contributes $E^{n/2}e^{-W}$. The factors involving $E$ and the volume density therefore satisfy $$\frac{L_T^m}{L_T^mL_N^n}E^{n/2}e^{-W}dV_h
   =b_*^{-n}q^{-n/2}\rho.$$ On the straight part, where the resolution metric is pulled back from the flat cube, averaging the normal derivative penalties together with the Hilbert bound for the cocycle form costs at most $C_nq^{n/2}$. For this contribution, if $r$ is the grid spacing, the cell coefficient, dual-cube volume, and density of grid centers contribute $r^n r^m r^{-q}=1$. The seam contribution is controlled by its small coordinate volume. The required lower bound for $E$ is an elementary function of $q$, bounded by an exponential tower whose fixed height and base polynomial depend only on $n$. It is fixed by this transfer construction before the entropy theorem is applied. These balances bound the detector pairing by $I\le C_n\int\rho\le C_n\mathop{\mathrm{Vol}}_g(M)$.

Section <a href="#sec:reduction-detector" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:reduction-detector">2</a> gives the conformal reduction and the detector. Section <a href="#sec:graphs" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:graphs">3</a> establishes the graph identity, the prescribed graph equations, and the estimate for one sweep of the probability coordinates. Section <a href="#sec:entropy" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:entropy">4</a> iterates that estimate and controls the label graph. Section <a href="#sec:degree" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:degree">5</a> proves the degree theorem, including the treatment of singular hypersurfaces. Section <a href="#sec:transfer" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:transfer">6</a> constructs the dual chains and their smooth resolution and completes the proof of Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a>.

<a id="sec:reduction-detector"></a>
### Reduction and the bounded cohomology detector

We first separate the conformal reduction from the quantitative part of the proof. We then construct a differential form on a simplex of probability vectors that detects simplicial volume. This form will remain fixed while the metric and the probability map are deformed.

<a id="pm-h-5"></a>
#### The conformal reduction

We use the following result, stated here in the precise form needed.

<a id="stmt-thm-vanishing-input"></a>
<div id="thm:vanishing-input" class="theorem">

**Theorem 2** (OpenAI, Corollary 1.5 of <a id="pm-cite-13"></a>[[OpenAIScalarInessentiality2026](#ref-OpenAIScalarInessentiality2026)]). *Let $M$ be a closed connected oriented smooth manifold of positive dimension. If $M$ admits a smooth Riemannian metric with nonnegative scalar curvature, then its real simplicial volume is zero.*

</div>

In particular, the hypothesis permits scalar curvature to vanish identically. Theorem <a href="#thm:vanishing-input" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:vanishing-input">2</a> is the only result from the companion manuscript invoked below without proof.

The Yamabe reformulation of the scalar curvature inequality is standard; <a id="pm-cite-14"></a>[[MinZhengZhu2026](#ref-MinZhengZhu2026), Lemma 2.1] records the equivalence of its uniform pointwise and integral forms. We include the short classwise reduction that identifies the use of the vanishing input.

<a id="stmt-prop-yamabe-reduction"></a>
<div id="prop:yamabe-reduction" class="proposition">

<a id="eq:scalar-minus-one-target"></a>
<a id="eq:yamabe-transfer"></a>
**Proposition 3**. *Fix $n\ge3$. Suppose that a constant $C_n<\infty$ has the following property: every closed connected oriented $n$-manifold with a smooth metric $h$ of constant scalar curvature $-1$ satisfies $$\|M\|\le C_n\mathop{\mathrm{Vol}}_h(M).
  \label{eq:scalar-minus-one-target}$$ Then every smooth Riemannian metric $g$ on every such manifold satisfies $$\|M\|\le C_n\int_M(\mathop{\mathrm{Scal}}_g^-)^{n/2}\,dV_g .
  \label{eq:yamabe-transfer}$$*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-prop-yamabe-reduction"><summary>Proof of stmt-prop-yamabe-reduction</summary>

*Proof.* The assertion is immediate when $\|M\|=0$, so assume that $\|M\|>0$. Write $Y(M,[g])$ for the Yamabe constant of the conformal class of $g$: $$Y(M,[g])=
\inf_{u\in C^\infty(M),\,u>0}
\frac{\displaystyle\int_M
  \left(\frac{4(n-1)}{n-2}|du|_g^2+\mathop{\mathrm{Scal}}_g u^2\right)dV_g}
{\displaystyle\left(\int_Mu^{2n/(n-2)}\,dV_g\right)^{(n-2)/n}}.$$ The Yamabe theorem, developed through the work of Yamabe, Trudinger, Aubin, and Schoen <a id="pm-cite-15"></a>[[Yamabe1960](#ref-Yamabe1960), [Trudinger1968](#ref-Trudinger1968), [Aubin1976](#ref-Aubin1976), [Schoen1984](#ref-Schoen1984)], gives a conformal metric attaining this value and having constant scalar curvature; see also <a id="pm-cite-16"></a>[[LeeParker1987](#ref-LeeParker1987), Section 11]. Its scalar curvature is negative by Theorem <a href="#thm:vanishing-input" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:vanishing-input">2</a>. After a constant rescaling, it becomes a metric $h$ with $\mathop{\mathrm{Scal}}_h=-1$. Evaluating the Yamabe quotient at the constant function for this minimizing metric gives $$-Y(M,[g])=\mathop{\mathrm{Vol}}_h(M)^{2/n}.$$ For each positive $u$, Hölder’s inequality gives $$\int_M\mathop{\mathrm{Scal}}_g u^2\,dV_g
\ge
-\left(\int_M(\mathop{\mathrm{Scal}}_g^-)^{n/2}\,dV_g\right)^{2/n}
 \left(\int_Mu^{2n/(n-2)}\,dV_g\right)^{(n-2)/n}.$$ The gradient term in the Yamabe quotient is nonnegative. Taking the infimum therefore yields $$-Y(M,[g])
\le \left(\int_M(\mathop{\mathrm{Scal}}_g^-)^{n/2}\,dV_g\right)^{2/n}.$$ Consequently $\mathop{\mathrm{Vol}}_h(M)\le\int_M(\mathop{\mathrm{Scal}}_g^-)^{n/2}\,dV_g$, and <a href="#eq:scalar-minus-one-target" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:scalar-minus-one-target">[eq:scalar-minus-one-target]</a> implies <a href="#eq:yamabe-transfer" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:yamabe-transfer">[eq:yamabe-transfer]</a>. ◻

</details>

For the rest of the proof we fix $M^n$ and $g$ with $\mathop{\mathrm{Scal}}_g=-1$, and prove <a href="#eq:scalar-minus-one-target" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:scalar-minus-one-target">[eq:scalar-minus-one-target]</a>. We may continue to assume $\|M\|>0$.

<a id="pm-h-6"></a>
#### A bounded cocycle and its probability form

<a id="eq:bounded-duality"></a>
Let $\Gamma=\pi_1(M)$. This group is countable because $M$ has a finite triangulation. We use two standard facts about bounded cohomology with trivial real coefficients. First, duality between the $\ell^1$-seminorm on homology and the supremum seminorm on bounded cohomology gives $$\|M\|=
\sup\left\{\langle\beta,[M]_{\mathbb R}\rangle:
 \beta\in H_b^n(M;\mathbb R),\ \|\beta\|_\infty\le1\right\}.
\label{eq:bounded-duality}$$ See <a id="pm-cite-17"></a>[[Gromov1982](#ref-Gromov1982), Section 1.1]. Second, the isometric Mapping Theorem identifies $H_b^k(M;\mathbb R)$ with $H_b^k(\Gamma;\mathbb R)$ for every $k\ge2$, through the classifying map. The version used here applies to connected countable CW spaces and preserves the seminorms. We use the Mapping Theorem of Gromov <a id="pm-cite-18"></a>[[Gromov1982](#ref-Gromov1982), Section 3.1] and its canonical group formulation in <a id="pm-cite-19"></a>[[IvanovNotes](#ref-IvanovNotes), Theorems 8.3 and 8.6].

We use the homogeneous model for group cochains. Thus an $n$-cochain is a bounded function on $\Gamma^{n+1}$, invariant under simultaneous left translation, and its coboundary is the alternating sum obtained by omitting one argument. Alternation is a cochain projection of norm at most one and is equivariantly cochain homotopic to the identity through maps bounded in each degree. It therefore preserves bounded cohomology classes <a id="pm-cite-20"></a>[[Frigerio2016](#ref-Frigerio2016), Section 4.10, Proposition 4.26]. By <a href="#eq:bounded-duality" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:bounded-duality">[eq:bounded-duality]</a>, the Mapping Theorem, and approximation of the seminorm by cocycle representatives, there is a homogeneous alternating cocycle $$c:\Gamma^{n+1}\longrightarrow\mathbb R,\qquad \|c\|_\infty\le1,$$ whose corresponding bounded singular cohomology class has value <a id="eq:detector-value"></a>$$I:=\langle[c],[M]_{\mathbb R}\rangle\ge\frac12\|M\|.
  \label{eq:detector-value}$$ For completeness, one can choose a class in <a href="#eq:bounded-duality" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:bounded-duality">[eq:bounded-duality]</a> with value at least $3\|M\|/4$, choose a group representative of norm at most $1+\varepsilon$, alternate it, and divide by $1+\varepsilon$, with $0<\varepsilon\le1/2$. Replacing the cocycle by its negative first, if necessary, fixes the sign.

We allow a finite number of copies of every group label. More precisely, let $\mathcal L=\Gamma\times\{1,\ldots,s\}$, with the free left $\Gamma$-action on the first factor, and evaluate $c$ on $\mathcal L^{n+1}$ by projection to $\Gamma^{n+1}$. The same notation will be used when further finite copies of $\mathcal L$ are introduced. Extend $c$ multilinearly to finitely supported vectors on $\mathcal L$.

<a id="eq:probability-form"></a>
Let $$\Delta_{\mathcal L}=
\left\{p=(p_\ell)_{\ell\in\mathcal L}:
 p_\ell\ge0,\ \sum_\ell p_\ell=1,\ p\text{ has finite support}\right\}.$$ On each finite-dimensional face define the $n$-form $$\eta_p(v_1,\ldots,v_n)=n!\,c(p,v_1,\ldots,v_n).
\label{eq:probability-form}$$ These definitions agree on common faces.

We call a map $F=(F_\ell)$ with nonnegative coordinates and $\sum_\ell F_\ell^2=1$ a *root probability map*. For the maps used here, whose coordinate supports are locally finite, the squared coordinates $p_\ell=F_\ell^2$ give the associated map to $\Delta_{\mathcal L}$.

<a id="stmt-prop-detector"></a>
<div id="prop:detector" class="proposition">

<a id="eq:detector-integral"></a>
**Proposition 4**. *The form $\eta$ is closed on every finite-dimensional face of $\Delta_{\mathcal L}$. There is a smooth $\Gamma$-equivariant map $$F:\widetilde M\longrightarrow\ell^2(\mathcal L),\qquad
 F_\ell\ge0,\qquad \sum_\ell F_\ell^2=1,$$ with finitely many label orbits and locally finite compact supports for its coordinates. If $p_\ell=F_\ell^2$, then the descended form satisfies $$\int_M p^*\eta=I.
  \label{eq:detector-integral}$$*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-prop-detector"><summary>Proof of stmt-prop-detector</summary>

*Proof.* Write $\epsilon(v)=\sum_\ell v_\ell$ for the mass of a finitely supported vector. Multilinear extension of the cocycle equation gives $$0=\sum_{j=0}^{n+1}(-1)^j\epsilon(w_j)
 c(w_0,\ldots,\widehat w_j,\ldots,w_{n+1}).$$ Choose $w_0$ of mass one and let $w_1,\ldots,w_{n+1}$ have mass zero. It follows that $c(w_1,\ldots,w_{n+1})=0$. All tangent vectors to a probability simplex have mass zero. Differentiating <a href="#eq:probability-form" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:probability-form">[eq:probability-form]</a> now shows $d\eta=0$.

To construct $F$, choose finitely many evenly covered open sets in $M$ and smooth nonnegative bumps subordinate to them whose nonzero sets cover $M$. Lift each bump to one chosen sheet and take all its deck translates, denoting the resulting functions by $b_\ell$. Their supports are compact and locally finite on $\widetilde M$. Set $$F_\ell=\frac{b_\ell}{\left(\sum_j b_j^2\right)^{1/2}}.$$ The denominator is positive, and the stated properties follow.

Choose a finite oriented triangulation of $M$. On its lift, assign one label to every vertex equivariantly, and let $p^{\mathrm{aff}}$ be the probability map affine in barycentric coordinates on each simplex. The homotopy $(1-t)p+t p^{\mathrm{aff}}$ is equivariant and has locally finite support. Applying Stokes’ theorem to each simplex times $[0,1]$, and cancelling the common faces, shows that its pullback integral is constant. On a simplex with ordered vertex labels $\ell_0,\ldots,\ell_n$, write $$p^{\mathrm{aff}}=e_{\ell_0}
+\sum_{j=1}^nt_j(e_{\ell_j}-e_{\ell_0}).$$ Alternation makes the value of $c(p^{\mathrm{aff}},dp^{\mathrm{aff}},\ldots,dp^{\mathrm{aff}})$ on the coordinate frame constant and equal to $c(\ell_0,\ldots,\ell_n)$. The coordinate simplex has volume $1/n!$, so the factor $n!$ in <a href="#eq:probability-form" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:probability-form">[eq:probability-form]</a> makes its integral exactly this cocycle value. Summing over the oriented fundamental triangulation gives <a href="#eq:detector-integral" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:detector-integral">[eq:detector-integral]</a>. The identification with the chosen group class is the usual cochain realization of the Mapping Theorem: an equivariant vertex labeling on the universal cover evaluates the homogeneous group cochain, and two such labelings give cochain-homotopic maps by the simplex prism construction. ◻

</details>

<a id="pm-h-7"></a>
#### Measured equivariant families

The deformations below use auxiliary probability choices. We record their integration convention so that the detector value remains unambiguous.

Let $(\Omega,\mu)$ be a standard Borel probability space with a measure-preserving $\Gamma$-action. A measured equivariant family over $M$ consists of $\Gamma$-equivariant Borel data on $\widetilde M\times\Omega$, smooth in the $\widetilde M$ variable. Geometrically one may regard the diagonal quotient $$\mathcal M=(\widetilde M\times\Omega)/\Gamma$$ as a space whose leaves are ordinary smooth manifolds. If $\Gamma_\omega$ is the stabilizer of $\omega$, the corresponding leaf is $\widetilde M/\Gamma_\omega$; the deck action is free and proper in the $\widetilde M$ factor.

Related equivariant measured bundles are central to the macroscopic volume argument of Braun and Sauer <a id="pm-cite-21"></a>[[BraunSauer2021](#ref-BraunSauer2021), Sections 1.2 and 4]. Here we need only Borel transverse data and use the following direct integration convention.

<a id="eq:measured-integral"></a>
Choose a finite evenly covered atlas $U_a$ of $M$, chosen lifts $\widetilde U_a$, and a smooth partition of unity $\chi_a$ subordinate to the atlas. For an integrable equivariant leafwise $n$-form $\alpha$, set $$\int_{\mathcal M}\alpha
 =
\sum_a\int_\Omega\int_{\widetilde U_a}
  (\chi_a\circ\pi)\,\alpha_\omega\,d\mu(\omega).
\label{eq:measured-integral}$$ Equivariance and invariance of $\mu$ show that this value is independent of the lifts and partition. The same convention applies to nonnegative densities. We write $dV_h$ for the resulting measured Riemannian density when $h=h_\omega$ is a leafwise metric.

The data used in the first deformation will have bounds in a fixed finite lifted atlas that are uniform over $\omega$. These bounds may depend on the instance: the original manifold and metric and the finite sequence of constructions already made. For the forms used here, these bounds ensure integrability over the compact base and justify differentiation and Fubini. Leafwise Stokes’ theorem follows from <a href="#eq:measured-integral" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:measured-integral">[eq:measured-integral]</a>: apply ordinary Stokes’ theorem in each lifted chart to the partitioned form; the terms containing $d\chi_a$ cancel on overlaps after the measure-preserving changes of transverse coordinate.

In particular, suppose that two equivariant probability maps $p^0,p^1$ have uniformly locally finite coordinate supports and uniform leafwise derivative bounds. Their affine homotopy has the same properties. Stokes’ theorem on the measured product with $[0,1]$ gives $$\int_{\mathcal M}(p^0)^*\eta
 =
\int_{\mathcal M}(p^1)^*\eta.$$ Thus every probability map constructed below continues to detect the same number $I$. Enlarging $\Omega$ by an independent probability space also preserves the integral by Fubini. We may discard invariant null sets whenever needed. More general measured spaces of leaves will enter the degree estimate in Section <a href="#sec:degree" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:degree">5</a>; their chart and integration conventions will be specified there.

<a id="sec:graphs"></a>
### Weighted graph deformations and a sweep

This section develops a deformation that changes the metric while preserving a weighted volume density. Its input is a smooth function on a Riemannian manifold; its output is the metric induced on the graph of that function. We first compute the change of weighted scalar curvature, then solve graph equations prescribing weighted mean curvature to control this change. A localized version of the construction will give an estimate for one sweep through the coordinates of a root probability map.

For a metric $h$ and a smooth weight $\phi$, write $$\mathcal S(h,\phi)=\mathop{\mathrm{Scal}}_h-2\Delta_h\phi-|d\phi|_h^2,
 \qquad
 \mathop{\mathrm{div}}_\phi V=e^{-\phi}\mathop{\mathrm{div}}(e^\phi V),
 \qquad
 \Delta_\phi=\mathop{\mathrm{div}}_\phi\nabla .$$ Thus the weighted operators use $e^\phi dV_h$. The scalar quantity is Perelman’s $R^m$, with his $f=-\phi$ <a id="pm-cite-22"></a>[[Perelman2002](#ref-Perelman2002), Section 1.3]. The estimates below concern its behavior under particular graph deformations. For antecedents of the graph and vertical Jacobi identities, see <a id="pm-cite-23"></a>[[OpenAIScalarInessentiality2026](#ref-OpenAIScalarInessentiality2026), Proposition 4.1]; we include the calculation with the conventions needed here.

<a id="pm-h-9"></a>
#### The graph identity

<a id="stmt-lem-graph-identity"></a>
<div id="lem:graph-identity" class="lemma">

<a id="eq:graph-identity"></a>
<a id="eq:graph-basic-bounds"></a>
**Lemma 5** (Weighted graph identity). *Let $(X,h)$ be a smooth Riemannian manifold, let $\phi,f$ be smooth real functions, and let $N>0$. Set $$L=(1+N^2|df|_h^2)^{1/2},\qquad v=L^{-1},\qquad
 U=\frac{N\nabla_h f}{L},\qquad
 h'=h+N^2df\otimes df,\qquad \phi'=\phi-\log L.$$ View $h'$ as the induced metric on the graph $\Sigma=\{(x,Nf(x)):x\in X\}$ in $(X\times\mathbb R,h+dz^2)$, and extend $\phi$ constantly in the $z$-direction.
